==== RULE FILE: .cursor/rules/01-code-standards/error-handling-and-reliability.mdc ====
---
alwaysApply: true
description: Error handling, retries, and reliability standards
---

# Error Taxonomy, Propagation, Retries & Timeouts

Define domain error classes, wrap third-party errors at boundaries, and retry only transient failures with capped backoff + jitter.

**Justification:** Clear error semantics and disciplined retries improve resilience and reduce noisy failures.

## Domain Error Types

Wrap third-party errors once at module boundaries; rethrow as domain errors.

```typescript
export class TransientNetworkError extends Error {
  constructor(message: string, cause?: unknown) {
    super(message); this.name = 'TransientNetworkError'; (this as any).cause = cause;
  }
}
```

## Retries & Timeouts

- Use capped exponential backoff with jitter for network calls
- Never retry non-idempotent operations
- Apply request timeouts and circuit breakers for unstable deps

# ESLint Configuration Standards - 2025

Unified ESLint configuration with TypeScript support, security rules, and framework-specific patterns for consistent code quality.

**Justification:** Consistent linting prevents bugs, enforces best practices, ensures code readability, and catches security issues early.

## 🎯 Single Responsibility
This rule ONLY defines ESLint configuration and rules. Not formatting (see prettier-config) or TypeScript compiler options (see typescript-standards).

## 🚫 Custom Project Rules

### Enforce Project Patterns
```javascript
// Custom rules for your project patterns
const customRules = {
  // Enforce error handling patterns
  'custom/catch-error-handling': {
    create(context) {
      return {
        CatchClause(node) {
          if (!node.param) {
            context.report({
              node,
              message: 'Catch blocks must handle the error parameter',
            });
          }
        }
      };
    }
  },

  // Enforce async/await over promises
  'custom/prefer-async-await': {
    create(context) {
      return {
        CallExpression(node) {
          if (node.callee.property?.name === 'then') {
            context.report({
              node,
              message: 'Prefer async/await over .then() chains',
              fix(fixer) {
                // Auto-fix logic here
              }
            });
          }
        }
      };
    }
  },

  // Enforce proper logging
  'custom/structured-logging': {
    create(context) {
      return {
        CallExpression(node) {
          if (node.callee.object?.name === 'console') {
            context.report({
              node,
              message: 'Use structured logger instead of console',
              fix(fixer) {
                return fixer.replaceText(node.callee.object, 'logger');
              }
            });
          }
        }
      };
    }
  },
};
```

    "lint:staged": "lint-staged"

}

### ESLint Performance Config
```javascript
// Optimize for large codebases
{
  settings: {
    // Cache results for faster subsequent runs
    cache: true,
    cacheLocation: '.eslintcache',
    
    // Limit TypeScript type checking to improve performance
    '@typescript-eslint/parser': {
      project: './tsconfig.json',
      createDefaultProgram: false, // Faster parsing
    }
  },
  
  // Disable expensive rules in development
  rules: process.env.NODE_ENV === 'development' ? {
    'import/no-cycle': 'off', // Expensive
    '@typescript-eslint/no-misused-promises': 'off', // Type-heavy
  } : {}
}
```

## 🚨 Common ESLint Issues & Solutions

```typescript
// ❌ Issue: Unexpected any
function process(data: any) { // Error: no-explicit-any
  return data;
}

// ✅ Solution: Use proper types or unknown
function process(data: unknown): ProcessedData {
  if (isValidData(data)) {
    return data as ProcessedData;
  }
  throw new Error('Invalid data');
}

// ❌ Issue: Floating promises
async function main() {
  doAsyncWork(); // Error: no-floating-promises
}

// ✅ Solution: Await or explicitly handle
async function main() {
  await doAsyncWork();
  // or
  void doAsyncWork(); // Explicitly ignore
}

// ❌ Issue: Unsafe member access
function getValue(obj: any) {
  return obj.nested.value; // Error: no-unsafe-member-access
}

// ✅ Solution: Type guards and optional chaining
function getValue(obj: unknown): string | undefined {
  if (isValidObject(obj)) {
    return obj?.nested?.value;
  }
  return undefined;
}
```

==== RULE FILE: .cursor/rules/01-code-standards/node-platform-standards.mdc ====
---
alwaysApply: true
description: Node platform standards for reliability and performance
---

# Node Platform Standards

Validate inputs, avoid blocking CPU on request threads, enforce request timeouts, and prefer streaming for large payloads.

**Justification:** Prevents reliability and performance pitfalls in services and webhooks.

## Input Validation

```typescript
const validate = ajv.compile(schema);
if (!validate(body)) throw new Error('invalid');
```

## Concurrency & Timeouts

- Reject long CPU work on request threads; offload to workers/queues
- Enforce per-request timeouts and sensible connection pooling



==== RULE FILE: .cursor/rules/01-code-standards/separation-of-concerns.mdc ====
---
description: Separation of concerns for layering and responsibilities
alwaysApply: true
---
# Separation of Concerns (SoC)

Ensure the codebase is divided into distinct layers: presentation (UI components), business logic (services), and data access (stores/adapters). Each layer should handle its specific concern without overlapping responsibilities. UI components should not contain parsing, storage, or network logic.

**Justification**: Separation prevents tight coupling, making the SummerySky extension easier to maintain, test, and extend across portals and workflows.

**Example**: Keep portal-specific parsing in `libs/summerysky/portal-*` and invoke it from the service worker, rather than parsing directly inside sidepanel components.

**Related Rules**: [single-responsibility.mdc](mdc:.cursor/rules/01-code-standards/single-responsibility.mdc), [simplicity.mdc](mdc:.cursor/rules/01-code-standards/simplicity.mdc)



==== RULE FILE: .cursor/rules/01-code-standards/simplicity.mdc ====
---
description: Favor simple, maintainable solutions over complexity
alwaysApply: true
---
# Simplicity

Favor simple, straightforward solutions over complex ones, avoiding unnecessary abstractions or over-engineering.

**Justification**: Simplicity reduces technical debt and enhances maintainability.

**Example**: Use basic hooks in `HomeTab.tsx` instead of complex state management unless needed.

**Related Rules**: [separation-of-concerns.mdc](mdc:.cursor/rules/01-code-standards/separation-of-concerns.mdc), [single-responsibility.mdc](mdc:.cursor/rules/01-code-standards/single-responsibility.mdc)



==== RULE FILE: .cursor/rules/01-code-standards/single-responsibility.mdc ====
---
description: Single-responsibility guidance for functions and modules
alwaysApply: true
---
# Single Responsibility Principle (SRP)

Each function, class, or module should have one, and only one, responsibility. This means focusing on a single task or concern.

**Justification**: Reliability is critical, SRP reduces bug risk by limiting scope and simplifies debugging and refactoring.

**Example**: `RecipeExecutor` should only handle recipe execution, while record export logic stays in `@ss/google-export` and storage logic stays in `@ss/patient-records`.

**Related Rules**: [separation-of-concerns.mdc](mdc:.cursor/rules/01-code-standards/separation-of-concerns.mdc)



==== RULE FILE: .cursor/rules/01-code-standards/ts-standards-and-strictness.mdc ====
---
alwaysApply: true
description: TypeScript strictness, imports, and public API standards
---

# TypeScript Standards: Strictness, Imports & Public Surfaces

Enable strict compiler options across all projects, avoid `any`, and restrict imports to stable public surfaces via path aliases.

**Justification:** Strict typing and stable import surfaces reduce defects, improve editor tooling, and make refactors safer across shared code.

## Compiler Options

Enable strict, noUncheckedIndexedAccess, noImplicitOverride, exactOptionalPropertyTypes, bundler module resolution, and isolatedModules for test compatibility.

```json
{
  "compilerOptions": {
    "strict": true,
    "noUncheckedIndexedAccess": true,
    "noImplicitOverride": true,
    "exactOptionalPropertyTypes": true,
    "moduleResolution": "bundler",
    "isolatedModules": true,
    "esModuleInterop": true,
    "allowSyntheticDefaultImports": true
  }
}
```

### Test Environment Configuration

For projects using ES modules in tests, ensure tsconfig supports both build and test environments:

```json
// tsconfig.json (project root)
{
  "extends": "../../tsconfig.base.json",
  "compilerOptions": {
    "isolatedModules": true,
    "esModuleInterop": true,
    "allowSyntheticDefaultImports": true,
    "module": "ESNext"
  },
  "include": ["src/**/*"],
  "exclude": ["node_modules", "dist", "coverage"]
}
```

⚠️ **Warning:** Do not disable strict mode in project tsconfigs; request waivers via ADR if absolutely necessary.

### tsconfig.json Path Calculation (NX Best Practice)

When creating `tsconfig.json` files in NX libraries, the `extends` path must correctly reference `tsconfig.base.json` at the workspace root. **Count directory levels carefully** to avoid build failures.

**Path Calculation Rules:**

1. Count the number of directory levels from the library's `tsconfig.json` to the workspace root
2. Use that many `../` segments in the `extends` path
3. Always verify the path exists before committing

**Examples:**

```json
// For libs/Google-Chrome-Extension-Library/core/runtime/tsconfig.json
// Path: libs/ -> Google-Chrome-Extension-Library/ -> core/ -> runtime/
// Levels: 4 (libs, Google-Chrome-Extension-Library, core, runtime)
{
  "extends": "../../../../tsconfig.base.json"
}

// For libs/Google-Chrome-Extension-Library/ui/shared-components/tsconfig.json
// Path: libs/ -> Google-Chrome-Extension-Library/ -> ui/ -> shared-components/
// Levels: 4 (libs, Google-Chrome-Extension-Library, ui, shared-components)
{
  "extends": "../../../../tsconfig.base.json"
}

// For apps/chrome-extensions/job-hunt-extension/tsconfig.json
// Path: apps/ -> chrome-extensions/ -> job-hunt-extension/
// Levels: 3 (apps, chrome-extensions, job-hunt-extension)
{
  "extends": "../../../tsconfig.base.json"
}
```

**Verification Command:**

Before committing, verify the path resolves correctly:

```bash
cd <library-directory>
ls -la <calculated-path>/tsconfig.base.json
```

**Common Error:** Using too many `../` segments (e.g., `../../../../../tsconfig.base.json` when `../../../../tsconfig.base.json` is correct) causes Vite/esbuild to fail with "Cannot read file" errors.

💡 **Tip:** When copying a `tsconfig.json` from another library, always verify the path depth matches your library's location.

## No `any` & Safer Assertions

Disallow `any`. Prefer `unknown` with user-defined type guards. Use `as` assertions sparingly at boundaries.

```typescript
function isLead(x: unknown): x is { id: string } {
  return typeof x === 'object' && x !== null && 'id' in x;
}
```

## Path Aliases & Public Surfaces

Expose only `src/index.ts` from each library through path aliases. Internal files are not importable across libs.

```json
{
  "compilerOptions": {
    "baseUrl": ".",
    "paths": { "@mm/*": ["libs/*/src/index.ts"] }
  }
}
```

💡 **Tip:** Create explicit barrel files per library to keep public APIs small and documented.

## Examples

```json
{ "rules": { "@typescript-eslint/no-explicit-any": "error" } }
```

## Related Rules

- architecture-and-nx-boundaries
- testing-strategy



==== RULE FILE: .cursor/rules/02-architecture/architecture-and-nx-boundaries.mdc ====
---
alwaysApply: true
description: "Monorepo layout and boundary rules"
---

# Architecture, Workspace Layout & Nx Boundaries

Define a predictable Nx layout with strict domain boundaries and dependency constraints to keep the monorepo fast and modular.

**Justification:** Clear layers and enforced constraints prevent accidental coupling, enable parallel work, and support safe refactors at scale.

## Workspace Layout

Organize the monorepo into top-level directories: apps, libs, tools, e2e, and docs.

Libraries follow <domain>/<type> where type ∈ feature, ui, data-access, util, config, testing.

Every project declares Nx tags to enable lint rules for dependency constraints.

- apps/: runnable applications and entry points
- libs/: reusable libraries grouped by domain and type
- tools/: repo automation, codegen, migration scripts
- e2e/: Playwright-based end-to-end suites
- docs/: ADRs, architecture diagrams, API references

## Cross-Initiative Reuse (SummerySky)

Features for `apps/chrome-extensions/PMHNP-Administration-SummerySky` MUST be implemented as reusable libs first. The app should only wire UI, routing, and Chrome/MV3 entry points.

- Place reusable code in `libs/summerysky/*` (or `libs/Google-Chrome-Extension-Library/*` for shared extension utilities).
- Avoid portal-specific assumptions in shared libs; keep portal adapters in data-access libraries.
- Expose clean public APIs via `index.ts`; do not import deep paths across libs.
- If a feature is SummerySky-only today, still design it as a portable library so other initiatives can adopt it with minimal changes.

## Dependency Constraints

Enforce domain and layer constraints using Nx and ESLint. Higher layers may depend on lower layers; never the reverse.

Only import from a library's public surface (index.ts).

```json
{
  "extends": ["plugin:@nx/typescript"],
  "rules": {
    "@nx/enforce-module-boundaries": [
      "error",
      {
        "enforceBuildableLibDependency": true,
        "allow": [],
        "depConstraints": [
          { "sourceTag": "type:feature", "onlyDependOnLibsWithTags": ["type:data-access","type:ui","type:util","type:config"] },
          { "sourceTag": "type:data-access", "onlyDependOnLibsWithTags": ["type:util","type:config"] },
          { "sourceTag": "type:ui", "onlyDependOnLibsWithTags": ["type:util","type:config"] }
        ]
      }
    ]
  }
}
```

💡 **Tip:** Prefer small, composable libraries over monolithic ones to maximize reuse and cache hits.

## Tagging Examples

```json
{
  "name": "@mm/patients/data-access",
  "tags": ["scope:patients","type:data-access"]
}
```

## Examples

```json
{"name":"@mm/pipeline/util","tags":["scope:eng","type:util"]}
```

- ts-standards-and-strictness
- dev-workflow-and-git
- ci-cd-and-releases



==== RULE FILE: .cursor/rules/03-workflow/ai-sdlc-orchestrator.mdc ====
---
alwaysApply: true
description: Repo-wide SDLC orchestrator driven by Codex continuity state
---

# SDLC Orchestrator (Codex + Cursor)

Use this rule to keep AI work aligned with the SDLC process defined in `/.cursor/rules/03-workflow/sdlc-setup.mdc` and to apply the practical feature/bug/hotfix workflows in `/.cursor/rules/03-workflow/ship-it-workflow.mdc`.

When `.codex/projects/**/current-task.json` exists, treat it as the phase source of truth for the matching project:
- Phase state: `sdlcPhase`, `currentPhase`, `activeBranch`
- Verification commands: `nextCommandSet`
- Completion criteria: `doneCriteria`

Do not re-state every checklist item here. Follow the existing SDLC and ship-it docs as the canonical text, and only use this file to orchestrate ordering and gates.

## 1) Detect change intent (silent inference)

Infer the closest workflow from the user request:
- If the user asks for “new feature”, “add capability”, “implement X”, follow the Feature workflow in `ship-it-workflow.mdc`.
- If the user asks for “bug”, “fix”, “regression”, “broken”, follow the Bug Fix workflow in `ship-it-workflow.mdc`.
- If the user asks for “urgent”, “prod break”, “sev-1”, “hotfix”, follow the Hotfix Fast Path in `ship-it-workflow.mdc`.
- If the request is documentation-only, follow the SDLC phases up to and including `document` and run the smallest relevant verification gates.

## 2) Pick the SDLC state source

Step A: Look for task-state continuity state (Codex first)
- Search for `.codex/projects/**/current-task.json`.
- Choose the project whose `project.path` matches (or is closest to) the files being changed, mentioned paths, or the primary target app.
- If no matching Codex project exists:
  - Search for a non-Codex task-state file at a recommended path like `.cursor/sdlc/task-state/<project>/current-task.json`.
  - If neither exists, treat the flow as generic and follow `sdlc-setup.mdc` phase ordering.

Step B: If a task-state file exists, read:
- `.codex/projects/<project>/SDLC_CHARTER.md` (project-specific SDLC sequence, guardrails, and quality gates)
- the active task-state file for:
  - `sdlcPhase`
  - `currentPhase`
  - `nextCommandSet`
  - `doneCriteria`

## 3) Map task-state `sdlcPhase` to SDLC checklists

If task-state `sdlcPhase` is present, follow these mappings:
- `plan`: Phase 1 (Project Initialization & Planning) from `sdlc-setup.mdc`
- `design`: Phase 2 (Quality Gates & Development Standards) + design decisions (tests strategy, ADR if needed) from `sdlc-setup.mdc` and the “Design the Change” section in `ship-it-workflow.mdc`
- `implement`: Phase 3 + Phase 4 implementation checklist from `sdlc-setup.mdc`
- `verify`: Phase 4 verification checklist from `sdlc-setup.mdc`, plus the “Run Affected Targets” step in `ship-it-workflow.mdc`
- `document`: Phase 7 documentation checklist from `sdlc-setup.mdc` and ADR expectations from `documentation-standards.mdc`
- `release`: Phase 5 deployment & release checklist from `sdlc-setup.mdc` and the “Staging Deploy / Production Rollout” guidance in `ship-it-workflow.mdc`
- `operate`: Phase 6 observability & operations checklist from `sdlc-setup.mdc` (monitoring, incident response, and improvements)

If task-state `sdlcPhase` is missing, use the ordered SDLC phases from `sdlc-setup.mdc` as the default progression.

## 4) Verification command policy (Nx-first)

If task-state `nextCommandSet` is non-empty:
- Run commands from `nextCommandSet` in the given order.
- After each command, confirm the specific gate it represents succeeded (typecheck/test/build/e2e slice).
- Only proceed to the next SDLC phase when the completion criteria for the current phase are satisfied (use task-state `doneCriteria` when present, otherwise use the `sdlc-setup.mdc` quality gates).

If task-state `nextCommandSet` is empty or task-state state is not present:
- Prefer Nx affected pipelines:
  - `pnpm exec nx affected:lint --parallel`
  - `pnpm exec nx affected:test --parallel`
  - `pnpm exec nx affected:build --parallel`
- Add the smallest targeted e2e/smoke verification that matches the change surface (UI vs service worker vs integration boundaries).

## 5) DoR / DoD gates (feature vs bug)

Treat the “Definition of Ready (DoR)” and “Definition of Done (DoD)” as mandatory phase exit criteria:

Feature DoR/DoD
- DoR must include: one-sentence goal + clear acceptance criteria + identified risks + rollback strategy + test plan outline.
- DoD must include: acceptance criteria pass + tests added and passing + documentation updated + staging smoke green + rollback plan validated, when applicable.

Bug Fix DoR/DoD
- DoR must include: a reproduction description and a failing test plan.
- DoD must include: failing test now passes + minimal fix + regression coverage + verification gates green.

Hotfix
- DoD includes the Sev-1 fast path checklist: incident note, minimal fix with failing test, fast CI lane, production deploy, post-deploy smoke, and monitored stability window.

## 6) Branch and commit safety

Use `ship-it-workflow.mdc` branching guidance:
- `feat/<scope>-<short-title>`
- `fix/<scope>-<issue#>`
- `hotfix/<short-title>`

Safety constraints from SDLC/charters:
- Keep work phase-scoped and independently verifiable.
- Do not work directly in the user’s dirty workspace when an applicable charter forbids it (if `.codex/**/SDLC_CHARTER.md` includes a “do not work directly” guardrail, comply).
- Before switching phases or branches, update the active task-state `currentPhase` and `nextCommandSet` for the new state.

## 7) Task-state continuity updates (before phase close)

If task-state state is present, maintain deterministic resumability:
- Update the active task-state file when:
  - `currentPhase.status` changes to `verified` or `done`
  - `activeBranch` changes
  - `nextCommit` / `nextCommandSet` changes
  - verification gates complete and `doneCriteria` can be satisfied
- Ensure the worktree matches the declared `currentPhase.branch` before continuing with the next phase.

## Canonical references (follow these)

These docs are the source of detailed checklist text; this rule only orchestrates ordering:
- `/.cursor/rules/03-workflow/sdlc-setup.mdc`
- `/.cursor/rules/03-workflow/ship-it-workflow.mdc`
- `/.cursor/rules/03-workflow/dev-workflow-and-git.mdc`
- `/.cursor/rules/03-workflow/documentation-standards.mdc`
- `/.cursor/rules/nx-sdlc/RULE.md`

# Development Workflow, Branching & Reviews

Use Conventional Commits, short-lived branches, CODEOWNERS-based reviews, and objective quality gates.

**Justification:** Consistent workflow and automated checks keep velocity high and reduce regressions.

## Conventional Commits

- Types: feat, fix, chore, docs, refactor, perf, test, build, ci, revert
- Scopes: use Nx domain tags (e.g., patients, dialer, intake)

**Example:** Example: feat(patients): add consent verification to intake flow

## PR Hygiene & Reviews

- Aim for ≤ 400 LOC per PR where feasible
- Require passing gates: lint, typecheck, unit, e2e smoke, docs
- CODEOWNERS assign domain reviewers automatically

```markdown
# CODEOWNERS
/apps/** @mm/frontend-leads
/libs/** @mm/architecture
/tools/** @mm/devex
```

## Pre-commit & Pre-push

Automate formatting, linting, and typechecking with Husky/lefthook.

```json
{
  "husky": {
    "hooks": {
      "pre-commit": "pnpm lint && pnpm format",
      "pre-push": "pnpm typecheck && pnpm test:ci"
    }
  }
}
```

- ci-cd-and-releases



==== RULE FILE: .cursor/rules/03-workflow/documentation-standards.mdc ====
---
description: Documentation standards for code, APIs, and project maintenance
globs: ["**/*.ts", "**/*.tsx", "**/*.js", "**/*.jsx", "**/README.md", "**/docs/**"]
alwaysApply: true
---

# Documentation Standards

## JSDoc Requirements

### Public API Documentation
All exported functions, classes, and interfaces must include comprehensive JSDoc comments:

```typescript
/**
 * Creates a new user account with email verification
 * @param userData - User registration data
 * @param options - Additional registration options
 * @returns Promise resolving to created user with verification token
 * @throws {ValidationError} When user data is invalid
 * @throws {DuplicateUserError} When email already exists
 * @example
 * ```typescript
 * const user = await createUser({
 *   email: 'user@example.com',
 *   name: 'John Doe'
 * });
 * ```
 */
export async function createUser(
  userData: UserData,
  options?: RegistrationOptions
): Promise<User> {
  // implementation
}
```

### Documentation Requirements Checklist
- [ ] **@param** - All parameters with types and descriptions
- [ ] **@returns** - Return value description and type
- [ ] **@throws** - All possible error types and conditions
- [ ] **@example** - At least one runnable usage example
- [ ] **@since** - Version when feature was added (for major APIs)
- [ ] **@deprecated** - Deprecation notice with migration path

## README Standards

### Project README Structure
Every project must have a comprehensive README.md:

```markdown
# Project Name

Brief description of what this project does and its purpose.

## Installation

```bash
pnpm install
```

## Quick Start

```typescript
import { mainFunction } from '@mm/project-name';

const result = await mainFunction();
```

## API Reference

### Core Functions

#### `mainFunction(options: Options): Promise<Result>`

Main entry point for the library.

**Parameters:**
- `options` - Configuration options

**Returns:** Promise resolving to operation result

**Example:**
```typescript
const result = await mainFunction({ debug: true });
```

## Environment Variables

| Variable | Description | Required | Default |
|----------|-------------|----------|---------|
| `API_KEY` | API authentication key | Yes | - |
| `LOG_LEVEL` | Logging verbosity | No | `info` |

## Nx Targets

| Target | Description | Command |
|--------|-------------|---------|
| `build` | Build the library | `nx build project-name` |
| `test` | Run unit tests | `nx test project-name` |
| `lint` | Lint the codebase | `nx lint project-name` |

## Development

### Prerequisites
- Node.js 18+
- pnpm 8+

### Setup
```bash
pnpm install
pnpm build
```

### Testing
```bash
pnpm test
pnpm test:watch
```

## Architecture

[Include diagrams or flowcharts showing key components]

## Contributing

See [CONTRIBUTING.md](../CONTRIBUTING.md) for development guidelines.

## License

MIT
```

## ADR (Architecture Decision Records)

### When to Create ADRs
Create an ADR for any non-trivial architectural decision:

- Choosing between major technology options
- Significant API design changes
- Performance vs maintainability trade-offs
- Security implementation decisions
- Breaking changes to existing patterns

### ADR Structure
```markdown
# ADR-001: Decision Title

## Status
Accepted | Proposed | Rejected

## Context
What problem are we solving? What constraints exist?

## Decision
What did we decide and why?

## Consequences
What becomes easier/harder? What are the trade-offs?

## Alternatives Considered
What other options did we evaluate?

- **Option A**: Pros/cons
- **Option B**: Pros/cons

## Implementation
How will this be implemented? Timeline? Migration plan?
```

### ADR Organization
```
docs/
  adr/
    001-use-typescript-strict-mode.md
    002-adopt-nx-monorepo.md
    003-implement-error-boundaries.md
```

## API Documentation

### OpenAPI/Swagger Standards
For HTTP APIs, maintain OpenAPI specifications:

```yaml
openapi: 3.0.3
info:
  title: User Management API
  version: 1.0.0
  description: User account and profile management
paths:
  /users:
    post:
      summary: Create new user
      requestBody:
        required: true
        content:
          application/json:
            schema:
              $ref: '#/components/schemas/UserInput'
      responses:
        '201':
          description: User created successfully
          content:
            application/json:
              schema:
                $ref: '#/components/schemas/User'
components:
  schemas:
    UserInput:
      type: object
      required:
        - email
        - name
      properties:
        email:
          type: string
          format: email
        name:
          type: string
```

## Auto-Generated Documentation

### CI Documentation Generation
Configure CI to automatically generate and publish documentation:

```yaml
# .github/workflows/docs.yml
name: Generate Docs
on:
  push:
    branches: [main]
  pull_request:

jobs:
  docs:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v3
      - uses: actions/setup-node@v3
        with:
          node-version: 18
          cache: 'pnpm'
      - run: pnpm install
      - run: pnpm docs:generate
      - run: pnpm docs:publish
```

### TypeDoc Configuration
```javascript
// typedoc.config.js
module.exports = {
  entryPoints: ['./src/index.ts'],
  out: './docs/api',
  exclude: ['**/*.spec.ts', '**/*.test.ts'],
  includeVersion: true,
  disableSources: false,
  excludePrivate: true,
  excludeProtected: true,
  hideGenerator: true,
  theme: 'default',
  readme: './README.md',
  name: 'My Library',
  tsconfig: './tsconfig.json'
};
```

## Code Comments Standards

### When to Comment
- **Complex business logic** - Explain why, not what
- **Workarounds** - Document temporary solutions and TODOs
- **Public APIs** - JSDoc as specified above
- **Configuration choices** - Why specific values were chosen

### Comment Style
```typescript
// ❌ Bad: states the obvious
const total = price * quantity; // multiply price by quantity

// ✅ Good: explains business logic
const total = price * quantity; // Apply volume discount threshold check

// ✅ Good: documents workaround
// TODO: Remove when API supports bulk operations
// Using individual requests due to rate limiting
for (const item of items) {
  await api.update(item);
}
```

## Diagrams and Visual Documentation

### Mermaid Diagrams
Use Mermaid for architecture and flow diagrams:

```mermaid
graph TD
    A[User Request] --> B[API Gateway]
    B --> C[Authentication]
    C --> D[Business Logic]
    D --> E[Database]
    D --> F[External API]
    E --> G[Response]
    F --> G
```

### Architecture Diagrams
Include diagrams for:
- System architecture overview
- Data flow diagrams
- Component interaction diagrams
- Deployment diagrams

## Documentation Quality Checklist

### Code Documentation
- [ ] All public APIs have JSDoc comments
- [ ] Examples are runnable and tested
- [ ] Error conditions are documented
- [ ] Breaking changes are versioned and documented

### Project Documentation
- [ ] README includes setup, usage, and API reference
- [ ] Environment variables are documented
- [ ] Nx targets are listed and described
- [ ] Architecture diagrams exist for complex systems

### Process Documentation
- [ ] ADRs exist for major decisions
- [ ] Development workflow is documented
- [ ] Deployment process is clear
- [ ] Troubleshooting guides exist

### Maintenance
- [ ] Documentation is reviewed in PRs
- [ ] Outdated docs are updated with code changes
- [ ] CI validates documentation generation
- [ ] Documentation is versioned with releases

## Success Metrics

- **Documentation Coverage**: > 90% of public APIs documented
- **README Completeness**: All projects have comprehensive READMEs
- **ADR Compliance**: Major decisions have corresponding ADRs
- **Documentation Freshness**: < 30 days since last update
- **Developer Satisfaction**: > 80% of developers can find needed information


==== RULE FILE: .cursor/rules/03-workflow/sdlc-setup.mdc ====
---
description: Apply to all files when setting up new projects, services, or major features to ensure complete SDLC coverage
alwaysApply: false
---
# SDLC Setup & Quality Gates - Freshworks App Monorepo

Comprehensive checklist for managing the Software Development Life Cycle in a Freshworks CRM app environment. Use this when spinning up new apps, services, or major features.

**Justification:** Structured SDLC prevents technical debt, ensures quality, and reduces production incidents in enterprise CRM integrations.

## SDLC Phases Overview

1. **Plan & Spec** → Define goals, users, scope, success criteria, risks
2. **Design** → Architecture, data models, APIs, UI flows, test strategy
3. **Implement** → Code + tests + configs (with continuous quality gates)
4. **Verify** → Automated + manual testing, QA
5. **Release** → Deploy, migrate, feature toggles
6. **Operate** → Monitor, alert, incident response
7. **Improve** → Learnings, backlog, iteration

## SDLC Checklist - Order of Operations

Use this checklist for new apps/services or major features. Each phase builds on the previous.

## Task Progress Tracking & Verification Orchestration (Codex optional)

Use this checklist for human-readable SDLC gates, but drive *phase progress* and *verification execution order* from a task-state file.

### Source of truth (use Codex when available)
- Codex projects:
  - Machine-readable task state: `.codex/projects/<project>/current-task.json`
  - Project charter / guardrails: `.codex/projects/<project>/SDLC_CHARTER.md` (optional but preferred)
- Non-Codex projects (fallback):
  - Create and maintain a task-state file using the same schema fields at a recommended path like `.cursor/sdlc/task-state/<project>/current-task.json`.
  - Use `.codex/schemas/task-state.schema.json` as the reference shape for required fields.

### Task-state fields and how they map to SDLC progression
- `sdlcPhase` selects the SDLC phase to target (`plan/design/implement/verify/document/release/operate`).
- `currentPhase` drives phase-level tracking:
  - `currentPhase.id` identifies the current phase artifact/bundle.
  - `currentPhase.status` must move through `pending` → `in_progress` → (`verified` or `blocked`) → `done`.
  - `currentPhase.branch` is the worktree/branch that must be checked out when continuing work for that phase.
- `nextCommandSet` is the verification execution plan for the *current* phase:
  - Prefer running those commands in order.
  - Only mark phase progress complete when the represented quality gates succeed.
- `doneCriteria` defines the completion/exit conditions for the phase:
  - Use it as the “can we close this phase?” checklist in addition to the SDLC quality gates below.
- `blockedBy` indicates known blockers:
  - If non-empty, set `currentPhase.status` to `blocked` and do not advance until blockers are resolved/cleared.
- `artifacts` is where required phase outputs (handoff docs, diagrams, exports, runbook snippets) should be created/updated before phase close.

### Resume protocol (deterministic restart)
Before continuing from an interruption, ensure the repo state matches the active task-state:
- Verify `activeBranch` and `currentPhase.branch`.
- Verify `nextCommit` and `nextCommandSet` match the intended phase.
- Run the commands in `nextCommandSet` and only continue when the phase’s `doneCriteria` can be satisfied.

### 🚀 Phase 1: Project Initialization & Planning

**Repository & Workspace Setup**
- [ ] Create repository with main branch protection rules
- [ ] Initialize pnpm workspace (monorepo structure)
- [ ] Set up Nx workspace configuration
- [ ] Add appropriate Nx tags for dependency constraints

**Documentation & Governance**
- [ ] Choose license (MIT for open-source components)
- [ ] Create README.md with setup instructions
- [ ] Add CONTRIBUTING.md guidelines
- [ ] Configure CODEOWNERS for domain reviewers
- [ ] Document success criteria and acceptance tests

### 🛡️ Phase 2: Quality Gates & Development Standards

**Code Quality Tools**
- [ ] Add Prettier formatter with consistent rules
- [ ] Configure ESLint with strict TypeScript rules
- [ ] Enable TypeScript strict mode (noImplicitAny, etc.)
- [ ] Add Husky pre-commit hooks + lint-staged
- [ ] Configure .editorconfig for consistent formatting

**Testing Framework Setup**
- [ ] Add Vitest for unit tests (preferred for new projects)
- [ ] Configure Jest for existing packages (backward compatibility)
- [ ] Set up test coverage thresholds (80% global, 70% per-file)
- [ ] Add mutation testing (Stryker) for critical paths
- [ ] Create smoke test for basic functionality

### 🔧 Phase 3: Infrastructure & Configuration

**Environment Management**
- [ ] Define environments: dev, staging, prod (+ PR previews)
- [ ] Create .env.example with all required variables
- [ ] Set up secret management (GitHub Secrets, SSM, Doppler)
- [ ] Implement 12-factor config (env vars, no secrets in code)
- [ ] Add runtime config validation (fail fast on boot)

**CI/CD Pipeline**
- [ ] Configure GitHub Actions with Nx affected commands
- [ ] Set up pnpm store caching for faster builds
- [ ] Implement PR pipeline: install → typecheck → lint → test → build
- [ ] Add accessibility checks (a11y) for UI components
- [ ] Configure SBOM generation and dependency scanning
- [ ] Ensure fast feedback (<10 min for basic checks)

### 🧪 Phase 4: Testing Strategy Implementation

**Unit Testing (White-box)**
- [ ] Write unit tests for all new functions and classes
- [ ] Use Vitest with TypeScript support
- [ ] Mock external dependencies (APIs, databases)
- [ ] Achieve coverage thresholds (80% global, 70% per-file)
- [ ] Add mutation testing for critical business logic

**Integration Testing**
- [ ] Test module/service interactions
- [ ] Add contract tests for API boundaries
- [ ] Mock external services (Freshsales, ElevenLabs, Telnyx)
- [ ] Test database operations with test containers
- [ ] Verify authentication and authorization flows

**End-to-End Testing**
- [ ] Implement smoke tests (basic functionality verification)
- [ ] Add sanity tests for critical user journeys
- [ ] Create regression tests for existing features
- [ ] Set up Playwright for UI component testing
- [ ] Automate webhook service integration tests

### 🚀 Phase 5: Deployment & Release

**Preview & Staging**
- [ ] Set up ephemeral preview deployments per PR
- [ ] Configure staging environment for main branch merges
- [ ] Implement feature flags for gradual rollouts
- [ ] Add database migration scripts (if needed)
- [ ] Configure blue-green deployment strategy

**Production Release**
- [ ] Set up manual approval gates for production
- [ ] Implement canary deployments for high-risk changes
- [ ] Add automated rollback procedures
- [ ] Configure health checks and monitoring
- [ ] Set up alerting for production incidents

### 📊 Phase 6: Observability & Operations

**Logging & Monitoring**
- [ ] Implement structured JSON logging
- [ ] Add OpenTelemetry traces and metrics
- [ ] Configure health endpoints (/healthz, /ready)
- [ ] Set up error tracking (Sentry) with context
- [ ] Create dashboards for key SLIs/SLOs

**Security & Compliance**
- [ ] Enable dependency vulnerability scanning
- [ ] Configure secret scanning in CI
- [ ] Set up least-privilege IAM roles
- [ ] Implement encryption at rest and in transit
- [ ] Add HIPAA compliance mode for PHI modules

**Operational Readiness**
- [ ] Document runbook (local dev, deploy, rollback)
- [ ] Set up on-call rotation and alerting
- [ ] Create incident response procedures
- [ ] Add post-deployment smoke tests
- [ ] Configure backup and disaster recovery

### 📚 Phase 7: Documentation & Knowledge

**Technical Documentation**
- [ ] Create architecture diagrams (system, data flow)
- [ ] Document API contracts and schemas
- [ ] Write ADRs for architectural decisions
- [ ] Add inline code documentation
- [ ] Create troubleshooting guides

**Operational Documentation**
- [ ] Document deployment procedures
- [ ] Create monitoring and alerting runbooks
- [ ] Add security incident response guides
- [ ] Document backup and recovery procedures
- [ ] Create performance tuning guidelines

## Quality Gates by Phase

### Pre-commit (Automatic)
- [ ] Code formatting (Prettier)
- [ ] Linting (ESLint)
- [ ] Type checking (TypeScript)
- [ ] Unit tests pass

### Pre-merge (PR Review)
- [ ] All pre-commit checks pass
- [ ] Code review by domain experts
- [ ] Integration tests pass
- [ ] Accessibility checks pass (for UI)
- [ ] Security scanning clean

### Pre-deploy (CI/CD)
- [ ] Full test suite passes
- [ ] Build artifacts generated
- [ ] SBOM and vulnerability scan complete
- [ ] Smoke tests pass in staging

### Post-deploy (Production)
- [ ] Health checks pass
- [ ] Monitoring alerts configured
- [ ] Rollback plan documented
- [ ] Incident response team notified

## Success Metrics

- **Development Velocity**: PR merge time < 24 hours
- **Code Quality**: 0 critical linting errors, 80%+ test coverage
- **Reliability**: 99.9% uptime, < 5 min incident response
- **Security**: 0 critical vulnerabilities in production
- **User Experience**: All accessibility standards met

description: Battle-tested order of operations for shipping features and fixing bugs in modern Nx monorepos with strict TypeScript, CI/CD, and feature flags

# Ship It! - Battle-Tested Order of Operations

Complete workflow for shipping features and fixing bugs in modern Nx monorepos. Follow these steps every time for predictable, safe deployments.

**Justification:** Consistent process prevents production incidents, ensures quality, and maintains velocity in enterprise CRM integrations.

## 🎯 Shared Principles

### Branching Strategy
```bash
# Features
feat/<scope>-<short-title>    # Example: feat/intake-guard-dedupe

# Bug fixes  
fix/<scope>-<issue#>          # Example: fix/telephony-asserted-id-421

# Hotfixes (Sev-1)
hotfix/<short-title>          # Example: hotfix/critical-auth-bypass
```

### Definition of Ready (DoR)
✅ **A task is ready when you can write:**
- One-sentence goal
- Clear acceptance criteria
- Identified risks
- Rollback strategy
- Test plan outline

❌ **If you can't write these, it's not ready to start**

### Definition of Done (DoD)
✅ **A task is done when:**
- All acceptance criteria pass
- Tests added and passing
- Documentation updated
- Post-deploy smoke tests green
- Monitoring alerts quiet
- Ticket closed

---

## 🚀 FEATURE WORKFLOW - Order of Operations

### 1️⃣ Triage & Spec (30-60 min)
```markdown
## User Story Template
As a [user type]
I want to [action]
So that [benefit]

## Acceptance Criteria
- [ ] AC-1: Specific measurable outcome
- [ ] AC-2: Another measurable outcome
- [ ] AC-3: Edge case handling

## Dependencies
- APIs: [List external APIs]
- Database: [Schema changes needed]
- Libraries: [New packages required]
- Feature Flag: `feature_<name>_enabled`

## Observability Plan
- Logs: Key events to log
- Metrics: Counters/timers to add
- Dashboard: Panels to create
```

### 2️⃣ Branch & Scaffold
```bash
# Create feature branch
git checkout -b feat/<scope>-<short-title>

# Generate Nx module/lib if needed
nx g @nx/js:lib features/<feature-name> --tags=scope:feature,type:feature

# Add feature flag (default OFF)
echo "FEATURE_<NAME>_ENABLED=false" >> .env.example
```

### 3️⃣ Test Plan First
```typescript
// Define test cases BEFORE coding

// Unit Tests (Pure/Fast)
describe('FeatureName Core Logic', () => {
  it('should handle happy path')
  it('should validate inputs')
  it('should handle edge cases')
});

// Integration Tests (API ↔ DB)
describe('FeatureName Integration', () => {
  it('should persist data correctly')
  it('should handle concurrent requests')
  it('should respect feature flag')
});

// E2E Test (User Flow)
describe('FeatureName E2E', () => {
  it('should complete user journey when flag enabled')
  it('should gracefully degrade when flag disabled')
});

// Smoke Test (Critical Path)
describe('FeatureName Smoke', () => {
  it('should not break existing flows')
  it('should respond to health check')
});
```

### 4️⃣ Design the Change
```markdown
## ADR Template (if changing patterns)
# ADR-XXX: [Decision Title]

## Status
Proposed | Accepted | Deprecated

## Context
What problem are we solving?

## Decision
What are we doing?

## Consequences
What becomes easier/harder?
```

### 5️⃣ Implement Incrementally (Flag-First)
```typescript
// Always check feature flag first
if (process.env.FEATURE_<NAME>_ENABLED === 'true') {
  // New implementation
  return newFeatureLogic();
} else {
  // Existing behavior
  return existingLogic();
}

// Tracer bullet approach - vertical slice
// 1. Route → 2. Controller → 3. Service → 4. Repository → 5. Database
```

### 6️⃣ Integration & E2E
```bash
# Add integration tests with test containers
docker-compose -f docker-compose.test.yml up -d
nx test <project> --testPathPattern=integration

# Add E2E test behind flag
FEATURE_<NAME>_ENABLED=true nx e2e <project>-e2e
```

### ⚠️ Testing Pitfalls to Avoid

#### Constructor Signature Changes
```typescript
// ❌ Don't test with hard-coded constructor params
const service = new Service({ logger, db, cache });

// ✅ Use dependency injection with mocks
const mockLogger = createMockLogger();
const mockDb = createMockDb();
const service = new Service(mockLogger, mockDb);
```

#### Private Method Testing
```typescript
// ❌ Don't test private methods directly
(service as any).privateMethod();

// ✅ Test public behavior instead
await expect(service.publicMethod()).toThrow('Validation error');
```

#### Node.js Built-in Module Imports
```typescript
// ❌ ES module syntax for Node.js built-ins
import fs from 'fs'; // Fails in Jest

// ✅ Namespace imports for Node.js built-ins
import * as fs from 'fs';
```

#### Mock Typing Issues
```typescript
// ❌ Incorrect mock return types
mockPage.innerText.mockResolvedValue('text'); // Type error

// ✅ Proper type assertions
mockPage.innerText.mockResolvedValue('text' as any);
```

### 7️⃣ Local Affected Checks (Nx)
```bash
# Run all affected checks in parallel
nx affected:lint --parallel
nx affected:test --parallel
nx affected:build --parallel

# If infrastructure changes
nx run <infra-project>:synth
cdk diff
```

### 8️⃣ Open PR Early (Draft)
```markdown
## PR Template
**Title:** feat(scope): short intent

**Summary:** What & why in 2-3 sentences

**Acceptance Criteria:**
- [ ] AC-1: Specific outcome
- [ ] AC-2: Another outcome

**Changes:**
- Endpoints: `/api/v1/new-endpoint`
- Schema: Added `field_name` to table
- Environment: `FEATURE_X_ENABLED`
- Feature Flag: `feature_x_enabled`

**Test Plan:**
- Unit: 15 new tests added
- Integration: 5 API tests added
- E2E: 1 user journey test
- Smoke: Health check verified

**Migration/Infra:**
- [ ] Run migration script
- [ ] Update CDK stack
- Rollback: `git revert` + flag OFF

**Observability:**
- Metrics: `feature_x_usage_count`
- Logs: Search for `feature_x`
- Dashboard: Link to dashboard

**Risks/Mitigations:**
- Risk: Performance impact
- Mitigation: Added caching layer

**Screenshots/GIFs:** [If UI changes]
```

### 9️⃣ CI Gates
```yaml
# CI must pass these gates
jobs:
  quality-gates:
    steps:
      - Typecheck    # TypeScript strict mode
      - Lint         # ESLint rules pass
      - Unit Tests   # 80% coverage
      - Integration  # API contracts valid
      - Build        # Artifacts created
      - E2E Preview  # Tests against preview URL
```

### 🔟 Review & Iterate
```bash
# Address review comments
# Keep commits clean
git commit -m "feat(intake): address review feedback"

# Re-run affected checks
nx affected:test --base=origin/main
```

### 1️⃣1️⃣ Staging Deploy (Flag OFF)
```bash
# Deploy with flag OFF
FEATURE_<NAME>_ENABLED=false npm run deploy:staging

# Run post-deploy smoke
npm run test:smoke:staging

# Enable for internal testing
FEATURE_<NAME>_ENABLED=true npm run test:e2e:staging

# Monitor logs/metrics
tail -f logs/staging.log | grep "feature_x"
```

### 1️⃣2️⃣ Production Rollout
```typescript
// Progressive flag ramp
const rolloutSchedule = {
  day1: { percentage: 1, cohort: 'internal' },
  day2: { percentage: 10, cohort: 'beta_users' },
  day3: { percentage: 50, cohort: 'all_users' },
  day4: { percentage: 100, cohort: 'all_users' }
};

// Monitor KPIs
const monitoring = {
  errorRate: '< 0.1%',
  latencyP95: '< 500ms',
  businessMetric: 'conversion_rate'
};
```

### 1️⃣3️⃣ Finish & Cleanup
```bash
# Update documentation
npm run docs:generate

# Update CHANGELOG
echo "### Added
- Feature X: Description" >> CHANGELOG.md

# Remove temporary code
git grep "TODO(feature_x)" | xargs -I {} sed -i '' 's/TODO(feature_x)//' {}

# Close ticket
gh issue close <issue-number> --comment "Feature shipped successfully"
```

## 🐛 BUG FIX WORKFLOW - Order of Operations

### 1️⃣ Triage
```markdown
## Bug Report Template
**Symptom:** What's broken?
**Scope/Impact:** How many users affected?
**First Bad Release:** v1.2.3
**Suspected Area:** Authentication module
**Severity:** P0 | P1 | P2 | P3

## For Sev-1/Hotfix
- Skip normal queue
- Write incident note
- Follow hotfix fast path
```

### 2️⃣ Reproduce & Add Failing Test
```typescript
// FIRST: Write a test that fails
describe('Bug #421', () => {
  it('should preserve caller ID on transfer', () => {
    // This test MUST fail before the fix
    const result = transferCall({ assertedId: '+1234567890' });
    expect(result.callerId).toBe('+1234567890'); // Currently fails
  });
});
```

### 3️⃣ Branch
```bash
git checkout -b fix/<scope>-<issue#>
# Example: fix/telephony-421
```

### 4️⃣ Root Cause & Minimal Fix
```typescript
// Prefer smallest change that addresses root cause
// BAD: Refactor entire module
// GOOD: Fix the specific line causing the issue

// Before (buggy)
const callerId = call.id; // Wrong field

// After (fixed)
const callerId = call.assertedId || call.id; // Correct field with fallback
```

### 5️⃣ Run Affected Targets
```bash
nx affected:test --base=origin/main
nx affected:build --base=origin/main

# If infrastructure changed
nx run <infra-project>:synth
cdk diff
```

### 6️⃣ PR (Can be marked "hotfix")
```markdown
## Bug Fix PR Template
**Root Cause:** Caller ID field was using wrong property
**Before:** System used `call.id` instead of `call.assertedId`
**After:** System correctly uses `call.assertedId` with fallback
**How to Verify:** Run test case in PR description
**Risk:** Low - isolated change with fallback
**Rollback:** `git revert` this commit
```

### 7️⃣ CI + Staging
```bash
# Deploy to staging
npm run deploy:staging

# Run focused sanity tests
npm run test:sanity --area=telephony

# Run global smoke tests
npm run test:smoke:staging
```

### 8️⃣ Production
```bash
# Deploy (with flag if added for safety)
BUGFIX_421_ENABLED=true npm run deploy:production

# Verify metrics
curl https://metrics.example.com/api/telephony/errors

# Post incident resolution
echo "Incident resolved: Bug #421 fixed and deployed" >> incidents.log
```

## 🚨 HOTFIX FAST PATH (Sev-1)

### Immediate Response
```bash
# 1. Create hotfix branch from prod tag
git checkout -b hotfix/<short-title> production-v1.2.3

# 2. Reproduce → Failing test → Minimal fix
npm run test:reproduce-bug

# 3. Fast CI lane (skip non-critical)
nx run-many -t lint,test,build --projects=affected

# 4. Deploy directly to prod
npm run deploy:production:hotfix

# 5. Post-deploy smoke
npm run test:smoke:production

# 6. Monitor for 15-30 minutes
watch -n 5 'curl https://api.example.com/health'

# 7. Back-merge to main
git checkout main
git merge hotfix/<short-title>

# 8. Create postmortem
gh issue create --title "Postmortem: <incident>" \
  --body "Root cause, timeline, action items"
```

## 🎛️ ROLLOUT & SAFETY PATTERNS

### Feature Flags
```typescript
// Ship dark, test safely, instant rollback
const featureFlag = {
  name: 'feature_x_enabled',
  defaultValue: false,
  rollout: {
    internal: true,
    beta: false,
    production: false
  }
};
```

### Migration Strategy
```typescript
// 1. Deploy forward-compatible code
function readData() {
  return newSchema || oldSchema; // Works with both
}

// 2. Run migration
await runMigration();

// 3. Flip to new path
function readData() {
  return newSchema; // Only new
}

// 4. Remove old code
// Delete oldSchema references
```

### Canary/Blue-Green
```yaml
deployment:
  strategy: canary
  stages:
    - weight: 5%   # 5% traffic
      duration: 1h
    - weight: 25%  # If metrics good
      duration: 2h
    - weight: 100% # Full rollout
```

### Observability
```typescript
// Add instrumentation
const timer = metrics.startTimer('feature_x_duration');
try {
  logger.info('feature_x_started', { userId, timestamp });
  const result = await featureXLogic();
  metrics.increment('feature_x_success');
  return result;
} catch (error) {
  metrics.increment('feature_x_error');
  logger.error('feature_x_failed', { error, userId });
  throw error;
} finally {
  timer.end();
}
```

### Kill Switch
```typescript
// Emergency bypass
if (process.env.KILL_FEATURE_X === 'true') {
  return legacyImplementation();
}
```

1. **Start with the test** - Write the failing test first
2. **Ship behind flags** - Always use feature flags for new features

4. **Automate everything** - If you do it twice, automate it
5. **Monitor proactively** - Add metrics before you need them
6. **Document decisions** - Future you will thank present you
7. **Rollback fast** - Better to rollback than debug in production
8. **Learn from incidents** - Every incident should improve the process

==== RULE FILE: .cursor/rules/04-testing/bridge-communication-testing.mdc ====
---
description: When working with Cursor-Flow communication always implement these testing standards
alwaysApply: false
---
# Bridge Communication Testing Standards

Ensure robust testing of browser-to-platform communication bridges to prevent event handling failures.

**Justification:** The "Open Editor" issue revealed that bridge functions can be silently overridden, causing events to be logged but never handled. This rule prevents such issues through comprehensive testing.

## 🎯 Single Responsibility
This rule ONLY covers testing of bridge communication patterns between browser scripts and platform handlers. Not general testing (see comprehensive-testing-strategy) or UI testing (see playwright-automation-standards).

## 📋 Implementation Checklist

### Bridge Testing Requirements
```markdown
- [ ] Verify bridge function exists before use
- [ ] Test bridge function signature matches expectations
- [ ] Ensure bridge is not overridden by subsequent scripts
- [ ] Validate event routing to correct handlers
- [ ] Test fallback mechanisms when bridge unavailable
- [ ] Verify timing of bridge setup vs script injection
- [ ] Monitor bridge events in development mode
- [ ] Create automated tests for bridge communication
```

## 🔍 Common Bridge Issues & Prevention

### Issue 1: Bridge Override
**Problem:** Scripts override the platform's bridge with their own implementation
```javascript
// ❌ BAD: Unconditional override
window.__bridgeFunction = function(event) { 
  console.log(event); // Just logs, never handles!
};

// ✅ GOOD: Guarded implementation
if (typeof window.__bridgeFunction !== 'function') {
  window.__bridgeFunction = function(event, payload) {
    // Fallback implementation
  };
}
```

### Issue 2: Signature Mismatch
**Problem:** Bridge function called with wrong number/type of arguments
```javascript
// ❌ BAD: Inconsistent signatures
// Platform expects: (event, payload)
// Script calls: (combinedData)

// ✅ GOOD: Consistent two-parameter signature
bridge('eventName', { data: 'payload' });
```

### Issue 3: Timing Issues
**Problem:** Bridge accessed before it's initialized
```javascript
// ❌ BAD: Immediate use without checking
window.__bridge('event', data); // May not exist yet!

// ✅ GOOD: Defensive checking
if (typeof window.__bridge === 'function') {
  window.__bridge('event', data);
} else {
  console.warn('Bridge not ready, queueing event');
  // Queue or retry logic
}
```

## 🧪 Required Bridge Tests

### 1. Existence Test
```javascript
describe('Bridge Communication', () => {
  it('should expose bridge function to page context', async () => {
    await page.exposeFunction('__bridge', handler);
    const exists = await page.evaluate(() => {
      return typeof window.__bridge === 'function';
    });
    expect(exists).toBe(true);
  });
});
```

### 2. Non-Override Test
```javascript
it('should not override existing bridge', async () => {
  // Setup bridge
  await page.exposeFunction('__bridge', originalHandler);
  
  // Inject script that tries to override
  await page.evaluate(() => {
    if (typeof window.__bridge !== 'function') {
      window.__bridge = () => console.log('override');
    }
  });
  
  // Verify original still works
  await page.evaluate(() => window.__bridge('test', {}));
  expect(originalHandler).toHaveBeenCalledWith('test', {});
});
```

### 3. Event Routing Test
```javascript
it('should route events to correct handlers', async () => {
  const handlers = {
    openEditor: jest.fn(),
    saveFlow: jest.fn()
  };
  
  await page.exposeFunction('__bridge', (event, payload) => {
    handlers[event]?.(payload);
  });
  
  await page.evaluate(() => {
    window.__bridge('openEditor', { source: 'test' });
  });
  
  expect(handlers.openEditor).toHaveBeenCalledWith({ source: 'test' });
});
```

### 4. Timing Test
```javascript
it('should handle bridge setup timing correctly', async () => {
  // Inject script first (before bridge)
  await page.addScriptTag({ content: scriptWithBridgeCheck });
  
  // Then setup bridge
  await page.exposeFunction('__bridge', handler);
  
  // Verify script can now use bridge
  const result = await page.evaluate(() => {
    return window.__tryBridge?.('test');
  });
  
  expect(result).toBe('success');
});
```

## 🔧 Debug Tooling

### Bridge Monitor
Create a development-only bridge monitor:
```javascript
class BridgeMonitor {
  constructor() {
    this.events = [];
    this.errors = [];
  }
  
  wrap(originalBridge) {
    return (event, payload) => {
      const timestamp = Date.now();
      console.log(`[Bridge] ${event} at ${new Date().toISOString()}`);
      
      try {
        const result = originalBridge(event, payload);
        this.events.push({ event, payload, timestamp, success: true });
        return result;
      } catch (error) {
        this.errors.push({ event, payload, timestamp, error });
        console.error('[Bridge] Error:', error);
        throw error;
      }
    };
  }
  
  getStats() {
    return {
      totalEvents: this.events.length,
      errors: this.errors.length,
      averageTime: this.calculateAverage()
    };
  }
}
```

### Runtime Assertions
Add runtime checks in development:
```javascript
if (process.env.NODE_ENV === 'development') {
  // Verify bridge setup
  await page.evaluate(() => {
    const checks = {
      exists: typeof window.__bridge === 'function',
      correctSignature: window.__bridge?.length === 2,
      notOverridden: window.__bridge?.toString().includes('exposeFunction')
    };
    
    console.table(checks);
    
    if (!checks.exists) {
      console.error('❌ Bridge not found!');
    }
    if (!checks.correctSignature) {
      console.error('❌ Bridge has wrong signature!');
    }
    if (!checks.notOverridden) {
      console.warn('⚠️ Bridge may have been overridden');
    }
  });
}
```

## 📊 Testing Checklist

### Before Every Release
```markdown
- [ ] Run bridge communication test suite
- [ ] Verify no bridge override warnings in console
- [ ] Check bridge event logs for dropped events
- [ ] Test with scripts loaded in different orders
- [ ] Verify fallback mechanisms work
- [ ] Test in multiple browsers
- [ ] Check for timing race conditions
```

### For New Features
```markdown
- [ ] Add bridge tests for new events
- [ ] Document expected bridge behavior
- [ ] Test error handling for bridge failures
- [ ] Verify no conflicts with existing bridges
- [ ] Add monitoring for new event types
```

## 🚨 Anti-Patterns to Avoid

```javascript
// ❌ Silent failures
window.bridge = () => {}; // No-op override

// ❌ Assuming bridge exists
bridge('event'); // No existence check

// ❌ Inconsistent signatures
bridge(combinedObject); // vs bridge(event, payload)

// ❌ No error handling
window.bridge('event', data); // What if it throws?

// ❌ No timing consideration
// Script runs before bridge is ready
```

## ✅ Best Practices

```javascript
// ✅ Defensive bridge usage
function safeBridge(event, payload) {
  if (typeof window.__bridge === 'function') {
    try {
      return window.__bridge(event, payload);
    } catch (error) {
      console.error('Bridge error:', error);
      // Fallback logic
    }
  } else {
    console.warn('Bridge not available');
    // Queue or retry
  }
}

// ✅ Bridge setup verification
async function setupBridge() {
  // 1. Expose function
  await page.exposeFunction('__bridge', handler);
  
  // 2. Verify it exists
  const exists = await page.evaluate(() => 
    typeof window.__bridge === 'function'
  );
  
  if (!exists) {
    throw new Error('Bridge setup failed');
  }
  
  // 3. Log confirmation
  console.log('✅ Bridge ready');
}

// ✅ Guarded script injection
const overlayScript = `
  // Don't override if already exists
  if (typeof window.__bridge !== 'function') {
    // Fallback implementation
    window.__bridge = async (event, payload) => {
      // HTTP fallback
      await fetch('/bridge', {
        method: 'POST',
        body: JSON.stringify({ event, payload })
      });
    };
  }
`;
```

## 📈 Success Metrics

- **Zero Bridge Failures**: No dropped events due to bridge issues
- **100% Event Delivery**: All events reach their handlers
- **Override Prevention**: No scripts override platform bridges
- **Timing Resilience**: No race conditions in bridge setup
- **Clear Error Messages**: Failures clearly indicate bridge issues
