## 📝 Alert Documentation

### Alert Runbook Template
```markdown
# Alert: {{ AlertName }}

## Overview
Brief description of what this alert means

## Impact
- **Customer Impact**: How this affects users
- **Business Impact**: Revenue/operations impact
- **Severity**: Critical/Warning/Info

## Diagnosis Steps
1. Check dashboard: [Link to dashboard]
2. Query logs: `query here`
3. Verify upstream dependencies
4. Check recent deployments

## Remediation Steps
1. **Immediate**: Quick fix to stop bleeding
2. **Short-term**: Temporary solution
3. **Long-term**: Permanent fix

## Escalation
- **L1**: Try remediation steps (15 min)
- **L2**: Page on-call engineer if not resolved
- **L3**: Escalate to team lead after 30 min
- **L4**: Involve VP Engineering for customer impact

## Prevention
- Add monitoring for X
- Improve test coverage for Y
- Consider architectural change Z

## Related Alerts
- [Alert A]: Often fires together
- [Alert B]: May be root cause

## Historical Context
- Last occurred: Date
- Frequency: X times per month
- Common causes: List here
```

## ✅ Testing Alerts

```typescript
describe('Alert Rules', () => {
  it('should fire high error rate alert', async () => {
    // Generate error metrics
    for (let i = 0; i < 100; i++) {
      metrics.recordError('500', '/api/orders');
    }
    
    // Wait for evaluation
    await sleep(5000);
    
    // Check if alert fired
    const alerts = await alertmanager.getAlerts();
    expect(alerts).toContainEqual(
      expect.objectContaining({
        name: 'HighErrorRate',
        severity: 'critical'
      })
    );
  });
  
  it('should calculate error budget correctly', () => {
    const calculator = new ErrorBudgetCalculator();
    const result = calculator.calculateRemaining(
      { target: 99.9, window: '30d' },
      99.5 // Current performance
    );
    
    expect(result.percentage).toBeLessThan(50);
    expect(result.timeUntilExhaustion).toMatch(/\d+ hours/);
  });
});
```

## 🚨 Common Alert Anti-Patterns

```yaml
# ❌ BAD: Too sensitive, will cause alert fatigue
- alert: HighCPU
  expr: cpu_usage > 50
  for: 1m

# ❌ BAD: No actionable information
- alert: SomethingWrong
  expr: errors > 0
  annotations:
    summary: "Errors detected"

# ❌ BAD: Missing labels for routing
- alert: DatabaseSlow
  expr: db_latency > 100

# ✅ GOOD: Appropriate threshold with context
- alert: HighCPUUtilization
  expr: |
    (
      100 * (1 - avg(rate(node_cpu_seconds_total{mode="idle"}[5m]))) > 85
    )
  for: 15m
  labels:
    severity: warning
    service: "{{ $labels.instance }}"
    team: infrastructure
  annotations:
    summary: "High CPU utilization on {{ $labels.instance }}"
    description: "CPU usage is {{ $value | humanizePercentage }} for 15 minutes"
    runbook: "https://runbooks.example.com/high-cpu"
```

- **MTTA (Mean Time to Alert)**: < 2 minutes
- **False Positive Rate**: < 10%
- **Alert Response Rate**: > 90% acknowledged within SLA
- **Error Budget Burn Rate**: < 100% per period

---
description: OpenTelemetry distributed tracing setup for complete request flow visibility across microservices
alwaysApply: false

# Distributed Tracing Standards - OpenTelemetry 2025

Implement comprehensive distributed tracing using OpenTelemetry for end-to-end visibility across all services.

**Justification:** Distributed tracing reveals bottlenecks, dependencies, and failure points across complex microservice architectures.

## 🎯 Single Responsibility
This rule is ONLY responsible for trace collection and propagation. Not logging (see structured-logging) or metrics (see metrics-collection).

## 📋 Implementation Checklist

### Initial Setup
```markdown
- [ ] Install OpenTelemetry SDK and instrumentation
- [ ] Configure trace exporters (Jaeger/Zipkin/AWS X-Ray)
- [ ] Set up automatic instrumentation
- [ ] Implement manual instrumentation for business logic
- [ ] Configure sampling strategy
- [ ] Set up trace visualization (Jaeger UI/AWS X-Ray)
- [ ] Implement context propagation
- [ ] Configure trace retention policies
```

## 🔧 OpenTelemetry Setup

### Core Dependencies
```json
{
  "dependencies": {
    "@opentelemetry/api": "^1.7.0",
    "@opentelemetry/sdk-node": "^0.46.0",
    "@opentelemetry/auto-instrumentations-node": "^0.40.0",
    "@opentelemetry/exporter-trace-otlp-http": "^0.46.0",
    "@opentelemetry/exporter-jaeger": "^1.19.0",
    "@opentelemetry/instrumentation-http": "^0.46.0",
    "@opentelemetry/instrumentation-express": "^0.34.0",
    "@opentelemetry/instrumentation-aws-sdk": "^0.37.0",
    "@opentelemetry/instrumentation-redis": "^0.35.0",
    "@opentelemetry/instrumentation-pg": "^0.38.0"
  }
}
```

### Tracer Initialization
```typescript
// tracing.ts - Initialize BEFORE importing other modules
import { NodeSDK } from '@opentelemetry/sdk-node';
import { getNodeAutoInstrumentations } from '@opentelemetry/auto-instrumentations-node';
import { Resource } from '@opentelemetry/resources';
import { SemanticResourceAttributes } from '@opentelemetry/semantic-conventions';
import { OTLPTraceExporter } from '@opentelemetry/exporter-trace-otlp-http';
import { BatchSpanProcessor } from '@opentelemetry/sdk-trace-base';
import { trace, context, SpanStatusCode, SpanKind } from '@opentelemetry/api';

// Configure resource attributes
const resource = new Resource({
  [SemanticResourceAttributes.SERVICE_NAME]: process.env.SERVICE_NAME || 'unknown-service',
  [SemanticResourceAttributes.SERVICE_VERSION]: process.env.npm_package_version || '0.0.0',
  [SemanticResourceAttributes.DEPLOYMENT_ENVIRONMENT]: process.env.NODE_ENV || 'development',
  [SemanticResourceAttributes.SERVICE_NAMESPACE]: 'freshsales-app',
  [SemanticResourceAttributes.SERVICE_INSTANCE_ID]: process.env.HOSTNAME || 'local',
});

// Configure trace exporter
const traceExporter = new OTLPTraceExporter({
  url: process.env.OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT || 'http://localhost:4318/v1/traces',
  headers: {
    'x-api-key': process.env.OTEL_API_KEY || '',
  },
});

// Initialize SDK
const sdk = new NodeSDK({
  resource,
  spanProcessor: new BatchSpanProcessor(traceExporter),
  instrumentations: [
    getNodeAutoInstrumentations({
      '@opentelemetry/instrumentation-fs': {
        enabled: false, // Too noisy
      },
    }),
  ],
});

// Start SDK
sdk.start()
  .then(() => console.log('Tracing initialized'))
  .catch((error) => console.error('Error initializing tracing', error));

// Graceful shutdown
process.on('SIGTERM', () => {
  sdk.shutdown()
    .then(() => console.log('Tracing terminated'))
    .catch((error) => console.error('Error terminating tracing', error))
    .finally(() => process.exit(0));
});

export const tracer = trace.getTracer(
  process.env.SERVICE_NAME || 'app',
  process.env.npm_package_version || '0.0.0'
);
```

## 🎯 Manual Instrumentation Patterns

### Basic Span Creation
```typescript
import { tracer } from './tracing';
import { SpanStatusCode, SpanKind } from '@opentelemetry/api';

async function processOrder(orderId: string) {
  // Start a new span
  return tracer.startActiveSpan('process-order', {
    kind: SpanKind.INTERNAL,
    attributes: {
      'order.id': orderId,
      'order.source': 'web',
    },
  }, async (span) => {
    try {
      // Add events
      span.addEvent('order-validation-started');
      
      // Business logic
      const order = await validateOrder(orderId);
      
      // Add more attributes
      span.setAttributes({
        'order.total': order.total,
        'order.items': order.items.length,
        'order.customer_id': order.customerId,
      });
      
      span.addEvent('order-validation-completed');
      
      // Process payment in nested span
      await tracer.startActiveSpan('process-payment', async (paymentSpan) => {
        try {
          const result = await processPayment(order);
          paymentSpan.setStatus({ code: SpanStatusCode.OK });
          return result;
        } catch (error) {
          paymentSpan.recordException(error);
          paymentSpan.setStatus({ 
            code: SpanStatusCode.ERROR,
            message: error.message 
          });
          throw error;
        } finally {
          paymentSpan.end();
        }
      });
      
      span.setStatus({ code: SpanStatusCode.OK });
      return order;
      
    } catch (error) {
      // Record exception
      span.recordException(error);
      span.setStatus({ 
        code: SpanStatusCode.ERROR,
        message: error.message 
      });
      throw error;
    } finally {
      // Always end the span
      span.end();
    }
  });
}
```

### Database Instrumentation
```typescript
import { SpanKind } from '@opentelemetry/api';

class DatabaseClient {
  async query(sql: string, params: any[] = []) {
    return tracer.startActiveSpan(`db.query`, {
      kind: SpanKind.CLIENT,
      attributes: {
        'db.system': 'postgresql',
        'db.statement': sql.substring(0, 100), // Truncate for safety
        'db.operation': sql.split(' ')[0].toUpperCase(),
        'db.name': process.env.DB_NAME,
        'server.address': process.env.DB_HOST,
        'server.port': process.env.DB_PORT,
      },
    }, async (span) => {
      const startTime = Date.now();
      
      try {
        const result = await this.pool.query(sql, params);
        
        span.setAttributes({
          'db.rows_affected': result.rowCount,
          'db.duration': Date.now() - startTime,
        });
        
        span.setStatus({ code: SpanStatusCode.OK });
        return result;
        
      } catch (error) {
        span.recordException(error);
        span.setStatus({ 
          code: SpanStatusCode.ERROR,
          message: `Database error: ${error.message}`
        });
        throw error;
      } finally {
        span.end();
      }
    });
  }
}
```

### HTTP Client Instrumentation
```typescript
import { SpanKind, context, trace, propagation } from '@opentelemetry/api';

async function callExternalAPI(url: string, options: RequestInit = {}) {
  return tracer.startActiveSpan(`http.request`, {
    kind: SpanKind.CLIENT,
    attributes: {
      'http.method': options.method || 'GET',
      'http.url': url,
      'http.target': new URL(url).pathname,
      'server.address': new URL(url).hostname,
    },
  }, async (span) => {
    // Inject trace context into headers
    const headers = new Headers(options.headers);
    propagation.inject(context.active(), headers, {
      set: (carrier, key, value) => carrier.set(key, value),
    });
    
    try {
      const response = await fetch(url, { ...options, headers });
      
      span.setAttributes({
        'http.status_code': response.status,
        'http.response_content_length': response.headers.get('content-length'),
      });
      
      if (response.ok) {
        span.setStatus({ code: SpanStatusCode.OK });
      } else {
        span.setStatus({ code: SpanStatusCode.ERROR });
      }
      
      return response;
      
    } catch (error) {
      span.recordException(error);
      span.setStatus({ 
        code: SpanStatusCode.ERROR,
        message: error.message 
      });
      throw error;
    } finally {
      span.end();
    }
  });
}
```

### Message Queue Instrumentation
```typescript
// Producer
async function publishMessage(topic: string, message: any) {
  return tracer.startActiveSpan(`mq.publish`, {
    kind: SpanKind.PRODUCER,
    attributes: {
      'messaging.system': 'sqs',
      'messaging.destination': topic,
      'messaging.operation': 'publish',
    },
  }, async (span) => {
    // Inject trace context into message
    const carrier = {};
    propagation.inject(context.active(), carrier);
    
    const enrichedMessage = {
      ...message,
      _traceContext: carrier,
    };
    
    try {
      const result = await sqs.sendMessage({
        QueueUrl: topic,
        MessageBody: JSON.stringify(enrichedMessage),
      });
      
      span.setAttributes({
        'messaging.message_id': result.MessageId,
      });
      
      span.setStatus({ code: SpanStatusCode.OK });
      return result;
    } catch (error) {
      span.recordException(error);
      span.setStatus({ code: SpanStatusCode.ERROR });
      throw error;
    } finally {
      span.end();
    }
  });
}

// Consumer
async function consumeMessage(message: any) {
  // Extract trace context from message
  const traceContext = message._traceContext || {};
  const extractedContext = propagation.extract(context.active(), traceContext);
  
  return context.with(extractedContext, () => {
    return tracer.startActiveSpan(`mq.consume`, {
      kind: SpanKind.CONSUMER,
      attributes: {
        'messaging.system': 'sqs',
        'messaging.operation': 'consume',
        'messaging.message_id': message.MessageId,
      },
    }, async (span) => {
      try {
        await processMessage(message);
        span.setStatus({ code: SpanStatusCode.OK });
      } catch (error) {
        span.recordException(error);
        span.setStatus({ code: SpanStatusCode.ERROR });
        throw error;
      } finally {
        span.end();
      }
    });
  });
}
```

## 📊 Sampling Strategies

### Adaptive Sampling
```typescript
import { AlwaysOnSampler, TraceIdRatioBasedSampler, ParentBasedSampler } from '@opentelemetry/sdk-trace-base';

// Development: Sample everything
const devSampler = new AlwaysOnSampler();

// Production: Intelligent sampling
const prodSampler = new ParentBasedSampler({
  root: new TraceIdRatioBasedSampler(0.1), // 10% of new traces
  remoteParentSampled: new AlwaysOnSampler(), // Always sample if parent sampled
  remoteParentNotSampled: new AlwaysOffSampler(), // Never sample if parent not sampled
});

// Custom sampler for important operations
class PrioritySampler {
  shouldSample(context, traceId, spanName, spanKind, attributes) {
    // Always sample errors
    if (attributes['error'] === true) return { decision: SamplingDecision.RECORD_AND_SAMPLE };
    
    // Always sample slow requests
    if (attributes['http.duration'] > 1000) return { decision: SamplingDecision.RECORD_AND_SAMPLE };
    
    // Sample critical business operations
    if (spanName.includes('payment') || spanName.includes('order')) {
      return { decision: SamplingDecision.RECORD_AND_SAMPLE };
    }
    
    // Default sampling
    return new TraceIdRatioBasedSampler(0.01).shouldSample(context, traceId, spanName, spanKind, attributes);
  }
}
```

## 🔍 Context Propagation

### Express Middleware
```typescript
import { context, propagation, trace } from '@opentelemetry/api';

export const tracingMiddleware = (req, res, next) => {
  // Extract trace context from headers
  const extractedContext = propagation.extract(context.active(), req.headers);
  
  // Create a new span for this request
  const span = tracer.startSpan(`${req.method} ${req.path}`, {
    kind: SpanKind.SERVER,
    attributes: {
      'http.method': req.method,
      'http.url': req.url,
      'http.target': req.path,
      'http.host': req.hostname,
      'http.scheme': req.protocol,
      'http.user_agent': req.get('user-agent'),
      'client.address': req.ip,
    },
  }, extractedContext);
  
  // Store span in request for later use
  req.span = span;
  
  // Continue with span context
  context.with(trace.setSpan(extractedContext, span), () => {
    // Capture response
    const originalSend = res.send;
    res.send = function(data) {
      span.setAttributes({
        'http.status_code': res.statusCode,
        'http.response_content_length': Buffer.byteLength(data),
      });
      
      if (res.statusCode >= 400) {
        span.setStatus({ code: SpanStatusCode.ERROR });
      } else {
        span.setStatus({ code: SpanStatusCode.OK });
      }
      
      span.end();
      return originalSend.call(this, data);
    };
    
    next();
  });
};
```

## ✅ Testing Traces

```typescript
import { InMemorySpanExporter, SimpleSpanProcessor } from '@opentelemetry/sdk-trace-base';

describe('Tracing', () => {
  let memoryExporter: InMemorySpanExporter;
  
  beforeEach(() => {
    memoryExporter = new InMemorySpanExporter();
    const provider = new NodeTracerProvider();
    provider.addSpanProcessor(new SimpleSpanProcessor(memoryExporter));
    provider.register();
  });
  
  it('should create spans for API calls', async () => {
    await processOrder('123');
    
    const spans = memoryExporter.getFinishedSpans();
    expect(spans).toHaveLength(2);
    expect(spans[0].name).toBe('process-payment');
    expect(spans[1].name).toBe('process-order');
    expect(spans[1].attributes['order.id']).toBe('123');
  });
});
```

## 🚨 Common Issues

```typescript
// ❌ BAD: Starting spans without ending them
const span = tracer.startSpan('operation');
// Missing span.end() - memory leak!

// ❌ BAD: Not propagating context
fetch(url); // Trace context lost

// ❌ BAD: Including sensitive data in spans
span.setAttribute('user.password', password);

// ✅ GOOD: Proper span lifecycle with context
tracer.startActiveSpan('operation', async (span) => {
  try {
    const result = await doWork();
    span.setStatus({ code: SpanStatusCode.OK });
    return result;
  } catch (error) {
    span.recordException(error);
    span.setStatus({ code: SpanStatusCode.ERROR });
    throw error;
  } finally {
    span.end();
  }
});
```

---
description: Incident response procedures, on-call rotation, escalation policies, and postmortem processes
alwaysApply: false
---
# Incident Management & Response - 2025

Comprehensive incident response framework with clear roles, communication protocols, and learning processes.

**Justification:** Well-defined incident management reduces MTTR, improves communication during outages, and prevents repeat incidents through systematic learning.

## 🎯 Single Responsibility
This rule defines HOW to respond to incidents. Not what to alert on (see alert-definitions) or how to monitor (see metrics-collection).

## 📋 Incident Response Checklist

### Preparation Phase
```markdown
- [ ] Define incident severity levels
- [ ] Establish on-call rotation
- [ ] Create communication channels
- [ ] Set up incident command structure
- [ ] Prepare runbook templates
- [ ] Configure incident tracking system
- [ ] Train incident commanders
- [ ] Set up war room procedures
- [ ] Define stakeholder communication
- [ ] Create postmortem template
```

## 🚨 Incident Severity Levels

### Severity Matrix
```yaml
severity_levels:
  SEV-1:
    name: "Critical"
    description: "Complete service outage or data loss"
    impact: "All users affected, revenue impact"
    response_time: "< 5 minutes"
    escalation: "Immediate page to on-call and management"
    communication: "Executive team, all customers"
    examples:
      - "Production database down"
      - "Payment processing failure"
      - "Security breach"
      - "Complete API outage"

  SEV-2:
    name: "Major"
    description: "Significant degradation or partial outage"
    impact: "Many users affected, feature unavailable"
    response_time: "< 15 minutes"
    escalation: "Page on-call engineer"
    communication: "Affected customers, status page"
    examples:
      - "Login failures for subset of users"
      - "Slow API responses (>5s)"
      - "Search functionality down"

  SEV-3:
    name: "Minor"
    description: "Minor feature issue or degradation"
    impact: "Some users affected, workaround available"
    response_time: "< 1 hour"
    escalation: "Notify on-call via Slack"
    communication: "Internal team, status page"
    examples:
      - "UI glitch in specific browser"
      - "Non-critical background job failure"
      - "Delayed notifications"

  SEV-4:
    name: "Low"
    description: "Minimal impact issue"
    impact: "Few users affected, cosmetic issues"
    response_time: "< 4 hours"
    escalation: "Create ticket"
    communication: "Internal tracking only"
    examples:
      - "Typo in UI"
      - "Non-critical log errors"
      - "Minor performance degradation"
```

## 👥 Incident Command Structure

### Roles & Responsibilities
```typescript
interface IncidentRoles {
  incidentCommander: {
    responsibilities: [
      'Overall incident coordination',
      'Decision making authority',
      'External communication',
      'Resource allocation',
      'Postmortem ownership'
    ];
    requirements: [
      'Senior engineer or above',
      'Incident command training',
      'System architecture knowledge'
    ];
  };

  technicalLead: {
    responsibilities: [
      'Technical investigation',
      'Solution implementation',
      'Technical decision making',
      'Team coordination'
    ];
    requirements: [
      'Deep system knowledge',
      'Debugging expertise'
    ];
  };

  communicationsLead: {
    responsibilities: [
      'Customer communication',
      'Status page updates',
      'Internal updates',
      'Stakeholder management'
    ];
    requirements: [
      'Clear communication skills',
      'Customer empathy'
    ];
  };

  scribe: {
    responsibilities: [
      'Document timeline',
      'Record actions taken',
      'Track open items',
      'Prepare incident report'
    ];
    requirements: [
      'Attention to detail',
      'Fast note-taking'
    ];
  };
}
```

## 📞 On-Call Rotation

### PagerDuty Configuration
```yaml
# pagerduty-schedule.yml
schedules:
  primary:
    name: "Platform Team Primary"
    rotation_type: "weekly"
    handoff_time: "09:00 PST Monday"
    layers:
      - name: "Weekly Rotation"
        users:
          - email: "engineer1@example.com"
          - email: "engineer2@example.com"
          - email: "engineer3@example.com"
        rotation_virtual_start: "2024-01-01T09:00:00-08:00"
        rotation_turn_length_seconds: 604800 # 1 week

  secondary:
    name: "Platform Team Secondary"
    rotation_type: "weekly"
    handoff_time: "09:00 PST Monday"
    escalation_delay: 15 # minutes

escalation_policies:
  - name: "Platform Escalation"
    num_loops: 0
    escalation_rules:
      - escalation_delay_in_minutes: 0
        targets:
          - type: "schedule_reference"
            id: "primary_schedule_id"
      - escalation_delay_in_minutes: 15
        targets:
          - type: "schedule_reference"
            id: "secondary_schedule_id"
      - escalation_delay_in_minutes: 30
        targets:
          - type: "user_reference"
            id: "engineering_manager_id"
```

## 📊 Postmortem Process

### Postmortem Template
```markdown
# Postmortem: [Incident Title]

## Incident Summary
- **Date**: YYYY-MM-DD
- **Duration**: X hours Y minutes
- **Impact**: [User/Revenue impact]
- **Severity**: SEV-X

## Timeline
| Time | Event | Actor |
|------|-------|-------|
| HH:MM | Alert triggered | System |
| HH:MM | On-call acknowledged | @engineer |
| HH:MM | Investigation started | @engineer |
| HH:MM | Root cause identified | @team |
| HH:MM | Fix deployed | @engineer |
| HH:MM | Incident resolved | @engineer |

## Root Cause Analysis

### What Happened
[Detailed explanation of the incident]

### Root Cause
[The fundamental reason the incident occurred]

### Contributing Factors
1. [Factor 1]
2. [Factor 2]
3. [Factor 3]

## Resolution
[How the incident was resolved]

## Lessons Learned

### What Went Well
- ✅ [Positive aspect 1]
- ✅ [Positive aspect 2]

### What Could Be Improved
- ⚠️ [Improvement area 1]
- ⚠️ [Improvement area 2]

## Action Items
| Action | Owner | Due Date | Priority |
|--------|-------|----------|----------|
| Add monitoring for X | @engineer1 | YYYY-MM-DD | High |
| Update runbook for Y | @engineer2 | YYYY-MM-DD | Medium |
| Implement circuit breaker | @engineer3 | YYYY-MM-DD | High |

## Supporting Data
- [Link to graphs]
- [Link to logs]
- [Link to traces]
```

## 🎯 Incident Metrics

### Key Performance Indicators
```typescript
interface IncidentKPIs {
  MTTD: number;  // Mean Time To Detect
  MTTA: number;  // Mean Time To Acknowledge  
  MTTR: number;  // Mean Time To Resolve
  MTBF: number;  // Mean Time Between Failures
  
  incidentFrequency: {
    sev1: number;  // per month
    sev2: number;
    sev3: number;
    sev4: number;
  };
  
  customerImpact: {
    affectedUsers: number;
    downtime: number;  // minutes
    revenueImpact: number;  // dollars
  };
  
  responseQuality: {
    slaCompliance: number;  // percentage
    postmortemCompletion: number;  // percentage
    actionItemCompletion: number;  // percentage
    repeatIncidents: number;  // count
  };
}

// Monthly targets
const incidentTargets = {
  MTTD: 5,    // minutes
  MTTA: 5,    // minutes
  MTTR: 30,   // minutes
  MTBF: 720,  // hours (30 days)
  
  slaCompliance: 99,  // percent
  postmortemCompletion: 100,  // percent
  actionItemCompletion: 90,  // percent
};
```

## 📊 Success Metrics

- **MTTR**: < 30 minutes for SEV-1
- **MTTD**: < 5 minutes for critical issues
- **SLA Compliance**: > 99%
- **Postmortem Completion**: 100% for SEV-1/2
- **Action Item Completion**: > 90% within deadline
- **Repeat Incident Rate**: < 10%
- **On-Call Happiness**: > 7/10 survey score

==== RULE FILE: .cursor/rules/10-observability/metrics-collection.mdc ====
---
globs: **/*.ts, **/*.js, **/server.js, **/main.ts, **/handler.ts
description: Application and business metrics collection using Prometheus/OpenTelemetry for comprehensive monitoring
alwaysApply: false
---
# Metrics Collection Standards - 2025

Implement comprehensive metrics collection for RED (Rate, Errors, Duration) and USE (Utilization, Saturation, Errors) methodologies.

**Justification:** Metrics provide quantitative insights into system health, performance, and business KPIs enabling proactive issue detection.

## 🎯 Single Responsibility
This rule is ONLY responsible for defining WHAT metrics to collect and HOW to collect them. Not alerting (see alert-definitions) or visualization (see dashboards).

## 📋 Implementation Checklist

### Initial Setup
```markdown
- [ ] Choose metrics library (Prometheus client, OpenTelemetry Metrics)
- [ ] Set up metrics endpoint (/metrics)
- [ ] Configure metric types (Counter, Gauge, Histogram, Summary)
- [ ] Implement RED metrics (Rate, Errors, Duration)
- [ ] Implement USE metrics (Utilization, Saturation, Errors)
- [ ] Add business KPI metrics
- [ ] Configure Prometheus scraping
- [ ] Set up Grafana dashboards
- [ ] Define SLIs and SLOs
- [ ] Configure metric retention
```

## 📊 Metrics Types & Usage

### Counter - Monotonically Increasing Values
```typescript
import { Counter } from 'prom-client';

// Define counters
const httpRequestsTotal = new Counter({
  name: 'http_requests_total',
  help: 'Total number of HTTP requests',
  labelNames: ['method', 'route', 'status_code'],
});

const ordersProcessedTotal = new Counter({
  name: 'orders_processed_total',
  help: 'Total number of orders processed',
  labelNames: ['status', 'payment_method', 'region'],
});

// Usage
httpRequestsTotal.inc({ 
  method: 'POST', 
  route: '/api/orders', 
  status_code: '200' 
});

ordersProcessedTotal.inc({ 
  status: 'completed',
  payment_method: 'credit_card',
  region: 'us-west'
});
```

### Gauge - Values That Go Up and Down
```typescript
import { Gauge } from 'prom-client';

// Define gauges
const activeConnections = new Gauge({
  name: 'active_connections',
  help: 'Number of active connections',
  labelNames: ['service'],
});

const queueDepth = new Gauge({
  name: 'queue_depth',
  help: 'Current depth of the message queue',
  labelNames: ['queue_name', 'priority'],
});

const memoryUsageBytes = new Gauge({
  name: 'memory_usage_bytes',
  help: 'Current memory usage in bytes',
  labelNames: ['type'], // heap, rss, external
});

// Usage
activeConnections.set({ service: 'webhook' }, 42);
queueDepth.inc({ queue_name: 'orders', priority: 'high' }); // Increment
queueDepth.dec({ queue_name: 'orders', priority: 'high' }); // Decrement
```

### Histogram - Distribution of Values
```typescript
import { Histogram } from 'prom-client';

// Define histograms with buckets
const httpRequestDuration = new Histogram({
  name: 'http_request_duration_seconds',
  help: 'Duration of HTTP requests in seconds',
  labelNames: ['method', 'route', 'status_code'],
  buckets: [0.1, 0.3, 0.5, 0.7, 1, 3, 5, 7, 10], // in seconds
});

const databaseQueryDuration = new Histogram({
  name: 'db_query_duration_seconds',
  help: 'Duration of database queries in seconds',
  labelNames: ['operation', 'table'],
  buckets: [0.001, 0.005, 0.01, 0.05, 0.1, 0.5, 1, 5],
});

// Usage with timer
const end = httpRequestDuration.startTimer({ 
  method: 'GET', 
  route: '/api/users' 
});
// ... do work ...
end({ status_code: '200' }); // Automatically records duration
```

### Summary - Percentiles Over Time Window
```typescript
import { Summary } from 'prom-client';

const apiLatency = new Summary({
  name: 'api_latency_seconds',
  help: 'API latency in seconds',
  labelNames: ['endpoint'],
  percentiles: [0.5, 0.9, 0.95, 0.99], // Calculate these percentiles
  maxAgeSeconds: 600, // 10-minute sliding window
  ageBuckets: 5, // Number of buckets for sliding window
});

// Usage
apiLatency.observe({ endpoint: '/api/orders' }, 0.123);
```

## 🔴 RED Metrics (Request-Oriented)

### Rate - Requests Per Second
```typescript
class REDMetrics {
  private requestRate = new Counter({
    name: 'http_requests_total',
    help: 'Total HTTP requests',
    labelNames: ['method', 'endpoint', 'service'],
  });

  recordRequest(method: string, endpoint: string) {
    this.requestRate.inc({
      method,
      endpoint,
      service: process.env.SERVICE_NAME,
    });
  }
}
```

### Errors - Error Rate
```typescript
private errorRate = new Counter({
  name: 'http_errors_total',
  help: 'Total HTTP errors',
  labelNames: ['method', 'endpoint', 'status_code', 'error_type'],
});

recordError(method: string, endpoint: string, statusCode: number, error?: Error) {
  this.errorRate.inc({
    method,
    endpoint,
    status_code: statusCode.toString(),
    error_type: error?.name || 'unknown',
  });
}
```

### Duration - Request Latency
```typescript
private requestDuration = new Histogram({
  name: 'http_request_duration_seconds',
  help: 'HTTP request duration in seconds',
  labelNames: ['method', 'endpoint', 'status_code'],
  buckets: [0.005, 0.01, 0.025, 0.05, 0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2.5, 5, 10],
});

// Express middleware
app.use((req, res, next) => {
  const timer = this.requestDuration.startTimer({
    method: req.method,
    endpoint: req.route?.path || req.path,
  });

  res.on('finish', () => {
    timer({ status_code: res.statusCode.toString() });
  });

  next();
});
```

## 🔧 USE Metrics (Resource-Oriented)

### Utilization - Average Resource Use
```typescript
class USEMetrics {
  private cpuUtilization = new Gauge({
    name: 'cpu_utilization_ratio',
    help: 'CPU utilization from 0 to 1',
    labelNames: ['service'],
  });

  private memoryUtilization = new Gauge({
    name: 'memory_utilization_ratio',
    help: 'Memory utilization from 0 to 1',
    labelNames: ['service', 'type'],
  });

  collectResourceMetrics() {
    // CPU utilization
    const cpuUsage = process.cpuUsage();
    const cpuPercent = (cpuUsage.user + cpuUsage.system) / 1000000; // Convert to seconds
    this.cpuUtilization.set({ service: process.env.SERVICE_NAME }, cpuPercent);

    // Memory utilization
    const memUsage = process.memoryUsage();
    this.memoryUtilization.set(
      { service: process.env.SERVICE_NAME, type: 'heap' },
      memUsage.heapUsed / memUsage.heapTotal
    );
    this.memoryUtilization.set(
      { service: process.env.SERVICE_NAME, type: 'rss' },
      memUsage.rss / os.totalmem()
    );
  }
}
```

### Saturation - Queue Depth, Thread Pool Usage
```typescript
private queueSaturation = new Gauge({
  name: 'queue_saturation_ratio',
  help: 'Queue saturation from 0 to 1',
  labelNames: ['queue_name'],
});

private threadPoolSaturation = new Gauge({
  name: 'thread_pool_saturation_ratio',
  help: 'Thread pool saturation from 0 to 1',
  labelNames: ['pool_name'],
});

recordQueueMetrics(queueName: string, currentSize: number, maxSize: number) {
  this.queueSaturation.set(
    { queue_name: queueName },
    currentSize / maxSize
  );
}
```

### Errors - Resource Errors
```typescript
private resourceErrors = new Counter({
  name: 'resource_errors_total',
  help: 'Total resource errors',
  labelNames: ['resource_type', 'error_type'],
});

recordResourceError(resourceType: string, errorType: string) {
  this.resourceErrors.inc({
    resource_type: resourceType, // 'database', 'cache', 'queue'
    error_type: errorType, // 'timeout', 'connection_refused', 'pool_exhausted'
  });
}
```

## 💼 Business KPI Metrics

```typescript
class BusinessMetrics {
  // Revenue metrics
  private orderValue = new Histogram({
    name: 'order_value_dollars',
    help: 'Order value in dollars',
    labelNames: ['product_category', 'customer_segment'],
    buckets: [10, 50, 100, 500, 1000, 5000, 10000],
  });

  // Conversion metrics
  private conversionRate = new Gauge({
    name: 'conversion_rate_ratio',
    help: 'Conversion rate from 0 to 1',
    labelNames: ['funnel_stage', 'channel'],
  });

  // User engagement
  private activeUsers = new Gauge({
    name: 'active_users_count',
    help: 'Number of active users',
    labelNames: ['time_window', 'user_type'],
  });

  // Feature adoption
  private featureUsage = new Counter({
    name: 'feature_usage_total',
    help: 'Feature usage count',
    labelNames: ['feature_name', 'user_segment'],
  });

  // Customer satisfaction
  private npsScore = new Gauge({
    name: 'nps_score',
    help: 'Net Promoter Score',
    labelNames: ['segment'],
  });

  recordOrder(value: number, category: string, segment: string) {
    this.orderValue.observe(
      { product_category: category, customer_segment: segment },
      value
    );
  }

  updateConversionRate(stage: string, channel: string, rate: number) {
    this.conversionRate.set(
      { funnel_stage: stage, channel: channel },
      rate
    );
  }
}
```

## 🔌 Metrics Endpoint Setup

### Express Metrics Endpoint
```typescript
import { register, collectDefaultMetrics } from 'prom-client';
import express from 'express';

const app = express();

// Collect default Node.js metrics
collectDefaultMetrics({
  prefix: 'node_',
  gcDurationBuckets: [0.001, 0.01, 0.1, 1, 2, 5],
});

// Metrics endpoint
app.get('/metrics', async (req, res) => {
  try {
    res.set('Content-Type', register.contentType);
    const metrics = await register.metrics();
    res.end(metrics);
  } catch (error) {
    res.status(500).end(error);
  }
});

// Health check endpoint
app.get('/health', (req, res) => {
  res.json({ status: 'healthy', timestamp: new Date().toISOString() });
});
```

### OpenTelemetry Metrics
```typescript
import { MeterProvider, PeriodicExportingMetricReader } from '@opentelemetry/sdk-metrics';
import { PrometheusExporter } from '@opentelemetry/exporter-prometheus';
import { metrics } from '@opentelemetry/api';

// Create Prometheus exporter
const prometheusExporter = new PrometheusExporter({
  port: 9090,
  endpoint: '/metrics',
}, () => {
  console.log('Prometheus metrics server started on port 9090');
});

// Create meter provider
const meterProvider = new MeterProvider({
  readers: [prometheusExporter],
});

// Set global meter provider
metrics.setGlobalMeterProvider(meterProvider);

// Get meter for your service
const meter = metrics.getMeter(process.env.SERVICE_NAME, process.env.npm_package_version);

// Create metrics
const requestCounter = meter.createCounter('requests', {
  description: 'Count of requests',
});

const requestDuration = meter.createHistogram('request_duration', {
  description: 'Duration of requests',
  unit: 'ms',
});
```

```json
    "title": "Freshsales App Metrics",
    "panels": [
      {
        "title": "Request Rate",

          "expr": "rate(http_requests_total[5m])",
          "legendFormat": "{{method}} {{endpoint}}"
        }]

      {
        "title": "Error Rate",
        "targets": [{

        }]
      },
      {
        "title": "P95 Latency",
        "targets": [{
          "expr": "histogram_quantile(0.95, rate(http_request_duration_seconds_bucket[5m]))",
          "legendFormat": "{{endpoint}}"
        }]
      },
      {
        "title": "Memory Usage",
        "targets": [{
          "expr": "process_resident_memory_bytes",
          "legendFormat": "{{service}}"
        }]
      }
    ]
  }
}
```

## ✅ Testing Metrics

```typescript
import { register } from 'prom-client';

describe('Metrics', () => {
  beforeEach(() => {
    register.clear();
  });

  it('should increment request counter', async () => {
    const metrics = new REDMetrics();
    
    metrics.recordRequest('GET', '/api/users');
    metrics.recordRequest('GET', '/api/users');
    
    const metricsText = await register.metrics();
    expect(metricsText).toContain('http_requests_total{method="GET",endpoint="/api/users"} 2');
  });

  it('should record request duration', async () => {
    const metrics = new REDMetrics();
    
    const timer = metrics.startTimer('GET', '/api/users');
    await new Promise(resolve => setTimeout(resolve, 100));
    timer({ status_code: '200' });
    
    const metricsText = await register.metrics();
    expect(metricsText).toContain('http_request_duration_seconds_bucket');
  });
});
```

## 🚨 Anti-Patterns

```typescript
// ❌ BAD: High cardinality labels
httpRequestsTotal.inc({ 
  user_id: userId, // Millions of unique values!
  timestamp: Date.now() // Infinite cardinality!
});

// ❌ BAD: Not using labels
const getUsersTotal = new Counter({ name: 'get_users_total' });
const postUsersTotal = new Counter({ name: 'post_users_total' });

// ❌ BAD: Wrong metric type
const requestCount = new Gauge({ // Should be Counter!
  name: 'request_count'
});

// ✅ GOOD: Proper labels and types
const httpRequests = new Counter({
  name: 'http_requests_total',
  labelNames: ['method', 'endpoint', 'status_code'],
});
```

==== RULE FILE: .cursor/rules/10-observability/structured-logging.mdc ====
---
description: Structured JSON logging standards for consistent, searchable, and analyzable logs across all services
alwaysApply: false
---
# Structured Logging Standards - 2025

Implement structured JSON logging with correlation IDs, proper levels, and PII redaction for complete observability.

**Justification:** Structured logs enable rapid debugging, compliance auditing, and automated alerting while protecting sensitive data.

## 🎯 Single Responsibility
This rule is ONLY responsible for defining HOW to log. Not when to log (that's in monitoring rules) or what to monitor (that's in metrics rules).

## 📋 Implementation Checklist

### Initial Setup
```markdown
- [ ] Choose logging library (Winston, Pino, Bunyan)
- [ ] Configure JSON output format
- [ ] Set up log levels (DEBUG, INFO, WARN, ERROR, FATAL)
- [ ] Implement correlation ID generation
- [ ] Configure PII redaction
- [ ] Set up log rotation
- [ ] Configure async logging for performance
```

## 📐 Log Structure Standard

### Required Fields (Every Log Entry)
```typescript
interface StandardLogEntry {
  // Metadata
  timestamp: string;        // ISO 8601 format
  level: LogLevel;         // DEBUG | INFO | WARN | ERROR | FATAL
  service: string;         // Service name from package.json
  version: string;         // Service version
  environment: string;     // dev | staging | prod
  
  // Correlation
  correlationId: string;   // Request correlation ID
  traceId?: string;       // OpenTelemetry trace ID
  spanId?: string;        // OpenTelemetry span ID
  userId?: string;        // Authenticated user ID (hashed)
  sessionId?: string;     // Session identifier
  
  // Context
  message: string;        // Human-readable message
  context?: object;       // Structured context data
  
  // Location
  file?: string;          // Source file
  function?: string;      // Function name
  line?: number;          // Line number
}
```

### Log Level Guidelines
```typescript
enum LogLevel {
  DEBUG = 'debug',    // Detailed diagnostic info (dev only)
  INFO = 'info',      // General informational messages
  WARN = 'warn',      // Warning conditions
  ERROR = 'error',    // Error conditions
  FATAL = 'fatal'     // Fatal errors requiring immediate attention
}

// Usage examples
logger.debug('Cache hit', { key, ttl });                    // DEBUG
logger.info('User login successful', { userId });           // INFO
logger.warn('Rate limit approaching', { current, max });    // WARN
logger.error('Database connection failed', { error });      // ERROR
logger.fatal('Service cannot start', { missingConfig });    // FATAL
```

## 🔒 PII Redaction

### Automatic Redaction Patterns
```typescript
const PII_PATTERNS = {
  email: /\b[A-Za-z0-9._%+-]+@[A-Za-z0-9.-]+\.[A-Z|a-z]{2,}\b/g,
  phone: /\b\d{3}[-.]?\d{3}[-.]?\d{4}\b/g,
  ssn: /\b\d{3}-\d{2}-\d{4}\b/g,
  creditCard: /\b\d{4}[\s-]?\d{4}[\s-]?\d{4}[\s-]?\d{4}\b/g,
  ipAddress: /\b(?:\d{1,3}\.){3}\d{1,3}\b/g,
  apiKey: /\b[A-Za-z0-9]{32,}\b/g,
};

// Redaction function
function redactPII(obj: any): any {
  const sensitiveFields = ['password', 'token', 'secret', 'apiKey', 'authorization'];
  // Implementation to recursively redact sensitive data
  return redactedObj;
}
```

### HIPAA Mode (When Enabled)
```typescript
if (process.env.HIPAA_MODE === 'true') {
  // Additional PHI field redaction
  const PHI_FIELDS = ['diagnosis', 'treatment', 'medication', 'dob', 'mrn'];
  // Only log PHI with explicit audit flag
  logger.audit('PHI Access', { 
    action: 'view',
    resource: 'patient_record',
    justification: 'treatment'
  });
}
```

## 🔄 Correlation ID Implementation

### Generate and Propagate
```typescript
import { v4 as uuidv4 } from 'uuid';

// Middleware for Express
export const correlationMiddleware = (req, res, next) => {
  const correlationId = req.headers['x-correlation-id'] || uuidv4();
  req.correlationId = correlationId;
  res.setHeader('x-correlation-id', correlationId);
  
  // Attach to async context
  AsyncLocalStorage.run({ correlationId }, next);
};

// Logger with correlation
class CorrelatedLogger {
  private getCorrelationId(): string {
    return AsyncLocalStorage.get()?.correlationId || 'no-correlation';
  }
  
  log(level: LogLevel, message: string, context?: object) {
    const entry = {
      timestamp: new Date().toISOString(),
      level,
      correlationId: this.getCorrelationId(),
      message,
      ...context
    };
    this.output(entry);
  }
}
```

## 🎨 Logging Patterns

### Service Startup
```typescript
logger.info('Service starting', {
  service: 'webhook-service',
  version: process.env.npm_package_version,
  node: process.version,
  environment: process.env.NODE_ENV,
  config: {
    port: CONFIG.PORT,
    features: CONFIG.FEATURES // Non-sensitive config only
  }
});
```

### API Request Logging
```typescript
// Request start
logger.info('API request received', {
  method: req.method,
  path: req.path,
  correlationId: req.correlationId,
  userId: req.user?.id,
  ip: req.ip // Redact in production
});

// Request complete
logger.info('API request completed', {
  method: req.method,
  path: req.path,
  correlationId: req.correlationId,
  statusCode: res.statusCode,
  duration: Date.now() - startTime,
  contentLength: res.get('content-length')
});
```

### Error Logging
```typescript
logger.error('Operation failed', {
  error: {
    name: error.name,
    message: error.message,
    stack: process.env.NODE_ENV === 'development' ? error.stack : undefined,
    code: error.code,
    details: error.details
  },
  operation: 'createContact',
  input: redactPII(input),
  correlationId
});
```

### Performance Logging
```typescript
const timer = logger.startTimer();
await someOperation();
timer.done({ 
  message: 'Operation completed',
  operation: 'databaseQuery',
  query: 'SELECT * FROM users', // Sanitized query
  rows: result.length
});
```

## 🔧 Library Configurations

### Winston Configuration
```typescript
import winston from 'winston';

const logger = winston.createLogger({
  level: process.env.LOG_LEVEL || 'info',
  format: winston.format.combine(
    winston.format.timestamp(),
    winston.format.errors({ stack: true }),
    winston.format.json(),
    winston.format.printf(({ timestamp, level, message, ...meta }) => {
      return JSON.stringify({
        timestamp,
        level,
        message,
        service: process.env.SERVICE_NAME,
        version: process.env.npm_package_version,
        ...meta
      });
    })
  ),
  defaultMeta: {
    service: process.env.SERVICE_NAME,
    environment: process.env.NODE_ENV
  },
  transports: [
    new winston.transports.Console(),
    new winston.transports.File({ 
      filename: 'error.log', 
      level: 'error',
      maxsize: 5242880, // 5MB
      maxFiles: 5
    })
  ]
});
```

### Pino Configuration (Faster)
```typescript
import pino from 'pino';

const logger = pino({
  level: process.env.LOG_LEVEL || 'info',
  formatters: {
    level: (label) => ({ level: label }),
    bindings: (bindings) => ({
      pid: bindings.pid,
      hostname: bindings.hostname,
      service: process.env.SERVICE_NAME,
      version: process.env.npm_package_version,
      environment: process.env.NODE_ENV
    })
  },
  timestamp: pino.stdTimeFunctions.isoTime,
  redact: {
    paths: ['password', 'token', '*.secret', '*.apiKey'],
    censor: '[REDACTED]'
  },
  transport: {
    target: 'pino-pretty',
    options: {
      colorize: process.env.NODE_ENV === 'development'
    }
  }
});
```

## 📊 Log Aggregation Setup

### CloudWatch Logs (AWS)
```typescript
import { CloudWatchLogs } from '@aws-sdk/client-cloudwatch-logs';

class CloudWatchTransport {
  async send(logs: LogEntry[]) {
    const params = {
      logGroupName: `/aws/application/${process.env.SERVICE_NAME}`,
      logStreamName: `${process.env.NODE_ENV}/${process.env.HOSTNAME}`,
      logEvents: logs.map(log => ({
        message: JSON.stringify(log),
        timestamp: Date.now()
      }))
    };
    await this.client.putLogEvents(params);
  }
}
```

## ✅ Testing Logs

### Unit Test Example
```typescript
describe('Logger', () => {
  it('should include correlation ID in all logs', () => {
    const correlationId = 'test-correlation-id';
    const spy = jest.spyOn(console, 'log');
    
    logger.info('Test message', { correlationId });
    
    expect(spy).toHaveBeenCalledWith(
      expect.stringContaining(correlationId)
    );
  });
  
  it('should redact sensitive information', () => {
    const sensitiveData = {
      email: 'user@example.com',
      password: 'secret123'
    };
    
    const logged = logger.formatLog('Test', sensitiveData);
    
    expect(logged).not.toContain('user@example.com');
    expect(logged).not.toContain('secret123');
    expect(logged).toContain('[REDACTED]');
  });
});
```

## 🚨 Anti-Patterns to Avoid

```typescript
// ❌ BAD: Unstructured string concatenation
console.log('User ' + userId + ' logged in at ' + new Date());

// ❌ BAD: Logging sensitive data
logger.info('Login attempt', { password: userPassword });

// ❌ BAD: Missing correlation ID
logger.error('Database error', { error: err });

// ❌ BAD: Synchronous logging in hot path
fs.appendFileSync('app.log', logMessage);

// ✅ GOOD: Structured, async, correlated, redacted
logger.info('User login', {
  userId: hashUserId(userId),
  correlationId,
  timestamp: new Date().toISOString()
});
```

- **Correlation Rate**: > 95% of logs have correlation IDs
- **PII Leakage**: 0 instances in production logs
- **Log Query Time**: < 5 seconds for 24-hour searches
- **Log Retention**: 30 days hot, 90 days cold storage

==== RULE FILE: .cursor/rules/11-security/audit-logging.mdc ====
---
globs: apps/chrome-extensions/PMHNP-Administration-SummerySky/**,libs/summerysky/**,libs/Google-Chrome-Extension-Library/**
alwaysApply: false
description: "Audit logging for compliance and troubleshooting"
---
# Audit Logging Standards

Maintain a minimal, non-PHI audit trail for regulated workflows and user actions.

## Requirements
- Record key workflow events: artifact import, parse, redaction, draft import, validation, export, and issue report creation.
- Use correlation ids to link events across background, content, and UI contexts.
- Store only non-PHI identifiers (patientKey, encounterId); hash any external ids.
- Make audit logs append-only; avoid editing or deleting individual entries (purge by TTL only).

## Event Shape
Each audit entry should include:
- timestamp (ISO-8601)
- eventType
- correlationId
- patientKey (non-PHI)
- encounterId (if applicable)
- actor (user action vs automation)
- outcome (success/failure) and errorCode (non-PHI)

## Retention
- Retain audit logs with TTL and max size bounds.
- Provide export only through the redacted issue bundle workflow.



==== RULE FILE: .cursor/rules/11-security/authentication-patterns.mdc ====
---
globs: apps/chrome-extensions/PMHNP-Administration-SummerySky/**,libs/summerysky/**,libs/Google-Chrome-Extension-Library/**
alwaysApply: false
description: "Authentication patterns for extension-based OAuth"
---
# Authentication Patterns (OAuth in MV3)

Use secure, least-privilege OAuth flows and avoid persisting access tokens.

## Requirements
- Use `chrome.identity` for OAuth flows; do not embed client secrets.
- Request least-privilege scopes; prefer `drive.file` and `documents` over broad Drive access.
- Do not persist access tokens; use short-lived tokens from `getAuthToken` and remove cached tokens on disconnect.
- Handle token expiry and `invalid_grant` by prompting for re-auth.
- Store only non-sensitive connection state (connected email, root folder id) and treat it as sensitive config.

## Verification
- Add unit tests for token retrieval, cache removal, and 401/403 re-auth handling.

# Authorization and Role Gating

If role-based access is introduced, enforce explicit permissions and least privilege.

## Requirements
- Define roles and permissions in a single shared module.
- Gate high-risk actions (bulk export, data purge, debug bundle export) behind explicit user confirmation.
- Keep authorization checks in the orchestration layer, not just in UI controls.

## Notes
- Today the extension is single-user; treat this as future-proofing, not active enforcement.



==== RULE FILE: .cursor/rules/11-security/data-encryption.mdc ====
---
globs: apps/chrome-extensions/PMHNP-Administration-SummerySky/**,libs/summerysky/**,libs/Google-Chrome-Extension-Library/**
alwaysApply: false
description: "Encryption at rest and in transit for SummerySky data"
---
# Data Encryption Standards

All PHI and sensitive configuration must be encrypted at rest and protected during transport.

## At Rest
- Use WebCrypto AES-GCM with unique IVs per record.
- Store encrypted payloads in IndexedDB; store only encrypted blobs in `chrome.storage.*`.
- Never persist raw keys alongside ciphertext; derive keys from a secure local key source and rotate on schema version changes.
- Include schema version and algorithm metadata with encrypted records for future migrations.

## In Transit
- Only send de-identified data to external AI providers.
- Use HTTPS-only endpoints; verify OAuth scopes and audiences.

## Key Management
- Scope keys per profile and per data domain when feasible (e.g., MPR vs redaction map).
- Provide a safe re-key path for schema upgrades; keep migrations idempotent.

## Verification
- Add tests that assert no plaintext PHI is written to storage or logs.
- Run encryption/decryption round-trip tests for all PHI-bearing models.



==== RULE FILE: .cursor/rules/11-security/data-retention.mdc ====
---
globs: apps/chrome-extensions/PMHNP-Administration-SummerySky/**,libs/summerysky/**,libs/Google-Chrome-Extension-Library/**
alwaysApply: false
description: "Retention and purge policies for local data"
---
# Data Retention and Purge

Ensure all local data has explicit retention boundaries and user-controlled purge paths.

## Requirements
- Define TTL and maximum size for logs, audit entries, and issue bundles.
- Default to the minimum necessary retention; avoid indefinite storage of PHI.
- Provide a user-visible "clear data" action that removes PHI, redaction maps, and cached artifacts.
- Purge transient caches on sign-out or when feature flags are disabled.

## PHI Handling
- Retain encrypted MPRs only as long as needed for local workflows.
- Avoid duplicating PHI across multiple stores; use a single source of truth.

## Verification
- Add tests to confirm TTL purge and size limits work under load.

---

# GDPR Compliance (Conditional)

Apply these rules if EU personal data is processed.

## Requirements
- Minimize collection; document lawful basis for processing.
- Support data access and deletion requests via a documented procedure.
- Keep data localized to the user device unless explicit consent and safeguards exist.
- Maintain retention limits and purge paths consistent with GDPR principles.

## Notes
- HIPAA requirements still apply to PHI; GDPR does not replace HIPAA obligations.



==== RULE FILE: .cursor/rules/11-security/hipaa-compliance.mdc ====
---
globs: apps/chrome-extensions/PMHNP-Administration-SummerySky/**,libs/summerysky/**,libs/Google-Chrome-Extension-Library/**
alwaysApply: false
description: "HIPAA-aligned PHI handling for SummerySky"
---
# HIPAA Compliance (SummerySky)

Protect PHI end-to-end: minimize collection, encrypt at rest, redact before any external AI, and keep logs PHI-free.

## Required Practices
- Treat all direct identifiers and clinical data as PHI; follow the HHS Safe Harbor list for redaction coverage.
- Store PHI only in encrypted local storage (IndexedDB + WebCrypto); never in plaintext `chrome.storage.*` or logs.
- Redact PHI before any AI interaction; only send de-identified payloads to external providers.
- When capturing UI HTML for AI guidance, strip text content and input values and remove URL query/path data; only share sanitized, redacted snapshots.
- Keep redaction maps encrypted and local-only; rehydration is performed locally just before export.
- Do not include PHI in issue reports, debug bundles, or telemetry; enforce redaction and PHI scanning checks.
- Use least-privilege scopes and explicit user consent for any external API access.
- Provide a user-visible "data purge" control to clear local PHI and redaction maps.

## PHI Redaction Standards
- Redact names, DOB, MRN, phone, email, address, portal identifiers, and any free-text that can identify a patient.
- Use deterministic placeholders (e.g., `[[PATIENT_NAME_1]]`) scoped to a patient key.
- Verify redaction coverage with automated tests and runtime checks before AI prompt generation.

## Compliance Guardrails
- No PHI in console logs or error messages; use hashed/internal ids.
- Audit trail entries must be non-PHI and include correlation ids for traceability.
- Retention policies must include TTL and size limits for logs, bundles, and transient caches.

## Verification
- Add PHI-leak tests for logs, prompts, issue bundles, and exported artifacts.
- Document any exception explicitly in an ADR and get approval before shipping.



==== RULE FILE: .cursor/rules/11-security/security-and-secrets-policy.mdc ====
---
alwaysApply: true
description: "Security baseline for web automation projects"
---

# Security Baseline, Secrets & Dependency Policy

Eliminate inline secrets, validate runtime config, manage dependencies with SBOM/CVE gates, and follow security best practices for automation tools.

**Justification:** Protects credentials and supply chain, reduces attack surface, and ensures secure automation operations.

## Secrets Management

- Never commit secrets; use cloud KMS/SM via OIDC
- Rotate credentials regularly and log access
- Redact sensitive fields in all logs by default

```typescript
export function requireEnv(name: string): string {
  const v = process.env[name];
  if (!v) throw new Error(`Missing env: ${name}`);
  return v;
}
```

## Runtime Config Guard

Validate required env vars and feature flag shapes at boot; fail fast on invalid configuration.

```typescript
const schema = z.object({
  API_URL: z.string().url(),
  LOG_LEVEL: z.enum(["debug","info","warn","error"]),
  APP_HIPAA: z.enum(["true","false"]).default("false")
});
const cfg = schema.parse(process.env);
```

## Dependency Policy & SBOM

- Prefer lightweight, audited dependencies
- Block PRs that introduce critical CVEs
- Generate SBOM in CI for each release

```markdown
### PR Checklist
- [ ] No heavy runtime deps added, or rationale included
- [ ] SBOM updated; no critical CVEs
- [ ] Tree-shaking verified for new deps
```

## Automation Security Considerations

For web automation projects, additional security measures include:

- **Session isolation**: Keep automation sessions separate from user sessions
- **Credential masking**: Never expose real credentials in automation scripts or logs
- **Rate limiting**: Respect target system rate limits to avoid being flagged as abuse
- **Input validation**: Validate all inputs to prevent injection attacks
- **Audit trails**: Log automation actions for debugging and compliance

# Performance: Caching, Named Inputs & Bundling

Enable Nx local+remote cache, incremental typechecking, code-splitting, and lazy loading for heavy routes/assets.

```json
    "default": [
```

==== RULE FILE: .cursor/rules/13-ux/accessibility-standards.mdc ====
---
description: Apply to HTML templates, CSS stylesheets, and TypeScript/JavaScript files in Freshworks apps that handle UI rendering, styling, or user interactions
alwaysApply: false
---
# Accessibility Standards (WCAG 2.2 AA) - Freshworks Apps

Ensure semantic HTML, proper focus management, keyboard navigation, and color contrast checks for Freshworks CRM apps that run in iframes.

**Justification:** Inclusive experiences broaden reach and reduce legal risk for enterprise CRM users.

## Core Practices

### HTML & Semantics
- Use semantic HTML elements (`<button>`, `<input>`, `<label>`, `<table>`, `<nav>`)
- Associate form controls with `<label>` elements using `for` attribute
- Use proper heading hierarchy (`<h1>` through `<h6>`)
- Add descriptive `title` attributes for interactive elements

### Keyboard Navigation & Focus
- Ensure all interactive elements are keyboard accessible
- Implement visible focus indicators (avoid `outline: none`)
- Maintain logical tab order
- Handle focus management for dynamic content (modals, tabs, tooltips)

### Screen Reader Support
- Use ARIA labels/roles only when semantic HTML isn't sufficient
- Provide descriptive button text (avoid generic "Click here")
- Include `alt` text for images and icons
- Use `aria-live` regions for dynamic status updates

### Color & Contrast
- Maintain 4.5:1 contrast ratio for normal text, 3:1 for large text
- Don't rely solely on color to convey meaning
- Test with color blindness simulators

### Freshworks-Specific Considerations
- Apps run in iframes - ensure proper focus trapping when needed
- Integrate with Freshworks notification system for status messages
- Support keyboard shortcuts that don't conflict with Freshworks UI
- Consider mobile/tablet accessibility within CRM interface

### Testing & CI
```bash
pnpm a11y:check
```

### Common Patterns in This Project

#### Entity Menu Apps (Intake Guard)
- Use `role="tooltip"` for help badges
- Ensure notification messages are accessible
- Make status maps readable by screen readers

#### Dashboard Apps (Agent Dashboard)
- Use proper `<nav>` elements for tab interfaces
- Ensure table headers are properly associated with data cells
- Provide keyboard shortcuts for common actions
- Use `aria-expanded` for collapsible sections

# React Performance & State Management

Favor memoization, colocation, and context over prop drilling; measure interactions and avoid unnecessary re-renders.

**Justification:** Keeps UI responsive, especially on low-end hardware and long call sessions.

## Memoization & Derived Data

Memoize expensive computations and callbacks where it matters.

```typescript
const visible = useMemo(() => items.filter(f), [items, f]);
```

## State Management

- Co-locate state with consumers when possible
- Use context for global app-level state like auth and feature flags



==== RULE FILE: .cursor/rules/15-chome-extension/batch-operations.mdc ====
---
globs: apps/chrome-extensions/**/src/service_worker/**,libs/Google-Chrome-Extension-Library/automation/batch/**
alwaysApply: false
description: Batch operation patterns for concurrent recipe execution with pause/resume, retry, and state persistence
---

# Batch Operations Standards

Apply when implementing batch operations that process multiple URLs/tasks concurrently using the BatchRunner library or custom batch implementations.

**Justification:** Batch operations require careful concurrency control, state persistence, error handling, and MV3 lifecycle management. Standardized patterns ensure reliable, resumable batch execution.

## Architecture

Use `@chrome-ext/automation-batch` BatchRunner for all batch operations:

```
Sidepanel → Service Worker → BatchRunner → TaskExecutor → Recipe Execution
                ↓
         BatchStateStore (persistence)
                ↓
         TabSessionManager (concurrency)
```

## BatchRunner Integration

### Required Components

1. **BatchRunner**: Core scheduler (from library)
2. **TaskExecutor**: Executes individual tasks (implement or use `RecipeTaskExecutor`)
3. **RowSink**: Handles task results (use `StorageTableSink` for UI integration)
4. **BatchStateStore**: Persists state (from library, auto-configured)

### Basic Setup

```typescript
import { BatchRunner } from '@chrome-ext/automation-batch';
import { RecipeTaskExecutor } from '@chrome-ext/automation-batch';
import { StorageTableSink } from '@chrome-ext/automation-batch';

const batchRunner = new BatchRunner();

// Create executor
const executor = new RecipeTaskExecutor({
  appId: 'my-app',
  recipeId: 'my-recipe',
  dependencies: {
    createContentRuntime,
    executeRecipe,
    getRecipe,
  },
});

// Create sink
const sink = new StorageTableSink({
  appId: 'my-app',
  operationId: 'my-operation',
  broadcastEvents: true, // Enable UI updates
});
```

## Task Definition

### Task Structure

```typescript
import type { BatchTask } from '@chrome-ext/automation-batch';

const tasks: BatchTask[] = urls.map((url, index) => ({
  id: `task-${index}`, // Deterministic ID for resume
  urlRef: url, // URL string or secure storage key (PHI mode)
  input: { /* task-specific input */ },
  meta: { /* task metadata */ },
}));
```

### Task ID Requirements

- **Deterministic**: Same input → same ID (required for resume)
- **Unique**: No duplicates within a batch run
- **Stable**: Don't change IDs between runs for same data

```typescript
// ✅ Good - deterministic
id: `patient-${patientId}`

// ✅ Good - hash-based
id: `hash-${sha256(url)}`

// ❌ Bad - non-deterministic
id: `task-${Date.now()}-${Math.random()}`
```

## Batch Run Options

### Required Options

```typescript
const options: BatchRunOptions = {
  maxConcurrency: 2, // Worker pool size
  closeTabsOnComplete: true, // Clean up tabs after task
  focusTabs: false, // Don't focus tabs (better UX)
  phiMode: false, // Enable PHI-safe storage if needed
  timeouts: {
    tabLoadMs: 30000,
    contentReadyMs: 5000,
    taskExecutionMs: 300000, // 5 minutes per task
  },
  retryPolicy: {
    maxAttempts: 3,
    initialDelayMs: 1000,
    maxDelayMs: 30000,
    backoffMultiplier: 2,
    jitter: true,
  },
};
```

### Concurrency Guidelines

- **Default**: 2-3 concurrent tasks (balance speed vs resource usage)
- **High-volume**: 3-5 concurrent tasks (if system can handle)
- **PHI-sensitive**: 1-2 concurrent tasks (reduce risk)
- **Rate-limited APIs**: 1 concurrent task (respect limits)

## Service Worker Handlers

### Message Handlers

```typescript
chrome.runtime.onMessage.addListener((msg, sender, sendResponse) => {
  if (msg.type === 'START_BATCH') {
    batchRunner.start({
      runId: msg.runId,
      appId: 'my-app',
      operationId: 'my-operation',
      tasks: msg.tasks,
      executor,
      sink,
      options: msg.options,
      correlationId: msg.correlationId,
    }).then(() => {
      sendResponse({ ok: true });
    }).catch(error => {
      sendResponse({ ok: false, error: error.message });
    });
    return true; // Async response
  }

  if (msg.type === 'PAUSE_BATCH') {
    batchRunner.pause(msg.runId).then(() => {
      sendResponse({ ok: true });
    });
    return true;
  }

  if (msg.type === 'RESUME_BATCH') {
    batchRunner.resume(msg.runId).then(() => {
      sendResponse({ ok: true });
    });
    return true;
  }

  if (msg.type === 'ABORT_BATCH') {
    batchRunner.abort(msg.runId).then(() => {
      sendResponse({ ok: true });
    });
    return true;
  }

  if (msg.type === 'RESUME_DEFERRED_BATCH') {
    batchRunner.resumeDeferred(msg.runId).then(() => {
      sendResponse({ ok: true });
    });
    return true;
  }
});
```

## State Persistence

### Automatic Persistence

BatchRunner automatically persists:
- Run snapshots (status, metrics, options)
- Task states (status, attempts, errors)
- Deferred tasks (captcha/timeout)

### Resume After Suspension

```typescript
// On service worker wake
async function rehydrateBatches() {
  const allRuns = await batchRunner.getAllRuns('my-app', 'my-operation');
  
  for (const runId of allRuns) {
    const snapshot = await batchRunner.getRunSnapshot('my-app', 'my-operation', runId);
    
    if (snapshot.status === 'paused') {
      // Auto-resume paused runs
      await batchRunner.resume(runId);
    } else if (snapshot.status === 'running') {
      // Check if actually running (may have been suspended mid-run)
      // Optionally resume or mark as paused
      await batchRunner.pause(runId);
    }
  }
}
```

## Error Handling

### Error Classification

BatchRunner distinguishes:

- **Transient Errors** (`BatchTransientError`): Retryable (timeout, network, captcha)
- **Permanent Errors** (`BatchPermanentError`): Don't retry (invalid input, auth failure)

### Custom Executor Error Handling

```typescript
class MyTaskExecutor implements TaskExecutor {
  async execute({ task, signal, emitProgress }) {
    try {
      // Execute task
      return result;
    } catch (error) {
      const errorMessage = error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error);
      
      // Classify error
      if (this.isTransientError(errorMessage)) {
        throw new BatchTransientError(errorMessage, error);
      } else {
        throw new BatchPermanentError(errorMessage, error);
      }
    }
  }

  private isTransientError(message: string): boolean {
    const transientPatterns = [
      'timeout',
      'network',
      'captcha',
      'rate limit',
      'temporary',
    ];
    return transientPatterns.some(pattern => 
      message.toLowerCase().includes(pattern)
    );
  }
}
```

### Retry Policy

```typescript
const retryPolicy: RetryPolicy = {
  maxAttempts: 3, // Total attempts (initial + 2 retries)
  initialDelayMs: 1000, // First retry delay
  maxDelayMs: 30000, // Cap on delay
  backoffMultiplier: 2, // Exponential backoff
  jitter: true, // Add randomness to prevent thundering herd
};
```

## Deferred Tasks

### Automatic Deferral

Tasks are automatically deferred on:
- Captcha detection (if `captchaPolicy` configured)
- Timeout (if task exceeds `taskExecutionMs`)
- Manual deferral (via executor)

### Resume Deferred

```typescript
// Resume all deferred tasks
await batchRunner.resumeDeferred(runId);

// Or handle deferred tasks separately
const deferred = await batchRunner.getDeferredTasks(runId);
// Process deferred tasks with different strategy
```

## UI Integration

### Subscribe to Events

```typescript
useEffect(() => {
  const listener = (msg: any) => {
    if (msg.type === 'BATCH_ROW_UPDATE' && msg.runId === runId) {
      // Reload table state
      loadTableState();
    }
    
    if (msg.type === 'BATCH_RUN_STARTED') {
      // Update UI
      setRunStatus('running');
    }
    
    if (msg.type === 'BATCH_RUN_COMPLETE') {
      // Update UI
      setRunStatus('completed');
    }
  };

  chrome.runtime.onMessage.addListener(listener);
  return () => chrome.runtime.onMessage.removeListener(listener);
}, [runId]);
```

### Load Table State

```typescript
import { StorageTableSink } from '@chrome-ext/automation-batch';

const tableState = await StorageTableSink.getTableState(
  'my-app',
  'my-operation',
  runId
);

// tableState.rowsById contains all task rows
const rows = Object.values(tableState.rowsById);
```

## PHI-Safe Batch Operations

### Enable PHI Mode

```typescript
const options: BatchRunOptions = {
  phiMode: true,
  storage: {
    useSecureStorage: true,
  },
};

// Initialize secure storage
const stateStore = new BatchStateStore({
  useSecureStorage: true,
});
await stateStore.initSecureStorage(passphrase); // Optional
```

### PHI-Safe Guidelines

- **URLs**: Store only in secure storage (never in `chrome.storage.local`)
- **Logs**: Redact URLs using `maskUrl()` helper
- **Errors**: Don't include URLs in error messages
- **Task IDs**: Use non-PHI identifiers (patient IDs, not URLs)

## Logging

### Batch Operation Logging

```typescript
logger.info('Batch run started', {
  runId,
  appId,
  operationId,
  taskCount: tasks.length,
  maxConcurrency: options.maxConcurrency,
  correlationId,
});

logger.info('Task started', {
  runId,
  taskId: task.id,
  attempt: taskState.attempts + 1,
  correlationId,
});

logger.info('Task completed', {
  runId,
  taskId: task.id,
  status: 'succeeded',
  duration: Date.now() - startTime,
  correlationId,
});

logger.error('Task failed', error, {
  runId,
  taskId: task.id,
  attempts: taskState.attempts,
  willRetry: shouldRetry,
  correlationId,
});
```

## Best Practices

### Concurrency

- Start with low concurrency (2), increase based on performance
- Monitor tab count and memory usage
- Close tabs promptly (`closeTabsOnComplete: true`)

### State Management

- Don't store large payloads in task states
- Use result references for large data
- Implement cleanup policies (TTL for old runs)

### Error Recovery

- Classify errors correctly (transient vs permanent)
- Use appropriate retry policies
- Defer captcha/timeout tasks for manual handling

### Testing

- Test pause/resume scenarios
- Test service worker suspension/rehydration
- Test error classification and retry logic
- Test deferred task handling

## Checklist

When implementing batch operations:

- [ ] Use BatchRunner from `@chrome-ext/automation-batch`
- [ ] Implement TaskExecutor (or use RecipeTaskExecutor)
- [ ] Configure appropriate concurrency limits
- [ ] Set up retry policies
- [ ] Handle deferred tasks (captcha/timeout)
- [ ] Implement service worker message handlers
- [ ] Subscribe to batch events in UI
- [ ] Test pause/resume functionality
- [ ] Test service worker suspension recovery
- [ ] Enable PHI-safe mode if handling sensitive data
- [ ] Add comprehensive logging with correlation IDs

## Commands the AI should reuse
- Build: `pnpm nx run pmhnp-administration-summerysky:build` (Vite, multi-entry).
- Dev build: `pnpm nx run pmhnp-administration-summerysky:build:dev`.
- Watch/serve: `pnpm nx run pmhnp-administration-summerysky:serve`.
- Package ZIP: `pnpm nx run pmhnp-administration-summerysky:package`.
- Copy static assets during build (manifest, popup/panel/options HTML/CSS, debug scripts): **preserve** the `cp` chain in the build step; add to it if new files are introduced.

> Output location is `apps/chrome-extensions/PMHNP-Administration-SummerySky/dist` with `background.js`, `content.js`, `popup.js`, `manifest.json`, `popup.html`, `chunks/`. Keep this contract stable.



==== RULE FILE: .cursor/rules/15-chome-extension/checkpoints-and-resume.mdc ====
