---

# Checkpoints and Resume Standards

Apply when implementing checkpoint-based resumption for long-running recipe executions that may be interrupted by navigation, service worker suspension, or user actions.

**Justification:** Long-running extractions need checkpoint/resume capabilities to handle interruptions gracefully. Standardized patterns ensure reliable state persistence and resumption.

## Checkpoint System Overview

Checkpoints save execution state at key milestones, allowing recipes to resume from saved points after interruption.

### When to Use Checkpoints

- **Long-running recipes**: Extractions that take > 5 minutes
- **Pagination**: Multi-page scraping operations
- **User interruptions**: Allow users to pause and resume
- **Error recovery**: Resume after transient failures
- **State preservation**: Save progress before risky operations

## Checkpoint Storage

### Storage Location

```typescript
// Checkpoints stored in chrome.storage.local
const checkpointKey = `checkpoint:${checkpointId}`;

await chrome.storage.local.set({
  [checkpointKey]: {
    id: checkpointId,
    savedAt: new Date().toISOString(),
    tabId: context.tabId,
    baseFolder: context.baseDownloadFolder,
    variables: context.variables,
    credentials: context.credentials,
    metadata: { /* custom metadata */ },
    correlationId: context.correlationId,
  },
});
```

### Checkpoint Structure

```typescript
interface Checkpoint {
  id: string; // Unique checkpoint ID
  savedAt: string; // ISO timestamp
  tabId: number; // Tab ID (may be stale after resume)
  baseFolder: string; // Base download folder
  variables: Record<string, unknown>; // Recipe variables
  credentials?: { username: string; password: string }; // Optional credentials
  metadata?: Record<string, unknown>; // Custom metadata
  correlationId: string; // Correlation ID for tracing
}
```

## Recipe Step Types

### Save Checkpoint

```json
{
  "type": "saveCheckpoint",
  "id": "page-{{currentPage}}",
  "description": "Save checkpoint before processing page",
  "metadata": {
    "currentPage": "{{currentPage}}",
    "totalPages": "{{totalPages}}"
  }
}
```

### Restore Checkpoint

```json
{
  "type": "restoreCheckpoint",
  "id": "page-{{resumePage}}",
  "description": "Restore checkpoint to resume from page",
  "fallbackSteps": [
    {
      "type": "goto",
      "url": "{{baseUrl}}/page/{{resumePage}}"
    }
  ]
}
```

## Checkpoint Implementation

### Saving Checkpoints

```typescript
// In recipe executor
if (step.type === 'saveCheckpoint') {
  const checkpointId = substituteVariables(step.id, context.variables);
  
  const checkpoint: Checkpoint = {
    id: checkpointId,
    savedAt: new Date().toISOString(),
    tabId: context.tabId,
    baseFolder: context.baseDownloadFolder,
    variables: { ...context.variables },
    credentials: context.credentials,
    metadata: step.metadata 
      ? substituteVariables(step.metadata, context.variables)
      : {},
    correlationId: context.correlationId,
  };

  await chrome.storage.local.set({
    [`checkpoint:${checkpointId}`]: checkpoint,
  });

  logger.info('Checkpoint saved', {
    checkpointId,
    correlationId: context.correlationId,
    variables: Object.keys(checkpoint.variables),
  });
}
```

### Restoring Checkpoints

```typescript
// In service worker
if (msg.type === 'RESUME_RECIPE') {
  const { checkpointId, app, recipeId, tabId } = msg;
  
  // Load checkpoint
  const checkpointKey = `checkpoint:${checkpointId}`;
  const result = await chrome.storage.local.get(checkpointKey);
  const checkpoint = result[checkpointKey];
  
  if (!checkpoint) {
    throw new Error(`Checkpoint not found: ${checkpointId}`);
  }

  // Restore execution context
  const executionContext: ExecutionContext = {
    tabId, // Use provided tabId (may differ from checkpoint.tabId)
    credentials: checkpoint.credentials,
    correlationId: checkpoint.correlationId || generateCorrelationId(),
    variables: checkpoint.variables || {},
    baseDownloadFolder: checkpoint.baseFolder || getBaseDownloadFolder(),
  };

  // Get recipe
  const recipe = getRecipe(app, recipeId);
  
  // Create runtime
  const runtime = createContentRuntime(tabId, {
    baseDownloadFolder: executionContext.baseDownloadFolder,
    variables: executionContext.variables,
    recipeKey: `${app}:${recipeId}`,
  });

  // Execute recipe (will restore checkpoint if recipe has restoreCheckpoint step)
  const result = await executeRecipe(recipe, runtime, executionContext);
}
```

## Checkpoint Hooks

### Content Runtime Hooks

```typescript
import type { ContentRuntimeHooks } from '@chrome-ext/automation-recipes';

export function createCheckpointHooks(): ContentRuntimeHooks {
  return {
    onCheckpointSaved: async (
      checkpointId: string,
      checkpoint: Record<string, unknown>
    ) => {
      logger.info('Checkpoint hook triggered', {
        checkpointId,
        checkpointType: checkpointId.startsWith('job-') ? 'job' : 
                        checkpointId.startsWith('page-') ? 'page' : 'other',
        metadataKeys: Object.keys(checkpoint),
        correlationId: checkpoint.correlationId,
      });

      // Process checkpoint (e.g., convert to postings, update UI)
      // This runs after checkpoint is saved
    },
  };
}

// Use in runtime creation
const runtime = createContentRuntime(tabId, config, {
  onCheckpointSaved: createCheckpointHooks().onCheckpointSaved,
});
```

## Checkpoint Naming Conventions

### Recommended Patterns

- **Page checkpoints**: `page-{{pageNumber}}`
- **Item checkpoints**: `item-{{itemId}}` or `job-{{jobId}}`
- **Status checkpoints**: `status-{{statusName}}`
- **Milestone checkpoints**: `milestone-{{milestoneName}}`

### Deterministic IDs

Checkpoint IDs should be deterministic based on recipe variables:

```typescript
// ✅ Good - deterministic
id: `page-${currentPage}`

// ✅ Good - includes context
id: `patient-${patientId}-encounter-${encounterId}`

// ❌ Bad - non-deterministic
id: `checkpoint-${Date.now()}`
```

## UI Integration

### List Available Checkpoints

```typescript
async function listCheckpoints(app: string, recipeId: string): Promise<Checkpoint[]> {
  const allData = await chrome.storage.local.get(null);
  const checkpoints: Checkpoint[] = [];

  for (const [key, value] of Object.entries(allData)) {
    if (key.startsWith('checkpoint:')) {
      const checkpoint = value as Checkpoint;
      // Filter by app/recipe if needed
      checkpoints.push(checkpoint);
    }
  }

  return checkpoints.sort((a, b) => 
    new Date(b.savedAt).getTime() - new Date(a.savedAt).getTime()
  );
}
```

### Resume from UI

```typescript
async function resumeFromCheckpoint(
  checkpointId: string,
  app: string,
  recipeId: string
) {
  // Get current tab or create new one
  const tabs = await chrome.tabs.query({ active: true, currentWindow: true });
  const tabId = tabs[0]?.id;
  
  if (!tabId) {
    throw new Error('No active tab');
  }

  // Send resume message
  const response = await chrome.runtime.sendMessage({
    type: 'RESUME_RECIPE',
    checkpointId,
    app,
    recipeId,
    tabId,
  });

  if (!response.ok) {
    throw new Error(response.error);
  }
}
```

## Error Handling

### Checkpoint Not Found

```typescript
try {
  const checkpoint = await loadCheckpoint(checkpointId);
  // Resume...
} catch (error) {
  if (error.message.includes('not found')) {
    logger.error('Checkpoint not found', {
      checkpointId,
      correlationId,
    });
    
    // Execute fallback steps if provided
    if (fallbackSteps) {
      await executeSteps(fallbackSteps, runtime, context);
    } else {
      throw error;
    }
  } else {
    throw error;
  }
}
```

### Stale Checkpoints

```typescript
// Check if checkpoint is too old
const checkpointAge = Date.now() - new Date(checkpoint.savedAt).getTime();
const maxAge = 7 * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000; // 7 days

if (checkpointAge > maxAge) {
  logger.warn('Checkpoint is stale', {
    checkpointId,
    age: checkpointAge,
    maxAge,
  });
  
  // Optionally skip or warn user
}
```

## Best Practices

### When to Save Checkpoints

- **Before pagination**: Save before navigating to next page
- **After major milestones**: Save after completing significant work
- **Before risky operations**: Save before operations that might fail
- **Periodic saves**: Save every N items processed

### Checkpoint Granularity

- **Too fine**: Checkpoints every step (overhead, storage bloat)
- **Too coarse**: Checkpoints only at start/end (poor resume granularity)
- **Just right**: Checkpoints at natural breakpoints (page boundaries, item boundaries)

### Variable Management

- **Include all necessary variables**: Variables needed to resume
- **Don't include large data**: Store references, not full data
- **Validate variables**: Ensure variables are valid before saving

### Storage Management

- **Cleanup old checkpoints**: Implement TTL or manual cleanup
- **Limit checkpoint count**: Keep only N most recent checkpoints
- **Archive important checkpoints**: Move to separate storage if needed

## Logging

### Checkpoint Logging

```typescript
logger.info('Checkpoint saved', {
  checkpointId,
  checkpointType: checkpointId.startsWith('page-') ? 'page' : 'item',
  variables: Object.keys(checkpoint.variables),
  metadata: Object.keys(checkpoint.metadata || {}),
  correlationId: checkpoint.correlationId,
});

logger.info('Checkpoint restored', {
  checkpointId,
  restoredVariables: Object.keys(checkpoint.variables),
  tabId: context.tabId,
  correlationId: checkpoint.correlationId,
});

logger.info('Resume from checkpoint', {
  checkpointId,
  app,
  recipeId,
  tabId,
  correlationId,
});
```

## Checklist

When implementing checkpoints:

- [ ] Define checkpoint structure with required fields
- [ ] Implement `saveCheckpoint` step handler
- [ ] Implement `restoreCheckpoint` step handler
- [ ] Add checkpoint hooks for post-processing
- [ ] Use deterministic checkpoint IDs
- [ ] Include all necessary variables in checkpoints
- [ ] Handle checkpoint not found errors
- [ ] Implement UI for listing/resuming checkpoints
- [ ] Add logging for checkpoint operations
- [ ] Test resume after interruption
- [ ] Test resume after service worker suspension
- [ ] Implement checkpoint cleanup policy

# Chrome Extension App Structure (MV3 + Side Panel)

**Goal:** Standardize structure for MV3 service worker, content scripts, side panel UI, and shared automation recipes so state stays reliable across navigations.

> Build context: Vite multi-entry builds copy MV3 artifacts into `dist/` during `nx build`.

## Directory Layout (SummerySky example)

```text
apps/chrome-extensions/PMHNP-Administration-SummerySky/
├─ src/
│  ├─ service_worker/            # MV3 background orchestration + storage + messaging
```

==== RULE FILE: .cursor/rules/15-chome-extension/chrome-extension-e2e-testing.mdc ====
---
description: End-to-end testing standards for Chrome Extension UI and hooks with comprehensive logging
alwaysApply: false
---

# Chrome Extension E2E Testing Standards

Apply to all E2E tests for Chrome extensions, especially when integrating new features that involve UI components, React hooks, or messaging between sidepanel and service worker.

**Justification:** Chrome extensions have unique testing challenges (MV3 service workers, sidepanel UI, cross-context messaging). Standardized patterns ensure reliable, debuggable tests with comprehensive logging.

## Test Structure & Organization

### Directory Layout

```
src/tests/e2e/
├── specs/
│   ├── framework/          # Framework/infrastructure tests
│   │   ├── ui-components.spec.ts
│   │   ├── message-handlers.spec.ts
│   │   ├── service-worker.spec.ts
│   │   └── ...
│   ├── recipes/            # Recipe execution tests
│   │   └── recipe-execution.spec.ts
│   └── smoke.spec.ts       # Smoke tests
├── utils/
│   ├── console-logger.ts   # Logging utility
│   ├── framework-helpers.ts # Common test helpers
│   └── global-setup.ts     # Playwright global setup
└── playwright.config.ts     # Playwright configuration
```

### Test File Structure

Every E2E test file should follow this pattern:

```typescript
import { test, expect, chromium, BrowserContext } from '@playwright/test';
import path from 'path';
import { existsSync } from 'fs';
import { fileURLToPath } from 'url';
import { ConsoleLogger } from '../../utils/console-logger';
import {
  getExtensionId,
  waitForServiceWorkerReady,
  openSidepanel,
  waitForSidepanelReady,
  navigateToTab,
  sendMessageToServiceWorker,
} from '../../utils/framework-helpers';
import os from 'os';

const __filename = fileURLToPath(import.meta.url);
const __dirname = path.dirname(__filename);
const extensionPath = path.join(__dirname, '../../../../../dist');

const getUserProfilePath = () => {
  if ((global as any).__CHROME_USER_PROFILE__) {
    return (global as any).__CHROME_USER_PROFILE__;
  }
  if (process.env.CHROME_USER_PROFILE) {
    return process.env.CHROME_USER_PROFILE;
  }
  return path.join(
    os.homedir(),
    'Library/Application Support/Google/Chrome/Profile 2'
  );
};

const getOutputDir = (testInfo?: any) => {
  const outputDir = testInfo?.outputDir;
  if (outputDir !== null && outputDir !== undefined && outputDir !== '' && typeof outputDir === 'string') {
    return outputDir;
  }
  if ((global as any).__OUTPUT_DIR__) {
    return (global as any).__OUTPUT_DIR__;
  }
  const timestamp = new Date().toISOString().replace(/[:.]/g, '-').slice(0, -5);
  return path.join(__dirname, '../../../../test-results', timestamp);
};

test.describe('Test Suite Name', () => {
  let userDataDir: string;
  let sharedContext: BrowserContext;

  test.beforeAll(async () => {
    if (!existsSync(extensionPath)) {
      throw new Error(`Extension dist directory not found: ${extensionPath}`);
    }

    userDataDir = getUserProfilePath();

    // Create ONE shared context for all tests in the suite
    const headlessEnv = process.env.HEADLESS;
    sharedContext = await chromium.launchPersistentContext(userDataDir, {
      headless: headlessEnv === 'true', // or !== 'false' depending on default
      args: [
        `--disable-extensions-except=${extensionPath}`,
        `--load-extension=${extensionPath}`,
      ],
    });
  });

  test.afterAll(async () => {
    if (sharedContext) {
      await sharedContext.close();
    }
  });

  test('@framework test name', async ({}, testInfo) => {
    const testOutputDir = getOutputDir(testInfo);
    const logger = new ConsoleLogger('test-name', testOutputDir);

    try {
      await logger.captureContextLogs(sharedContext);
      await waitForServiceWorkerReady(sharedContext);

      // Test implementation here

    } finally {
      await logger.cleanup();
    }
  });
});
```

## Logging Requirements

### ConsoleLogger Usage

**Every test MUST use ConsoleLogger** to capture logs from all contexts:

```typescript
const logger = new ConsoleLogger('test-name', testOutputDir);

try {
  // Capture logs BEFORE any actions
  await logger.captureContextLogs(sharedContext);
  
  // Test actions...
  
} finally {
  // Always cleanup to write summary
  await logger.cleanup();
}
```

### What Gets Logged

ConsoleLogger captures:
- **Page console logs**: All `console.log()`, `console.error()`, etc. from regular pages
- **Extension page logs**: Console output from `chrome-extension://` pages
- **Service worker logs**: Via CDP (Chrome DevTools Protocol) for MV3 service workers
- **Page errors**: Uncaught exceptions and page errors
- **Runtime exceptions**: Service worker exceptions via CDP

### Log File Structure

Logs are written to:
```
test-results/
  <timestamp>/
    logs/
      <sanitized-test-name>.log
```

Each log entry includes:
- Timestamp (ISO format)
- Log level (INFO, WARN, ERROR, DEBUG)
- Source (page, extension, service-worker, console)
- Location (file:line:column when available)
- Message

## Browser Context Management

### Shared Context Pattern

Use a **single shared BrowserContext** per test suite (`beforeAll`/`afterAll`):

```typescript
let sharedContext: BrowserContext;

test.beforeAll(async () => {
  sharedContext = await chromium.launchPersistentContext(userDataDir, {
    headless: process.env.HEADLESS === 'true',
    args: [
      `--disable-extensions-except=${extensionPath}`,
      `--load-extension=${extensionPath}`,
    ],
  });
});

test.afterAll(async () => {
  await sharedContext.close();
});
```

**Why:** Chrome extensions persist state across page navigations. A shared context ensures:
- Extension state is preserved between tests
- Service worker stays alive
- Storage (chrome.storage.local/session) persists
- Faster test execution (no extension reload)

### Extension Loading

Always load the extension with:
- `--disable-extensions-except=${extensionPath}`: Disable other extensions
- `--load-extension=${extensionPath}`: Load the extension from dist directory

## Service Worker Testing

### Wait for Service Worker Ready

**Always wait for service worker initialization** before testing:

```typescript
await waitForServiceWorkerReady(sharedContext);
```

This helper:
1. Waits for service worker to be registered
2. Pings the service worker with `{ type: 'PING' }` message
3. Verifies response includes `{ ok: true, initialized: true }`
4. Times out after 10 seconds (configurable)

### Testing Service Worker Messages

Use `sendMessageToServiceWorker` helper:

```typescript
const response = await sendMessageToServiceWorker<{
  ok: boolean;
  recipes: any[];
}>(sidepanel, { type: 'LIST_RECIPES' });

expect(response.ok).toBe(true);
expect(Array.isArray(response.recipes)).toBe(true);
```

**Note:** The helper automatically adds correlation IDs for tracing.

## Sidepanel UI Testing

### Opening the Sidepanel

```typescript
const extensionId = await getExtensionId(sharedContext);
const sidepanel = await openSidepanel(sharedContext, extensionId);
await waitForSidepanelReady(sidepanel);
```

### Waiting for Sidepanel Ready

`waitForSidepanelReady` ensures:
- Root element (`#root`) is visible
- Navigation container (`.nav-container`) is rendered
- React components are initialized (1 second wait)

### Tab Navigation

```typescript
await navigateToTab(sidepanel, 'recipes');
const activeTab = await getActiveTab(sidepanel);
expect(activeTab.toLowerCase()).toBe('recipes');
```

### UI Component Testing Patterns

#### Testing Component Rendering

```typescript
test('@framework ComponentName should render', async ({}, testInfo) => {
  const logger = new ConsoleLogger('component-render', testOutputDir);
  
  try {
    await logger.captureContextLogs(sharedContext);
    await waitForServiceWorkerReady(sharedContext);
    
    const extensionId = await getExtensionId(sharedContext);
    const sidepanel = await openSidepanel(sharedContext, extensionId);
    await waitForSidepanelReady(sidepanel);
    
    await navigateToTab(sidepanel, 'tab-name');
    
    // Wait for component to load
    await sidepanel.waitForTimeout(2000);
    
    // Use resilient selectors
    const component = sidepanel.locator('text=Component Title, .component-class').first();
    await expect(component).toBeVisible({ timeout: 5000 });
    
    await sidepanel.close();
  } finally {
    await logger.cleanup();
  }
});
```

#### Testing User Interactions

```typescript
// Click button
const button = sidepanel.locator('button:has-text("Submit")');
await button.click();
await sidepanel.waitForTimeout(500); // Wait for state update

// Fill form inputs
const input = sidepanel.locator('input[type="text"]');
await input.fill('test value');
const value = await input.inputValue();
expect(value).toBe('test value');

// Select dropdown
const select = sidepanel.locator('select').first();
await select.selectOption('option-value');
```

#### Testing Loading States

```typescript
// Check for loading indicator (might be brief)
const loadingIndicator = sidepanel.locator('text=Loading, [aria-busy="true"]').first();
const hasLoading = await loadingIndicator.isVisible({ timeout: 1000 }).catch(() => false);

// After wait, content should be loaded
await waitForSidepanelReady(sidepanel);
const content = sidepanel.locator('#root');
await expect(content).toBeVisible();
```

## React Hooks Testing

### Testing Hooks Through UI

**Hooks are tested indirectly through UI behavior**, not directly:

```typescript
test('@framework useRecipeList hook should load recipes', async ({}, testInfo) => {
  const logger = new ConsoleLogger('recipe-list-hook', testOutputDir);
  
  try {
    await logger.captureContextLogs(sharedContext);
    await waitForServiceWorkerReady(sharedContext);
    
    const extensionId = await getExtensionId(sharedContext);
    const sidepanel = await openSidepanel(sharedContext, extensionId);
    await waitForSidepanelReady(sidepanel);
    
    await navigateToTab(sidepanel, 'recipes');
    
    // Wait for hook to fetch data
    await sidepanel.waitForTimeout(2000);
    
    // Verify hook result through UI
    const recipeList = sidepanel.locator('.recipe-item, [data-testid*="recipe"]');
    const count = await recipeList.count();
    expect(count).toBeGreaterThan(0);
    
    await sidepanel.close();
  } finally {
    await logger.cleanup();
  }
});
```

### Testing Hook State Changes

```typescript
// Trigger action that updates hook state
const button = sidepanel.locator('button:has-text("Refresh")');
await button.click();

// Wait for state update
await sidepanel.waitForTimeout(1000);

// Verify state change through UI
const updatedContent = sidepanel.locator('.updated-content');
await expect(updatedContent).toBeVisible();
```

### Testing Hook Error States

```typescript
// Simulate error condition (e.g., invalid message response)
// Verify error UI is displayed
const errorMessage = sidepanel.locator('text=Error, .error-message');
await expect(errorMessage).toBeVisible({ timeout: 5000 });

// Check logs for error details
const logs = logger.getLogs();
const errorLogs = logs.filter(l => l.level === 'error');
expect(errorLogs.length).toBeGreaterThan(0);
```

## Message Handler Testing

### Testing Sidepanel → Service Worker Messages

```typescript
test('@framework Sidepanel → Service Worker: MESSAGE_TYPE', async ({}, testInfo) => {
  const logger = new ConsoleLogger('message-type-integration', testOutputDir);

    await logger.captureContextLogs(sharedContext);

      data: any;
    }>(sidepanel, { type: 'MESSAGE_TYPE', /* params */ });

    expect(response.data).toBeDefined();

  } finally {
    await logger.cleanup();
  }
```

### Testing Error Responses

```typescript
// Trigger error condition
const response = await sendMessageToServiceWorker<{
  ok: boolean;
  error?: string;
}>(sidepanel, {
  type: 'GET_RECIPE',
  app: 'invalid-app',
  id: 'non-existent-id',

expect(response.ok).toBe(false);
expect(response.error).toBeDefined();
```

```typescript
const testCorrelationId = 'test-correlation-' + Date.now();

const response = await sendMessageToServiceWorker<{
```

## Playwright Configuration

### Required Configuration

```typescript
export default defineConfig({
  testDir: './specs',
  fullyParallel: false, // Disable parallel to avoid profile conflicts
  forbidOnly: !!process.env.CI,
  retries: process.env.CI ? 2 : 0,
  workers: 1, // Single worker to avoid profile locking issues
  outputDir: outputDir,
  reporter: [
    ['json', { outputFile: path.join(outputDir, 'test-results.json') }],
    ['./utils/custom-reporter.ts'],
    ['list'],
  ],
  use: {
    headless: process.env.HEADLESS === 'true', // Configurable
    trace: 'on-first-retry',
    screenshot: 'only-on-failure',
    video: 'retain-on-failure',
  },
  projects: [
    {
      name: 'framework',
      use: { ...devices['Desktop Chrome'] },
      grep: /@framework/,
    },
    {
      name: 'smoke',
      use: { ...devices['Desktop Chrome'] },
      grep: /@smoke/,
    },
    {
      name: 'recipes',
      use: { ...devices['Desktop Chrome'] },
      grep: /@recipes/,
    },
  ],
  webServer: {
    command: 'pnpm nx build <extension-name>',
    port: 0,
    reuseExistingServer: !process.env.CI,
    timeout: 120 * 1000,
  },
  globalSetup: './utils/global-setup.ts',
});
```

### Key Configuration Points

- **`workers: 1`**: Chrome profile locking prevents parallel execution
- **`fullyParallel: false`**: Shared browser context requires sequential tests
- **`webServer`**: Automatically builds extension before tests
- **`grep`**: Use test tags (`@framework`, `@smoke`, `@recipes`) to filter tests

## Test Tags

Use consistent test tags for filtering:

- **`@framework`**: Framework/infrastructure tests (UI components, message handlers, service worker)
- **`@smoke`**: Smoke tests (basic extension loading, critical paths)
- **`@recipes`**: Recipe execution tests (end-to-end recipe workflows)

## Selector Strategy

### Preferred Selectors (in order)

1. **Text-based**: `locator('text=Button Label')`
2. **Role-based**: `locator('button:has-text("Submit")')`
3. **Data attributes**: `locator('[data-testid="component"]')`
4. **Semantic HTML**: `locator('input[type="email"]')`
5. **CSS classes**: Only when above options unavailable (`.component-class`)

### Avoid

- Complex CSS selectors: `.container > div:nth-child(2) > button`
- XPath selectors
- IDs (unless stable and documented)

## Error Handling & Debugging

### Log Analysis

When tests fail, check log files:

```typescript
const logFilePath = logger.getLogFilePath();
console.log(`Logs written to: ${logFilePath}`);
```

Log files include:
- All console output from all contexts
- Error stack traces
- Summary with error/warning counts

### Common Issues

1. **Service worker not ready**: Increase timeout or check extension build
2. **Extension not loaded**: Verify `extensionPath` points to correct dist directory
3. **Profile locking**: Ensure `workers: 1` and `fullyParallel: false`
4. **Missing logs**: Ensure `logger.captureContextLogs()` is called before actions

## Checklist for New Feature Tests

When adding E2E tests for new features:

- [ ] Test file follows standard structure (imports, helpers, setup/teardown)
- [ ] ConsoleLogger is used with try/finally cleanup
- [ ] `waitForServiceWorkerReady()` is called before actions
- [ ] Sidepanel is opened with `openSidepanel()` and `waitForSidepanelReady()`
- [ ] Selectors use resilient patterns (text, role, data attributes)
- [ ] Test includes appropriate tag (`@framework`, `@smoke`, `@recipes`)
- [ ] Logs are captured for debugging
- [ ] Test handles loading states and async operations
- [ ] Error scenarios are tested (if applicable)
- [ ] Test is added to appropriate Playwright project in config

- **TypeScript strict** only; no `any`. Repo already standardized on TS + ESLint/Prettier + Vite.

==== RULE FILE: .cursor/rules/15-chome-extension/error-recovery.mdc ====
---

# Error Recovery Standards

Apply when implementing error handling, retry logic, and recovery mechanisms for recipe execution, batch operations, and content script interactions.

**Justification:** Robust error handling with appropriate retries and recovery ensures reliable automation even when facing transient failures, network issues, or unexpected conditions.

## Error Classification

### Error Types

Classify errors as **transient** (retryable) or **permanent** (non-retryable):

```typescript
class TransientError extends Error {
  constructor(message: string, cause?: Error) {
    super(message);
    this.name = 'TransientError';
    this.cause = cause;
  }
}

class PermanentError extends Error {
  constructor(message: string, cause?: Error) {
    super(message);
    this.name = 'PermanentError';
    this.cause = cause;
  }
}
```

### Transient Error Patterns

```typescript
function isTransientError(error: Error): boolean {
  const message = error.message.toLowerCase();
  const transientPatterns = [
    'timeout',
    'network',
    'connection',
    'econnrefused',
    'etimedout',
    'captcha',
    'rate limit',
    '429', // HTTP 429 Too Many Requests
    '503', // HTTP 503 Service Unavailable
    'temporary',
    'retry',
    'unavailable',
  ];
  
  return transientPatterns.some(pattern => message.includes(pattern));
}
```

### Permanent Error Patterns

```typescript
function isPermanentError(error: Error): boolean {
  const message = error.message.toLowerCase();
  const permanentPatterns = [
    'not found',
    '404', // HTTP 404 Not Found
    '401', // HTTP 401 Unauthorized
    '403', // HTTP 403 Forbidden
    'invalid',
    'malformed',
    'syntax error',
    'permission denied',
    'unauthorized',
  ];
  
  return permanentPatterns.some(pattern => message.includes(pattern));
}
```

## Retry Logic

### Exponential Backoff with Jitter

```typescript
interface RetryConfig {
  maxRetries: number; // Total attempts (initial + retries)
  baseDelayMs: number; // Initial delay
  maxDelayMs: number; // Maximum delay cap
  backoffMultiplier: number; // Exponential multiplier
  jitter: boolean; // Add randomness
}

function calculateBackoffDelay(
  attempt: number,
  config: RetryConfig
): number {
  const exponentialDelay = config.baseDelayMs * 
    Math.pow(config.backoffMultiplier, attempt);
  
  const cappedDelay = Math.min(exponentialDelay, config.maxDelayMs);
  
  if (config.jitter) {
    // Add ±20% jitter
    const jitterAmount = cappedDelay * 0.2;
    const jitter = (Math.random() * 2 - 1) * jitterAmount;
    return Math.max(0, cappedDelay + jitter);
  }
  
  return cappedDelay;
}
```

### Retry Implementation

```typescript
async function executeWithRetry<T>(
  operation: () => Promise<T>,
  config: RetryConfig,
  context: ExecutionContext
): Promise<T> {
  let lastError: Error | null = null;

  for (let attempt = 0; attempt <= config.maxRetries; attempt++) {
    try {
      // Check cancellation
      assertNotCancelled(context);
      
      // Execute operation
      return await operation();
    } catch (error) {
      lastError = error instanceof Error ? error : new Error(String(error));
      
      // Don't retry permanent errors
      if (isPermanentError(lastError)) {
        throw lastError;
      }
      
      // Don't retry if max attempts reached
      if (attempt >= config.maxRetries) {
        throw lastError;
      }
      
      // Calculate delay
      const delayMs = calculateBackoffDelay(attempt, config);
      
      logger.warn('Operation failed, retrying', {
        attempt: attempt + 1,
        maxRetries: config.maxRetries + 1,
        delayMs,
        error: lastError.message,
        correlationId: context.correlationId,
      });
      
      // Wait before retry
      await new Promise(resolve => setTimeout(resolve, delayMs));
    }
  }
  
  throw lastError || new Error('Operation failed after retries');
}
```

## Circuit Breaker Pattern

### Circuit Breaker Implementation

```typescript
class CircuitBreaker {
  private failureCount = 0;
  private lastFailureTime: number | null = null;
  private state: 'closed' | 'open' | 'half-open' = 'closed';
  
  constructor(
    private failureThreshold: number = 5,
    private timeoutMs: number = 60000, // 1 minute
    private successThreshold: number = 2
  ) {}

  recordError(errorMessage: string): boolean {
    this.failureCount++;
    this.lastFailureTime = Date.now();
    
    if (this.failureCount >= this.failureThreshold) {
      this.state = 'open';
      logger.warn('Circuit breaker opened', {
        failureCount: this.failureCount,
        errorMessage,
      });
      return true; // Should break
    }
    
    return false; // Continue
  }

  recordSuccess(): void {
    if (this.state === 'half-open') {
      // Count successes in half-open state
      this.failureCount--;
      if (this.failureCount <= 0) {
        this.state = 'closed';
        this.failureCount = 0;
        logger.info('Circuit breaker closed', {
          state: this.state,
        });
      }
    } else {
      // Reset on success in closed state
      this.failureCount = 0;
    }
  }

  shouldBreak(): boolean {
    if (this.state === 'open') {
      // Check if timeout expired
      if (this.lastFailureTime && 
          Date.now() - this.lastFailureTime > this.timeoutMs) {
        this.state = 'half-open';
        this.failureCount = this.successThreshold;
        logger.info('Circuit breaker half-open', {
          state: this.state,
        });
        return false; // Try again
      }
      return true; // Still open
    }
    
    return false; // Closed or half-open
  }

  getState(): string {
    return this.state;
  }
}
```

### Using Circuit Breaker

```typescript
const circuitBreaker = new CircuitBreaker(5, 60000, 2);

async function executeStepWithCircuitBreaker(
  step: RecipeStep,
  runtime: FlowRuntime,
  context: ExecutionContext
): Promise<unknown> {
  // Check circuit breaker
  if (circuitBreaker.shouldBreak()) {
    throw new Error('Circuit breaker is open - too many failures');
  }

  try {
    const result = await executeStep(step, runtime, context);
    circuitBreaker.recordSuccess();
    return result;
  } catch (error) {
    const errorMessage = error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error);
    const shouldBreak = circuitBreaker.recordError(errorMessage);
    
    if (shouldBreak) {
      throw new Error('Circuit breaker opened due to repeated failures');
    }
    
    throw error; // Re-throw for retry logic
  }
}
```

## Idempotent Operations

### Step Idempotency

All recipe steps should be idempotent (safe to retry):

```typescript
// ✅ Idempotent - checks state before acting
async function clickButton(selector: string): Promise<void> {
  const button = await waitForSelector(selector);
  const isVisible = await button.isVisible();
  
  if (!isVisible) {
    return; // Already clicked or not needed
  }
  
  await button.click();
}

// ✅ Idempotent - checks if already done
async function fillInput(selector: string, value: string): Promise<void> {
  const input = await waitForSelector(selector);
  const currentValue = await input.inputValue();
  
  if (currentValue === value) {
    return; // Already filled
  }
  
  await input.fill(value);
}

// ❌ Not idempotent - will fail on retry
async function clickButtonBad(selector: string): Promise<void> {
  const button = await waitForSelector(selector);
  await button.click(); // May fail if already clicked
}
```

## Error Recovery Strategies

### Retry with Different Strategy

```typescript
async function executeWithFallback<T>(
  primary: () => Promise<T>,
  fallback: () => Promise<T>,
  context: ExecutionContext
): Promise<T> {
  try {
    return await primary();
  } catch (error) {
    if (isTransientError(error as Error)) {
      logger.warn('Primary operation failed, trying fallback', {
        error: (error as Error).message,
        correlationId: context.correlationId,
      });
      
      try {
        return await fallback();
      } catch (fallbackError) {
        logger.error('Fallback operation also failed', fallbackError as Error, {
          correlationId: context.correlationId,
        });
        throw fallbackError;
      }
    }
    throw error;
  }
}
```

### Graceful Degradation

```typescript
async function executeWithGracefulDegradation<T>(
  operation: () => Promise<T>,
  fallbackValue: T,
  context: ExecutionContext
): Promise<T> {
  try {
    return await operation();
  } catch (error) {
    if (isTransientError(error as Error)) {
      logger.warn('Operation failed, using fallback value', {
        error: (error as Error).message,
        correlationId: context.correlationId,
      });
      return fallbackValue;
    }
    throw error;
  }
}
```

## Error Logging

### Structured Error Logging

```typescript
logger.error('Operation failed', error, {
  operation: 'executeStep',
  stepType: step.type,
  stepDescription: step.description,
  attempt: attempt + 1,
  maxRetries: config.maxRetries + 1,
  willRetry: attempt < config.maxRetries && isTransientError(error),
  errorType: isTransientError(error) ? 'transient' : 'permanent',
  correlationId: context.correlationId,
  tabId: context.tabId,
});
```

### Error Context

Always include context in error logs:

- Correlation ID
- Tab ID
- Step information
- Retry attempt number
- Error classification (transient/permanent)
- Will retry flag

## Best Practices

### Retry Configuration

- **Max retries**: 3-5 attempts (balance reliability vs time)
- **Base delay**: 1-2 seconds (not too aggressive)
- **Max delay**: 30-60 seconds (cap exponential growth)
- **Jitter**: Always enable (prevent thundering herd)

### Error Classification

- **Be conservative**: When unsure, classify as transient
- **Update patterns**: Add new patterns as you encounter errors
- **Log classifications**: Log why errors are classified as transient/permanent

### Circuit Breaker

- **Failure threshold**: 5-10 failures
- **Timeout**: 60 seconds (1 minute)
- **Success threshold**: 2-3 successes to close

### Idempotency

- **Check state first**: Verify current state before acting
- **Safe to retry**: All steps should be safe to retry
- **No side effects**: Avoid operations with irreversible side effects

## Checklist

When implementing error recovery:

- [ ] Classify errors as transient or permanent
- [ ] Implement exponential backoff with jitter
- [ ] Add circuit breaker for repeated failures
- [ ] Ensure all steps are idempotent
- [ ] Add retry configuration to recipe options
- [ ] Log errors with full context
- [ ] Test retry logic with simulated failures
- [ ] Test circuit breaker behavior
- [ ] Handle cancellation signals during retries
- [ ] Implement graceful degradation where appropriate

# Recipe Spec & Execution

Use `@chrome-ext/automation-recipes` as the canonical schema for recipe steps and locators.

## Recipe Shape (conceptual)

```typescript
type Locator = { by: 'css' | 'role'; selector?: string; role?: string; name?: string };
type StepType =
  | 'goto' | 'waitFor' | 'assertVisible' | 'fill' | 'type' | 'click'
  | 'extract' | 'loop' | 'captureHtml' | 'saveUrl' | 'setVariable'
  | 'saveCheckpoint' | 'restoreCheckpoint' | 'ensureFolder'
  | 'selectDropdown' | 'waitForPagination' | 'navigateBack'
  | 'validateFolder' | 'validateFile' | 'skipIf';
```

## Execution Rules

- Service worker owns orchestration; content scripts execute DOM actions.
- Prefer deterministic selectors (role + name) and include `description` on steps for better logs.
- Retry only idempotent waits; avoid re-clicking if a step already caused navigation.
- Store recipe overrides locally when selector repair is applied; export override JSON for repo updates.

## Packaging

- JSON recipes live in `apps/chrome-extensions/**/src/recipes/jsons/**`.
- Loader/registry registers promoted recipes and merges overrides at startup.



==== RULE FILE: .cursor/rules/15-chome-extension/logging-and-observability.mdc ====
---
globs: apps/chrome-extensions/**/src/**
alwaysApply: false
description: Logging, telemetry, and run status expectations for extension flows
---

# Logging and Observability Standards

Apply to all logging, telemetry, and observability implementations across service worker, content scripts, and sidepanel.

**Justification:** Comprehensive logging with correlation IDs, structured data, and proper log levels enables debugging, monitoring, and troubleshooting of complex automation workflows.

## Logger Usage

### Logger Wrapper

Use `@chrome-ext/core-telemetry` logger with levels: `debug`, `info`, `warn`, `error`.

```typescript
import { logger } from '@chrome-ext/core-telemetry';

logger.debug('Detailed debugging information', { /* context */ });
logger.info('Informational message', { /* context */ });
logger.warn('Warning message', { /* context */ });
logger.error('Error occurred', error, { /* context */ });
```

### App Tag Prefixing

Prefix automation logs with app tag for filtering:

```typescript
logger.info('[Bravado] Patient extraction started', {
  patientId,
  correlationId,
});

logger.info('[Indeed] Job scraping completed', {
  jobCount,
  correlationId,
});
```

## Structured Logging

### Required Context Fields

Always include these fields in log context:

```typescript
logger.info('Operation started', {
  operation: 'executeRecipe',
  app: 'bravado',
  recipeId: 'patients-export-v3',
  tabId: context.tabId,
  correlationId: context.correlationId,
  runId: runId, // If batch operation
  stepId: step.id, // If recipe step
});
```

### Log Levels

- **debug**: Detailed debugging information (development only)
- **info**: Normal operation events (start, progress, completion)
- **warn**: Warning conditions (retries, fallbacks, degraded performance)
- **error**: Error conditions (failures, exceptions)

## Run Log Storage

### Session Storage

Store last 1000 entries in `chrome.storage.session` keyed by `runId`:

```typescript
const RUN_LOG_KEY = `run_log:${runId}`;
const MAX_LOG_ENTRIES = 1000;

interface LogEntry {
  timestamp: string;
  level: 'debug' | 'info' | 'warn' | 'error';
  message: string;
  context?: Record<string, unknown>;
}

async function appendToRunLog(
  runId: string,
  entry: LogEntry
): Promise<void> {
  const key = `run_log:${runId}`;
  const result = await chrome.storage.session.get(key);
  const logs = (result[key] as LogEntry[]) || [];
  
  logs.push(entry);
  
  // Keep only last MAX_LOG_ENTRIES
  if (logs.length > MAX_LOG_ENTRIES) {
    logs.splice(0, logs.length - MAX_LOG_ENTRIES);
  }
  
  await chrome.storage.session.set({ [key]: logs });
}
```

### Step Execution

```typescript
logger.info('Step started', {
  stepType: step.type,
  stepDescription: step.description,

logger.info('Step completed', {

  stepId: step.id,
  duration: Date.now() - startTime,
  tabId: context.tabId,
  correlationId: context.correlationId,
```

```typescript
logger.info('Step progress', {
  stepType: step.type,
  stepId: step.id,
  progress: {
    current: currentIndex,
    total: totalItems,
    percentage: (currentIndex / totalItems) * 100,
  },
  correlationId: context.correlationId,
});
```

## Batch Operation Logging

### Batch Run Events

```typescript
logger.info('Batch run started', {
  runId,
  appId,
  operationId,
  taskCount: tasks.length,
  maxConcurrency: options.maxConcurrency,
  correlationId,
});

logger.info('Task started', {
  runId,
  taskId: task.id,
  attempt: taskState.attempts + 1,
  correlationId,
});

logger.info('Task completed', {
  runId,
  taskId: task.id,
  status: 'succeeded',
  duration: Date.now() - startTime,
  correlationId,
});

logger.error('Task failed', error, {
  runId,
  taskId: task.id,
  attempts: taskState.attempts,
  willRetry: shouldRetry,
  correlationId,

```

## Checkpoint Logging

### Checkpoint Operations

```typescript
logger.info('Checkpoint saved', {
  checkpointId,
  checkpointType: checkpointId.startsWith('page-') ? 'page' : 'item',
  variables: Object.keys(checkpoint.variables),
  metadata: Object.keys(checkpoint.metadata || {}),

logger.info('Checkpoint restored', {
  checkpointId,
  restoredVariables: Object.keys(checkpoint.variables),
  tabId: context.tabId,
  correlationId: checkpoint.correlationId,
```

## Error Logging

### Error Context

```typescript
logger.error('Operation failed', error, {
  operation: 'executeStep',
  stepType: step.type,
  stepDescription: step.description,
  attempt: attempt + 1,
  maxRetries: config.maxRetries + 1,
  willRetry: attempt < config.maxRetries && isTransientError(error),
  errorType: isTransientError(error) ? 'transient' : 'permanent',
  correlationId: context.correlationId,
  tabId: context.tabId,
  errorDetails: {
    name: error.name,
    message: error.message,
    stack: process.env.NODE_ENV === 'development' ? error.stack : undefined,
  },
});
```

## State Transition Logging

### State Changes

```typescript
logger.info('State transition', {
  from: previousState,
  to: newState,
  reason: 'user_action' | 'error' | 'completion' | 'timeout',
  correlationId,
  runId, // If batch operation
});
```

## Sidepanel Log Display

### Live Tail

Sidepanel should show live tail of run logs:

```typescript
function useRunLog(runId: string) {
  const [logs, setLogs] = useState<LogEntry[]>([]);

  useEffect(() => {
    // Load initial logs
    chrome.storage.session.get(`run_log:${runId}`).then(result => {
      setLogs(result[`run_log:${runId}`] || []);
    });

    // Subscribe to updates
    const listener = (changes: { [key: string]: chrome.storage.StorageChange }) => {
      if (changes[`run_log:${runId}`]) {
        setLogs(changes[`run_log:${runId}`].newValue || []);
      }
    };

    chrome.storage.onChanged.addListener(listener);
    return () => chrome.storage.onChanged.removeListener(listener);
  }, [runId]);

  return logs;
}
```

### Log Export

Export run log as JSON:

```typescript
async function exportRunLog(runId: string): Promise<string> {
  const result = await chrome.storage.session.get(`run_log:${runId}`);
  const logs = result[`run_log:${runId}`] || [];
  
  return JSON.stringify(logs, null, 2);
}
```

## PHI-Safe Logging

### Redaction

Never log PHI in plaintext:

```typescript
function redactUrl(url: string): string {
  // Mask URLs in PHI mode
  if (phiMode) {
    return '[REDACTED]';
  }
  return url;
}

logger.info('Navigation', {
  url: redactUrl(url),
  correlationId,
});
```

### Secure Logging

```typescript
// Store sensitive logs in secure storage
if (phiMode) {
  await secureStorage.append('secure_log', logEntry);
} else {
  await appendToRunLog(runId, logEntry);
}
```

## Performance Logging

### Duration Tracking

```typescript
const startTime = Date.now();

// ... operation ...

logger.info('Operation completed', {
  operation: 'executeRecipe',
  duration: Date.now() - startTime,
  correlationId,
});
```

### Metrics

```typescript
logger.info('Metrics', {
  operation: 'batchRun',
  metrics: {
    totalTasks: 100,
    completedTasks: 95,
    failedTasks: 3,
    deferredTasks: 2,
    averageTaskDuration: 5000,
    totalDuration: 500000,
  },
  correlationId,
});
```

## Best Practices

### Logging Guidelines

- **Be concise**: Include only necessary context
- **Use structured data**: Always use context objects, not string interpolation
- **Include correlation IDs**: Every log entry should have correlation ID
- **Log at appropriate levels**: Don't log debug info in production
- **Don't log PHI**: Redact sensitive data
- **Log state transitions**: Important state changes should be logged
- **Log errors with context**: Always include full error context

### Log Volume

- **Limit log entries**: Keep last 1000 entries per run
- **Rotate logs**: Clean up old logs periodically
- **Use log levels**: Filter by level to reduce noise

## Checklist

When implementing logging:

- [ ] Use `@chrome-ext/core-telemetry` logger
- [ ] Prefix logs with app tag
- [ ] Include correlation ID in all logs
- [ ] Include tabId, stepId, runId where applicable
- [ ] Store run logs in chrome.storage.session
- [ ] Implement live tail in sidepanel
- [ ] Implement log export functionality
- [ ] Redact PHI in logs
- [ ] Log state transitions
- [ ] Log errors with full context
- [ ] Track performance metrics
- [ ] Limit log volume (1000 entries per run)

==== RULE FILE: .cursor/rules/15-chome-extension/messaging-contracts.mdc ====
---
globs: apps/chrome-extensions/**/src/**
alwaysApply: false
description: Messaging contracts for MV3 service worker, content, and sidepanel
---
## Contracts (TypeScript types)
- `HelloMsg { kind:'HELLO', tabId:number, url:string, frameId:number }`
- `SyncMsg { kind:'SYNC', flow?: FlowSnapshot }`
- `StepRequest { kind:'STEP_REQUEST', step: StepSpec, runId:string }`
- `StepResult { kind:'STEP_RESULT', stepId:string, status:'ok'|'retry'|'fail', detail?:any }`
- `IntentMsg { kind:'INTENT', action:'StartFlow'|'Pause'|'Resume'|'Abort'|'ExportLog', payload?:any }`

## Ports vs onMessage
- **Long-lived**: use `chrome.tabs.connect(tabId, {name:'content' })` for content ↔ background; use `chrome.runtime.connect({name:'sidepanel'})` for side panel.
- **One-shots**: `chrome.runtime.sendMessage` for simple triggers from popup.

## Robustness
- On `onDisconnect`, background sets a **reconnect timer** and marks tab as **dehydrated**; content on reload resends `HELLO` to rebind the port.

> Your own README emphasizes “Web Automation Bridge” and typed message contracts—so we codify it here.



==== RULE FILE: .cursor/rules/15-chome-extension/mv3-lifecycle-and-state.mdc ====
---
globs: apps/chrome-extensions/**/src/service_worker/**,apps/chrome-extensions/**/src/**
alwaysApply: false
description: MV3 service worker lifecycle, rehydration, and state handling
---
## Goals
Make the AI handle **service worker suspension**, **tab reloads**, and **content-script rehydration** without losing in-flight flow state.

## Golden Rules
1) **Single source of truth in background**  
   - Keep runtime state (active flow, current step, timers) in **background**. Persist a minimal snapshot to `chrome.storage.session` on every step so worker suspension or tab navigations can be recovered.
   - On worker wake (`chrome.runtime.onStartup`, `onInstalled`, `onMessage`, `onConnect`), rehydrate snapshot and **probe** all live tabs to rebuild ports (see “Messaging Contracts” rule).

2) **Rehydrate after navigation**  
   - Content script on `document_start` immediately sends a **HELLO(port, tabId, url, frameId)**. Background responds with **SYNC(flowState)** if a flow is in progress for that tab.  
   - If background knows a step was “Navigate to first result → then wait for contact page”, it must **arm phased waits** again after `goto()`. (Your README already frames phased waits/pagination—apply the same robustness for SPA routes.)

3) **Never rely on `setInterval` keep-alives**  
   - MV3 workers sleep. Use **events**: `onMessage`, `onConnect`, `chrome.alarms` lightweight pings for long gaps, and **idempotent step execution** (steps re-run safely after resume).

4) **Flow step idempotency**  
   - Every step should accept re-entry. Example: a `waitFor(selector)` that already found the node simply resolves; a `click(selector)` ensures visibility/attached DOM before acting.

5) **Durable step log**  
   - Append each step result to a **ring buffer** in `chrome.storage.session` and mirror to a **per-run log** file only when the user requests export from the popup/panel (see “Logging & Observability”).

## Implementation nudges for Cursor
- Add `rehydrateContent(tabId)` in `service_worker/index.ts` that:  
  a) looks up `flowState[tabId]`,  
  b) posts `SYNC` to content,  
  c) re-arms any **post-navigation waits**.
- Add a `content/handshake.ts` (or `content.ts` handler) that sends `HELLO` on load and on `visibilitychange` when it returns to `visible`.
- Require **typed** messages: `HelloMsg`, `SyncMsg`, `StepRequest`, `StepResult`.

## Build/Test Anchors
- The extension is **MV3 + Vite multi-entry**; the build outputs `background.js`, `content.js`, `popup.js` to `apps/chrome-extensions/PMHNP-Administration-SummerySky/dist` and ships `manifest.json`/`popup.html`. Keep that artifact map stable.

description: Playwright/E2E expectations for Chrome extensions

## Use existing targets & tags
- Smoke: `pnpm nx run pmhnp-administration-summerysky:e2e -- --grep @smoke`
- Recipe slices: use `pnpm nx run pmhnp-administration-summerysky:e2e -- --grep "@recipes"` and extend existing tags.
- Side panel slice: `pnpm nx run pmhnp-administration-summerysky:e2e -- --grep @sidepanel`

## Authoring rules
- Tests must **load the unpacked extension** and **exercise the exact waits** used by content scripts (spy on logs if needed).
- Prefer **route-anchored** assertions over hard sleeps: `expect(page.url()).toMatch(/contacts\/\d+/)` **before** waiting for `[data-test-id="fieldSectionWrapperShowAll"]`.

---
- **No secrets in code**. If integration creds are needed, depend on the panel to prompt and store in `chrome.storage.session` only.
- Use **host_permissions** as narrowly as possible (prefer *specific* domains).

==== RULE FILE: .cursor/rules/15-chome-extension/selectors-and-waits.mdc ====
---
globs: apps/chrome-extensions/**/src/content/**,apps/chrome-extensions/**/src/recipes/**
alwaysApply: false
description: Selector resilience and phased waits for content scripts/recipes
---
## Purpose
Eliminate flaky waits like your failing step “Wait for contact page load (post-navigation phased)” after clicking the first search result.

## Rules
1) **Phased wait recipe** (content side)
   - Phase A: Wait for **URL or route token** change (e.g., `/contacts/` segment).  
   - Phase B: Wait for **layout anchor** (stable container) to attach.  
   - Phase C: Wait for **feature element** (e.g., `[data-test-id="fieldSectionWrapperShowAll"]`) to be **visible + enabled**.
   - Phase D: Extra: check for **SPA loader gone** (common `aria-busy=false`, or absence of busy indicator).

2) **If Phase C times out**
   - Confirm not in an iframe; if frames exist, query within the correct frame.
   - If SPA renders in waves, add a **MutationObserver** for up to 5s to catch lateness.
   - Allow **one route-refresh** fallback (history.replaceState → same URL) only once.

3) **Wait API**
   - Add `waitForRouteAndSelector({ routePattern: /contacts\/\d+/, selector: '[data-test-id="fieldSectionWrapperShowAll"]', timeoutMs: 30000 })`.

4) **Idempotent “Show All”**
   - Clicking the “Show All” button should check an “expanded” state (aria-expanded or a body class). If already expanded, **no-op**.

5) **Instrumentation**
   - Each phase logs `phase:start` → `phase:ok|timeout`, emits ms duration, stored in session log for panel playback.

> Keep this consistent with the “robust page load detection”/phased approach your README uses for pagination.



==== RULE FILE: .cursor/rules/15-chome-extension/sidepanel-application-model.mdc ====
---
globs: apps/chrome-extensions/**/src/sidepanel/**,apps/chrome-extensions/**/src/service_worker/**,apps/chrome-extensions/**/src/sidepanel.tsx
alwaysApply: false
description: Sidepanel state model and messaging expectations
---
## Goals
Treat the **Side Panel** as a **view** bound to background state; never as the source of truth.

## Rules
1) **Open/close orchestration in background**
   - Background owns `chrome.sidePanel.setOptions` and `open` flows; panel UI only requests actions.

2) **Panel <-> Background contract**
   - Establish a `chrome.runtime.connect({name:'sidepanel'})` port when panel mounts.
   - On connect, background pushes current `RunStatus` + steps log.
   - Panel sends **INTENT** messages (StartFlow, Pause, Resume, Abort, ExportLog).

3) **Panel survives reloads**
   - If panel is reopened or side panel toggled for the active tab, background re-sends last `RunStatus` + last 100 log entries from `chrome.storage.session`.

4) **E2E targets already exist**
   - Keep the `@sidepanel` E2E slice in Playwright; extend only in-place (don’t invent new configs):  
     `pnpm nx run pmhnp-administration-summerysky:e2e -- --grep @sidepanel`

5) **Static assets are copied at build**
   - Don’t forget to copy `panel.html` and panel styles to `dist`—your build pipeline already does this with a `cp` chain; preserve/extend it rather than replacing.



==== RULE FILE: .cursor/rules/15-chome-extension/summerysky-extension-standards.mdc ====
---
globs: apps/chrome-extensions/PMHNP-Administration-SummerySky/**,libs/summerysky/**,libs/Google-Chrome-Extension-Library/**
alwaysApply: false
description: "SummerySky extension standards (MV3, HIPAA, reusable modules)"
---
# SummerySky Extension Standards

Build the SummerySky MV3 extension as a local-first, HIPAA-aligned product with reusable libraries.

## Architecture
- Background service worker owns orchestration, storage, and cross-context messaging.
- Sidepanel UI is a view of background state; no business logic lives only in the UI.
- Content scripts are scoped to allowlisted domains and contain only DOM extraction or ChatGPT response capture.

## Reuse and Modularity
- App code is wiring only; implement features in libs (`libs/summerysky/*` or `libs/Google-Chrome-Extension-Library/*`).
- Portal-specific logic lives in data-access parser libs, not UI or background.
- Public APIs only through `index.ts`; avoid deep imports.

## Security and Compliance
- Never log or store PHI in plaintext; all PHI storage is encrypted.
- Only redacted data may be sent to AI providers.
- Use least-privilege OAuth scopes (drive.file + documents) and never persist access tokens; validate Drive folder IDs via API/picker before use.
- Use strict message schemas for background <-> UI <-> content communications.
- Task-assistant guidance must validate session tokens + origin locks, and require explicit user confirmation for click/type actions.

## Reliability
- Use correlation ids for all workflows (import, draft, export).
- Implement retry/backoff for external API calls.
- Feature flags gate AI drafts and Google export.



==== RULE FILE: .cursor/rules/15-web-automation/service-worker-dom-mocking.mdc ====
# Service Worker DOM API Mocking Standards

**Applies to:** All Chrome Extension service worker files (`service_worker/index.ts`)

## Purpose

Chrome Extension service workers (MV3) run in a restricted environment without DOM APIs. Bundlers like Vite may inject error handling code that attempts to access `window` and `document`, causing runtime errors. This rule defines comprehensive DOM API mocks to prevent these errors.

## Required Implementation

### 1. Mock Placement

**CRITICAL:** DOM API mocks MUST be placed at the very top of the service worker file, BEFORE any imports.

```typescript
// ✅ CORRECT: Mocks at top, before imports
if (typeof window === 'undefined') {
  (globalThis as any).window = { /* ... */ };
}
if (typeof document === 'undefined') {
  (globalThis as any).document = { /* ... */ };
}

import { bootstrapBackground } from '@chrome-ext/core-runtime'; // ✅ After mocks

// ❌ WRONG: Imports before mocks
import { bootstrapBackground } from '@chrome-ext/core-runtime';
if (typeof window === 'undefined') { /* ... */ } // ❌ Too late!
```

### 2. Window Mock Requirements

The `window` mock MUST include:

```typescript
if (typeof window === 'undefined') {
  (globalThis as any).window = {
    dispatchEvent: () => true,
    addEventListener: () => {},
    removeEventListener: () => {},
    postMessage: () => {},
    location: {
      href: '',
      origin: '',
      protocol: '',
      host: '',
      hostname: '',
      port: '',
      pathname: '',
      search: '',
      hash: '',
    },
  } as Window;
}
```

**Required Methods:**
- `dispatchEvent` - Used by Vite's error handler
- `addEventListener` / `removeEventListener` - Event handling
- `postMessage` - Cross-context messaging
- `location` - URL access (for parsers that check `window.location.href`)

### 3. Document Mock Requirements

The `document` mock MUST include:

```typescript
if (typeof document === 'undefined') {
  // Create a mock element with appendChild support
  const createMockElement = () => ({
    appendChild: () => ({} as Node),
    removeChild: () => ({} as Node),
    setAttribute: () => {},
    getAttribute: () => null,
    querySelector: () => null,
    querySelectorAll: () => [],
    textContent: '',
    innerHTML: '',
    style: {},
    id: '',
    className: '',
    href: '',
  } as HTMLElement);

  const mockBody = createMockElement();
  const mockHead = createMockElement();
  const mockDocumentElement = createMockElement();

  (globalThis as any).document = {
    getElementsByTagName: () => [],
    querySelector: () => null,
    querySelectorAll: () => [],
    createElement: () => createMockElement(),
    addEventListener: () => {},
    removeEventListener: () => {},
    body: mockBody,              // ✅ Required for appendChild
    head: mockHead,              // ✅ Required for script injection
    documentElement: mockDocumentElement, // ✅ Required for appendChild
  } as Document;
}
```

**Required Properties:**
- `body` - Used by Vite's error handler for `document.body.appendChild()`
- `head` - Used for script injection
- `documentElement` - Used as fallback append target
- `querySelector` / `querySelectorAll` - Used by parser code
- `createElement` - Must return element with `appendChild` method

**Required Methods:**
- `getElementsByTagName` - Used by Vite's error handler
- `querySelector` / `querySelectorAll` - Used by parser code
- `createElement` - Must return mock element with `appendChild`
- `addEventListener` / `removeEventListener` - Event handling

### 4. Mock Element Requirements

Elements created by `createElement` MUST support:

- `appendChild()` - Returns a mock Node
- `removeChild()` - Returns a mock Node
- `setAttribute()` / `getAttribute()` - Attribute manipulation
- `querySelector()` / `querySelectorAll()` - Element querying
- `textContent` / `innerHTML` - Content properties
- `style` - Style object
- `id` / `className` - Element identifiers
- `href` - For anchor elements

## Common Error Patterns & Fixes

### Error: "window is not defined"

**Cause:** Mock not placed before imports, or mock missing required methods.

**Fix:**
1. Move mock to top of file (before all imports)
2. Ensure `dispatchEvent`, `addEventListener`, `postMessage` are included

### Error: "document.querySelector is not a function"

**Cause:** `querySelector` method missing from document mock.

**Fix:** Add `querySelector: () => null` to document mock.

### Error: "Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'appendChild')"

**Cause:** `document.body` is undefined, or `createElement` doesn't return element with `appendChild`.

**Fix:**
1. Add `body: createMockElement()` to document mock
2. Ensure `createElement` returns element with `appendChild` method

### Error: "window.location.href is not a function"

**Cause:** `window.location` is undefined or not an object.

**Fix:** Add `location` object with `href` property to window mock.

### Error: "import() is disallowed on ServiceWorkerGlobalScope"

**Cause:** Using dynamic `import()` in service worker, which is not allowed by HTML spec.

**Fix:** Replace all `await import()` calls with static imports at the top of the file. Use helper functions to check module availability for optional modules.

## Verification Checklist

Before committing a service worker file, verify:

- [ ] Window mock is at the very top of the file (before imports)
- [ ] Document mock is at the very top of the file (before imports)
- [ ] Window mock includes: `dispatchEvent`, `addEventListener`, `removeEventListener`, `postMessage`, `location`
- [ ] Document mock includes: `body`, `head`, `documentElement`, `querySelector`, `querySelectorAll`, `createElement`
- [ ] `createElement` returns element with `appendChild` method
- [ ] All mock methods are no-ops (don't throw errors)
- [ ] Mock elements support common DOM operations
- [ ] No dynamic `import()` calls - all imports are static
- [ ] Optional modules use helper functions to check availability

## Testing

### Manual Test

1. Load extension in Chrome
2. Reload extension 5+ times
3. Check service worker console for errors
4. Verify no "window is not defined" or "document is not defined" errors

### Automated Test

```typescript
test('service worker has DOM mocks', async () => {
  const swCode = readFileSync('src/service_worker/index.ts', 'utf-8');
  
  // Verify mocks are before imports
  const mockIndex = swCode.indexOf('if (typeof window === \'undefined\')');
  const importIndex = swCode.indexOf('import');
  expect(mockIndex).toBeLessThan(importIndex);
  
  // Verify required methods exist
  expect(swCode).toContain('dispatchEvent');
  expect(swCode).toContain('querySelector');
  expect(swCode).toContain('appendChild');
  expect(swCode).toContain('document.body');
});
```

## Static Imports Only

**CRITICAL:** Dynamic `import()` is disallowed in service workers by the HTML specification. All imports MUST be static.

```typescript
// ✅ CORRECT: Static import at top of file
import * as zoomInfoModule from '@chrome-ext/integrations-zoominfo';

// ❌ WRONG: Dynamic import (disallowed)
const module = await import('@chrome-ext/integrations-zoominfo');
```

**For Optional Modules:**
Use a helper function to check module availability:

```typescript
// Static import at top
import * as zoomInfoModule from '@chrome-ext/integrations-zoominfo';

// Helper function
function getZoomInfoModule(): typeof zoomInfoModule | null {
  try {
    if (
      zoomInfoModule &&
      typeof zoomInfoModule.getZoomInfoCredentials === 'function'
    ) {
      return zoomInfoModule;
    }
    return null;
  } catch (error) {
    return null;
  }
}

// Usage
const module = getZoomInfoModule();
if (!module) {
  // Handle missing module gracefully
  return;
}
```

## Related Rules

- [browser-injection-standards](./browser-injection-standards.mdc) - Browser script injection
- [flow-standards](./flow-standards.mdc) - Automation flow definitions
- [web-automation-development](./web-automation-development.mdc) - Development standards

## Examples

### ✅ Good: Complete Mock Implementation

```typescript
// Top of service_worker/index.ts
if (typeof window === 'undefined') {
  (globalThis as any).window = {
    dispatchEvent: () => true,
    addEventListener: () => {},
    removeEventListener: () => {},
    postMessage: () => {},
    location: { href: '', origin: '', /* ... */ },
  } as Window;
}

if (typeof document === 'undefined') {
  const createMockElement = () => ({
    appendChild: () => ({} as Node),
    removeChild: () => ({} as Node),
    querySelector: () => null,
    querySelectorAll: () => [],
    // ... other methods
  } as HTMLElement);

  (globalThis as any).document = {
    body: createMockElement(),
    head: createMockElement(),
    documentElement: createMockElement(),
    querySelector: () => null,
    querySelectorAll: () => [],
    createElement: () => createMockElement(),
    // ... other methods
  } as Document;
}

// Now imports are safe
import { bootstrapBackground } from '@chrome-ext/core-runtime';
```

### ❌ Bad: Missing Required Methods

```typescript
// Missing appendChild support
if (typeof document === 'undefined') {
  (globalThis as any).document = {
    querySelector: () => null,
    // ❌ Missing body, head, documentElement
    // ❌ createElement doesn't return element with appendChild
  } as Document;
}
```

- **2025-01-XX:** Added appendChild mock requirements
- **2025-01-XX:** Added comprehensive element mock requirements



==== RULE FILE: .cursor/rules/16-web-automation/browser-injection-standards.mdc ====
---
description: Standards for browser script injection and content security in web automation
globs: ["apps/**", "libs/**", "tools/**"]
alwaysApply: true
---

# Browser Injection Standards

## Content Validation
Validate all content being injected into browser contexts for security and compatibility.

### Security Checks
- **No template literals**: Avoid template literals in injected scripts as they can introduce XSS vulnerabilities
- **No Unicode characters**: Use escaped sequences or avoid Unicode in injected JavaScript
- **ES module compatibility**: Ensure injected scripts work in browser environments
- **Input sanitization**: Validate and sanitize all dynamic content before injection

### Code Quality
- **Strict TypeScript**: Use TypeScript strict mode for all injection scripts
- **Error handling**: Include proper try-catch blocks and error reporting
- **Performance**: Minimize script size and execution time
- **Compatibility**: Test across target browsers (Chrome, Firefox, Safari)

## Injection Patterns
```typescript
// 👍 Good - static, validated content
const script = `
  (function() {
    const button = document.querySelector('[data-automation-id="submit"]');
    if (button) {
      button.addEventListener('click', function() {
        console.log('Button clicked');
      });
    }
  })();
`;

// 👎 Avoid - template literals with dynamic content
const script = `
  (function() {
    const selector = '${dynamicSelector}'; // XSS vulnerability
    document.querySelector(selector);
  })();
`;
```

## Validation Checklist
- [ ] No template literals in injected scripts
- [ ] No Unicode characters that could cause encoding issues
- [ ] Scripts tested in target browser environments
- [ ] Error handling included for runtime failures
- [ ] Content properly escaped and sanitized
- [ ] Scripts follow CSP (Content Security Policy) requirements


==== RULE FILE: .cursor/rules/16-web-automation/flow-standards.mdc ====
---
description: Standards for automation flow definitions and structure
globs: ["**/flows/*.json"]
alwaysApply: true
---

# Flow Standards

## Flow Structure
Automation flows should follow consistent, maintainable patterns for reliability and reusability.

### Required Fields
Every flow step must include:
- **id**: Unique identifier for the step
- **type**: Action type (click, type, wait, etc.)
- **locator**: Robust locator object (prefer data-attributes, roles, text)
- **description**: Human-readable description of the action

### Locator Standards
- **Prefer data attributes**: Use `data-automation-id`, `data-testid`, or similar
- **ARIA roles**: Use `role` and `aria-label` when available
- **Text-based**: Use visible text as fallback, but prefer explicit locators
- **Avoid CSS/XPath**: Only use as last resort when semantic locators unavailable

```json
{
  "steps": [
    {
      "id": "login-button-click",
      "type": "click",
      "locator": {
        "type": "data-attribute",
        "value": "data-automation-id=login-button"
      },
      "description": "Click the login button to proceed"
    },
    {
      "id": "username-input",
      "type": "type",
      "locator": {
        "type": "role",
        "value": "textbox",
        "name": "Username"
      },
      "value": "{{username}}",
      "description": "Enter username in the login form"
    }
  ]
}
```

## Flow Validation
- **Dependency checking**: Ensure no circular dependencies in flow prerequisites
- **Required parameters**: All referenced variables must be defined
- **Step sequencing**: Steps should be logically ordered and reversible where possible
- **Error handling**: Include recovery steps for common failure scenarios

## Best Practices
- **Modular flows**: Break complex flows into smaller, reusable components
- **Parameterization**: Use variables for environment-specific values
- **Documentation**: Include clear descriptions for maintenance
- **Testing**: Each flow should have corresponding test coverage
- **Versioning**: Track flow changes with semantic versioning

## Flow Checklist
- [ ] All steps have required id, type, locator, and description fields
- [ ] Locators use semantic attributes over CSS selectors
- [ ] No circular dependencies in prerequisites
- [ ] Parameters are properly defined and validated
- [ ] Flow includes error recovery steps where appropriate
- [ ] Flow is documented with usage examples
- [ ] Corresponding tests exist and pass


==== RULE FILE: .cursor/rules/16-web-automation/web-automation-development.mdc ====
---
description: Development standards for web automation projects
globs: ["apps/**", "libs/**", "tools/**"]
alwaysApply: true
---

# Web Automation Development Standards

## Code Organization
Maintain clean separation between automation logic, UI components, and business rules.

### Architecture Patterns
- **Single Responsibility**: Each module handles one automation concern
- **Composition over inheritance**: Build complex flows from simple, reusable components
- **Dependency injection**: Make modules testable by injecting dependencies

### UI Integration
- **Overlay registration**: Register UI elements in injected overlays with clear action mapping
- **Event routing**: Route user interactions through centralized handlers
- **State management**: Maintain automation state separate from application state

## Selector Strategy
Robust selectors are critical for reliable automation.

### Preferred Selectors (in order)
1. **Data attributes**: `data-automation-id`, `data-testid`, `data-cy`
2. **ARIA attributes**: `role`, `aria-label`, `aria-describedby`
3. **Semantic HTML**: `button[name="submit"]`, `input[type="email"]`
4. **Text content**: Only when above options unavailable
5. **CSS/XPath**: Last resort, avoid `nth-child()` and complex selectors

### Selector Examples
```typescript
// 👍 Preferred - semantic and robust
page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Save Changes' })
page.getByTestId('user-profile')
page.getByLabel('Email Address')

// 👎 Avoid - brittle and maintenance-heavy
page.locator('.btn-primary:nth-child(2)')
page.locator('#main > div > form > input[type="text"]')
```

## Error Handling & Recovery
Automation must gracefully handle failures and edge cases.

### Recovery Patterns
- **Retry logic**: Implement exponential backoff for transient failures
- **Fallback selectors**: Provide alternative locators for the same element
- **State validation**: Check expected state before and after actions
- **Logging**: Comprehensive logging for debugging and monitoring

### Error Types
```typescript
class AutomationError extends Error {
  constructor(
    message: string,
    public readonly code: string,
    public readonly recoverable: boolean = false
  ) {
    super(message);
    this.name = 'AutomationError';
  }
}

// Usage
throw new AutomationError(
  'Element not found after retries',
  'ELEMENT_NOT_FOUND',
  true // Can retry
);
```

## Testing Strategy
Comprehensive testing ensures automation reliability.

### Test Categories
- **Unit tests**: Individual functions and utilities
- **Integration tests**: Component interactions and API calls
- **E2E tests**: Complete user workflows
- **Visual regression**: UI consistency across browsers

### Test Organization
- **Page Objects**: Encapsulate page-specific logic and selectors
- **Fixtures**: Reusable test data and setup/teardown
- **Parallel execution**: Run tests concurrently for efficiency
- **Cross-browser**: Test compatibility across target browsers

## Performance Considerations
Optimize for speed and resource efficiency.

### Performance Best Practices
- **Lazy loading**: Load automation features only when needed
- **Debouncing**: Prevent excessive API calls and UI updates
- **Caching**: Cache expensive operations and DOM queries
- **Monitoring**: Track execution times and failure rates

### Resource Management
- **Memory leaks**: Clean up event listeners and DOM references
- **Connection pooling**: Reuse connections for external services
- **Timeout management**: Set appropriate timeouts for all async operations

## Security Standards
Protect sensitive data and maintain user privacy.

### Data Protection
- **No credential storage**: Never store real credentials in code or configs
- **Input sanitization**: Validate and sanitize all user inputs
- **Session isolation**: Keep automation sessions separate from user sessions
- **Audit logging**: Log automation actions for compliance

### Compliance
- **GDPR**: Handle personal data appropriately
- **CSP compliance**: Ensure injected scripts follow Content Security Policies
- **Rate limiting**: Respect API rate limits and implement backoff

## Development Workflow
Streamlined process for automation development.

### Version Control
- **Small commits**: Commit frequently with clear, descriptive messages
- **Branch strategy**: Feature branches with clear naming conventions
- **Code reviews**: Mandatory reviews for all automation changes

### Documentation
- **Flow documentation**: Document all automation flows with examples
- **API references**: Maintain up-to-date API documentation
- **Troubleshooting**: Include common issues and solutions
- **Changelog**: Track changes and breaking updates

## Quality Checklist
- [ ] Code follows TypeScript strict mode standards
- [ ] Selectors use semantic, robust patterns
- [ ] Error handling includes recovery mechanisms
- [ ] Tests cover happy path and error scenarios
- [ ] Performance optimized for target environments
- [ ] Security best practices implemented
- [ ] Documentation updated with changes
- [ ] Code reviewed by at least one other developer
