==== RULE FILE: .cursor/rules/15-chome-extension/chrome-extension-master-standards-v2.md ====
# Chrome Extension Engineering Standard (CEES)

Version: 2.0 (audit-based)

Purpose: a single, comprehensive ruleset for building Manifest V3 Chrome extensions to a very high standard (security, privacy, maintainability, performance, and Chrome Web Store readiness). Designed to be pasted into a GPT workspace / interview environment.

---

## 0) Non‑negotiables

### Security & privacy
- **Never ship secrets** in the extension package (API keys, usernames/passwords, private keys, tokens, test credentials, etc.).
- **Never expose sensitive files via `web_accessible_resources`**.
- **No PHI/PII in logs** (including debug logs) unless the user explicitly enables a “safe debug mode” that redacts by default.
- **No remote code execution**: do not load or execute remote JavaScript/WASM. All executable code must ship with the extension.
- **No `eval()` / `new Function()` / dynamic script strings**.

### Permissions
- Default to **minimum permissions**. Every permission must map to a user-visible feature.
- Prefer **optional permissions / optional host permissions** and request them **only at the moment a feature is enabled**.
- If a feature is not enabled, the extension must still function without crashing.

### MV3 reality
- The service worker **will be terminated frequently**. Everything must survive restarts.

### Chrome Web Store readiness
- The extension must have a **single clear purpose**.
- If user data is handled, an accurate **privacy policy** is required.
- Do not collect browsing activity unless it is strictly required for the declared purpose.

---

## 1) Platform constraints you must design around

### 1.1 MV3 service worker lifecycle constraints
- Treat the service worker as **ephemeral**:
  - No reliance on global state.
  - Persist all durable state.
  - Make every handler **idempotent**.
  - Every long-running workflow must be resumable.
- Handlers must be structured as:
  1) validate input + permissions
  2) load state (storage)
  3) perform bounded work
  4) persist state
  5) respond

### 1.2 Permission model constraints
- Script injection (`chrome.scripting`) requires **host access** (either `activeTab` or host permissions).
- Prefer a UX that requests host access **per site** and **only when needed**.
- Assume host access can be denied; every feature must have a graceful failure path.

### 1.3 Web accessible resources constraints
- `web_accessible_resources` is an allowlist of extension files that websites can fetch.
- Treat it as a **public surface area**: if it’s web accessible, assume hostile sites can read it.

### 1.4 Storage constraints
- Web Storage (`localStorage`) is not available in the service worker.
- `chrome.storage.*` is JSON-based and quota-limited; use IndexedDB for larger data.

---

## 2) Architecture standard

### 2.1 Mandatory component boundaries
- **UI surfaces** (side panel / popup / options) are “clients” and must not contain business logic.
- **Service worker** is the orchestrator, but must be thin:
  - route messages
  - enforce permissions
  - enforce security policy
  - call domain services
- **Domain services** (automation engine, storage, providers, telemetry) live in libraries and are unit-testable.
- **Content scripts** are “adapters”:
  - minimal DOM work
  - no secrets
  - no persistent state

### 2.2 Message-driven, versioned protocol
- All UI ↔ SW ↔ content-script interactions use a **single message protocol**.
- Every message uses this envelope:
  - `type`: string literal union
  - `payload`: validated at runtime
  - `correlationId`: stable identifier for request/response pairs
  - `schemaVersion`: incremented when breaking changes occur
- Reject unknown message types.

### 2.3 Trust boundaries & sender validation
- Treat all inbound message payloads as untrusted.
- For messages from content scripts, validate:
  - `sender.tab?.id` (expected tab)
  - `sender.url` (expected origin / match)
  - optional: handshake token established during injection

### 2.4 State & migrations
- Every stored object has:
  - a schema (TypeScript type + runtime validation)
  - a `schemaVersion`
  - deterministic migrations
- All migrations must be unit tested.

### 2.5 Configuration & feature flags
- Remote configuration is allowed only as **data**, never as executable code.
- Feature flags must be:
  - default-off for risky features
  - kill-switchable (disable without new release)

---

## 3) Security, privacy, and compliance

### 3.1 Data classification
Classify every field as one of:
- **Public**: safe anywhere.
- **Internal**: safe in extension storage, not in logs.
- **Sensitive**: credentials, tokens, personal identifiers.
- **Regulated**: PHI/health, financial, government IDs.

### 3.2 Storage rules
- **Credentials/tokens**
  - Prefer not storing.
  - If storing: encrypt-at-rest; keep in **session** by default; allow “remember me” only with explicit consent.
- **Regulated data**
  - Default to session-only.
  - If persistence is required: user must opt-in; encryption, retention policy, and secure-delete workflow required.

### 3.3 Cryptography standard (WebCrypto)
- Use `crypto.subtle` only (no custom crypto).
- **AES-GCM** for encryption with:
  - random 96-bit IV per encryption
  - authentication enforced (no silent decrypt failures)
- **KDF** for passphrase-derived keys:
  - PBKDF2 with a **random salt** stored alongside ciphertext
  - iteration count should be configurable and revisited periodically
- **Never** use deterministic salts based on public data (like extension IDs).
- Key management:
  - keys must be unlockable/rotatable
  - key material must not be written to disk unencrypted

### 3.4 Logging & telemetry
- Logs must be structured.
- Default logs include only:
  - event name
  - stable identifiers (non-sensitive)
  - timing
  - extension version
- Sensitive/regulated content must be:
  - removed, or
  - replaced with stable hashes, or
  - redacted.

### 3.5 Network & exfiltration controls
- All network access must be over HTTPS.
- Maintain an allowlist of domains your extension contacts.
- Never send sensitive/regulated data off-device without:
  - explicit user consent
  - documented retention
  - transport security

### 3.6 CSP rules
- Keep CSP strict.
- No `unsafe-eval`.
- No inline scripts.

### 3.7 `web_accessible_resources` rules
- Only include the minimum set of static assets required.
- Never expose:
  - credential files
  - private keys
  - debug dumps
  - any file that can be used to fingerprint users or leak data
- Restrict `matches` to only origins that need access.
- Consider `use_dynamic_url` for resources that don’t need stable URLs.

### 3.8 External connectivity
- `externally_connectable` is **off by default**.
- If enabled, restrict to your owned domains and validate message origins.

---

## 4) API usage rules (high-impact)

### 4.1 `chrome.action`
- If you define `action.default_popup`, do **not** rely on `chrome.action.onClicked`.
- Treat `chrome.action.openPopup()` as best-effort (requires user gesture).

### 4.2 `chrome.permissions`
- Use optional permissions/hosts and request at point-of-use.
- Always show a human explanation in the UI before prompting.
- Store per-feature permission state so you can explain why something is disabled.

### 4.3 `chrome.scripting`
- Prefer declarative content scripts when possible.
- If you must inject dynamically:
  - require user gesture
  - ensure host access exists
  - inject only bundled files (no code strings)
  - implement an “already injected?” handshake
  - avoid injecting repeatedly without idempotency guarantees

### 4.4 Messaging (`chrome.runtime.onMessage`, ports)
- All message handlers must:
  - validate schema
  - validate sender
  - return structured errors
- For async `sendResponse`, return `true` from the listener.
- Prefer long-lived ports (`chrome.runtime.connect`) for streaming progress.

### 4.5 `chrome.storage`
- Use the smallest appropriate store:
  - `session` for ephemeral
  - `local` for durable
  - `sync` only when explicitly needed
- Do not store secrets unencrypted.
- Implement write coalescing and avoid hot loops writing storage.

### 4.6 `chrome.alarms`
- Use alarms only for coarse scheduling.
- Always persist the job queue and recreate alarms on startup.
- Never assume alarms persist across restarts.

### 4.7 Side panel
- Side panel is the preferred “always-on” UI for complex flows.
- Use it to host any long-lived UI state (e.g., unlocked session state).

### 4.8 `chrome.identity`
- Prefer OAuth via `chrome.identity` for Google integrations.
- Minimize scopes.
- Never embed client secrets.

### 4.9 `chrome.cookies`
- Requires explicit justification; only use if essential.
- Keep host permissions narrow.

### 4.10 Network interception
- Prefer `declarativeNetRequest` over `webRequest` where possible.

---

## 5) Implementation process (surgical iteration)

### 5.1 Feature slice template
For each feature, produce these artifacts:
1. **One-sentence purpose** (user-visible).
2. **Permission delta**: changes in `permissions`, `host_permissions` / `optional_host_permissions`, `web_accessible_resources`.
3. **Data delta**: what data is collected/stored, retention, redaction.
4. **Threat model**: misuse cases + mitigations.
5. **Architecture delta**: modules touched, message types added.
6. **Test plan**: unit + integration + e2e.
7. **Rollback strategy**: how to disable feature without bricking.

### 5.2 Definition of Done
- Feature works after service worker restart.
- No new required permissions without a user-facing feature.
- No secrets.
- Tests added.
- Logs are PHI-safe.

---

## 6) PR checklist

- [ ] Manifest diff reviewed (permissions, hosts, `web_accessible_resources`).
- [ ] Message schemas updated and validated.
- [ ] Sender validation exists for new message paths.
- [ ] Storage writes have schemas + migrations.
- [ ] Long workflows checkpoint/resume.
- [ ] No secrets added.
- [ ] CSP unchanged or tightened.
- [ ] E2E test updated or added.

---

## 7) Release and operational readiness

### 7.1 Build & reproducibility
- Builds must be deterministic from lockfiles.
- Bundle analysis must exist for size regressions.

### 7.2 Key management (stable IDs)
- Never commit private keys.
- If stable unpacked-extension IDs are required, use a **public key** (manifest `key`) derived from a PEM keypair, and store it safely.
- Public keys may be committed if that aligns with your distribution model; private keys never.

### 7.3 Pre-submission checklist
- Verify:
  - permissions are minimal
  - privacy policy is accurate
  - data handling disclosures match reality
  - no PHI/PII in logs
  - no remote code

---

## 8) Audit checklists

### 8.1 Security audit checklist
- [ ] No secrets in repo or build output.
- [ ] No secrets in `web_accessible_resources`.
- [ ] Host permissions minimized; optional where possible.
- [ ] Sender validation on all inbound messages.
- [ ] Encryption-at-rest for sensitive data.

### 8.2 Performance audit checklist
- [ ] No tight polling loops.
- [ ] Storage writes coalesced.
- [ ] Content scripts minimal and page-safe.

### 8.3 Reliability audit checklist
- [ ] Works after SW restart.
- [ ] Jobs resume from checkpoints.
- [ ] Alarms recreated on startup.

---

## 9) GPT workspace “system prompt” (paste into your workspace)

You are a staff-level Chrome extension engineer working in a monorepo.

Rules:
- Follow CEES strictly.
- Before writing code, produce a plan with: permission delta, message schema delta, data delta, tests.
- Implement minimal diffs.
- Never introduce secrets.
- If any change touches permissions or `web_accessible_resources`, explicitly justify it and propose a safer alternative.

Commands:
- `/spec` Produce the feature slice template.
- `/design` Produce architecture + message schemas.
- `/impl` Implement minimal diff in TypeScript.
- `/test` Produce unit/integration/e2e test plan and code.
- `/review` Run PR checklist against the diff.



==== RULE FILE: .cursor/rules/15-chome-extension/RULES_AUDIT.md ====
# Chrome Extension Rules Audit Report

**Date**: 2025-01-15  
**Scope**: All Chrome extension rules and project analysis  
**Projects Analyzed**: 
- `apps/chrome-extensions/PMHNP-Administration-SummerySky`
- `apps/chrome-extensions/job-hunt-extension`

## Executive Summary

This audit identified **5 major gaps** in Chrome extension rules covering batch operations, checkpoints/resume, error recovery, state persistence, and comprehensive logging. All gaps have been addressed with new comprehensive rule files.

## Rules Audited

### Existing Rules (15 files)

1. ✅ `build-and-serve.mdc` - Build commands and dist output
2. ✅ `chrome-extension-app-structure.mdc` - MV3 app structure
3. ✅ `chrome-extension-e2e-testing.mdc` - E2E testing standards
4. ✅ `code-style-and-structure.mdc` - TypeScript conventions
5. ✅ `flows-spec-and-execution.mdc` - Recipe spec and execution
6. ⚠️ `logging-and-observability.mdc` - **ENHANCED** (was minimal, now comprehensive)
7. ✅ `messaging-contracts.mdc` - MV3 messaging contracts
8. ✅ `mv3-lifecycle-and-state.mdc` - Service worker lifecycle
9. ✅ `playwright-and-e2e.mdc` - Playwright expectations
10. ✅ `prompts-and-workflows.mdc` - Workflow expectations
11. ✅ `repo-conventions.mdc` - Repository conventions
12. ✅ `security-and-permissions.mdc` - Security standards
13. ✅ `selectors-and-waits.mdc` - Selector resilience
14. ✅ `sidepanel-application-model.mdc` - Sidepanel state model
15. ✅ `summerysky-extension-standards.mdc` - SummerySky-specific standards

## Gaps Identified

### 1. Batch Operations ❌ → ✅ FIXED

**Gap**: No rule covering batch execution patterns, concurrency control, pause/resume, deferred tasks, or state persistence for batch operations.

**Evidence Found**:
- `@chrome-ext/automation-batch` library extensively used in both projects
- BatchRunner implementation with worker pools, retry logic, deferred tasks
- Batch state persistence in `chrome.storage.local`
- PHI-safe batch operations with secure storage

**Impact**: High - Batch operations are critical for both projects but lacked standardized patterns.

**Resolution**: Created `batch-operations.mdc` covering:
- BatchRunner integration patterns
- Task definition and ID requirements
- Concurrency control guidelines
- Service worker handlers
- State persistence and resume
- Error handling and retry policies
- Deferred task handling
- UI integration patterns
- PHI-safe batch operations

### 2. Checkpoints and Resume ❌ → ✅ FIXED

**Gap**: No rule covering checkpoint-based resumption for long-running recipe executions.

**Evidence Found**:
- Checkpoint system implemented in both projects
- `saveCheckpoint` and `restoreCheckpoint` recipe step types
- Checkpoint hooks for post-processing (e.g., converting checkpoints to postings)
- UI integration for listing and resuming checkpoints
- Checkpoint storage in `chrome.storage.local`

**Impact**: High - Checkpoints are essential for long-running extractions but lacked documentation.

**Resolution**: Created `checkpoints-and-resume.mdc` covering:
- Checkpoint system overview
- Storage structure and location
- Recipe step types (saveCheckpoint, restoreCheckpoint)
- Checkpoint implementation patterns
- Checkpoint hooks
- Naming conventions
- UI integration
- Error handling
- Best practices

### 3. Error Recovery ❌ → ✅ FIXED

**Gap**: No comprehensive rule covering graceful error handling, retry logic, circuit breakers, or error classification.

**Evidence Found**:
- Retry logic with exponential backoff in recipe executor
- Circuit breaker pattern in batch operations
- Transient vs permanent error classification
- Error recovery strategies in both projects
- Idempotent step execution patterns

**Impact**: High - Error recovery is critical for reliable automation but lacked standardized patterns.

**Resolution**: Created `error-recovery.mdc` covering:
- Error classification (transient vs permanent)
- Exponential backoff with jitter
- Retry implementation patterns
- Circuit breaker pattern
- Idempotent operations
- Error recovery strategies
- Graceful degradation
- Error logging

### 4. State Persistence ⚠️ → ✅ ENHANCED

**Gap**: `mv3-lifecycle-and-state.mdc` covers basic state persistence but lacks details on:
- Batch state persistence patterns
- Checkpoint state management
- State rehydration after suspension
- State cleanup policies

**Evidence Found**:
- BatchStateStore for batch run persistence
- Checkpoint storage patterns
- State rehydration on service worker wake
- Stale state cleanup logic

**Impact**: Medium - Basic patterns exist but need enhancement.

**Resolution**: Enhanced existing rule and added cross-references to new rules.

### 5. Logging and Observability ⚠️ → ✅ ENHANCED

**Gap**: Original rule was minimal (13 lines). Missing:
- Structured logging patterns
- Batch operation logging
- Checkpoint logging
- Error logging with context
- State transition logging
- Performance logging
- PHI-safe logging

**Evidence Found**:
- Comprehensive logging throughout codebase
- Correlation ID usage
- Run log storage in `chrome.storage.session`
- Log export functionality
- PHI-safe logging patterns

**Impact**: High - Logging is critical for debugging but rule was too minimal.

**Resolution**: Enhanced `logging-and-observability.mdc` from 13 lines to comprehensive guide covering:
- Logger usage and levels
- Structured logging with required context
- Run log storage
- Recipe execution logging
- Batch operation logging
- Checkpoint logging
- Error logging
- State transition logging
- Sidepanel log display
- Log export
- PHI-safe logging
- Performance logging

## New Rules Created

### 1. `batch-operations.mdc` (NEW)

**Purpose**: Standardize batch operation patterns for concurrent recipe execution.

**Key Sections**:
- Architecture and BatchRunner integration
- Task definition and ID requirements
- Batch run options and concurrency guidelines
- Service worker handlers
- State persistence and resume
- Error handling and retry policies
- Deferred tasks
- UI integration

- Checkpoint hooks
- Naming conventions
- UI integration
- Error handling
- Best practices and checklist

**Purpose**: Standardize error handling, retry logic, and recovery patterns.

**Key Sections**:
- Error classification (transient vs permanent)
- Exponential backoff with jitter
- Retry implementation
- Circuit breaker pattern
- Idempotent operations
- Error recovery strategies
- Graceful degradation
- Error logging
- Best practices and checklist

  "targetDefaults": {
    "build": { "cache": true, "outputs": ["{workspaceRoot}/dist/{projectRoot}"] },
    "test":  { "cache": true },
    "lint":  { "cache": true },
    "e2e":   { "cache": true }
  }
}


(Use targetDefaults and declare outputs to maximize cache hits.)

{
  "compilerOptions": {
    "baseUrl": ".",

      "@mm/ads-data-access-core": ["libs/ads/data-access/core/src/index.ts"],
      "@mm/shared-ui-charts": ["libs/shared/ui/charts/src/index.ts"]
    }
  }

Caching & CI

==== RULE FILE: .cursor/rules/nx-sdlc/RULE.md ====
---
alwaysApply: true
description: Nx monorepo SDLC rules, tagging, and boundaries
---
Nx Monorepo SDLC Rules
Objectives

Keep architecture clean and evolvable with strict boundaries and clear library types.

Enforce repo-wide consistency via generators, tags, lint rules, and CI.

Prefer local-first iteration; validate via unit → integration → E2E gates.

Tag Taxonomy (repo-aligned)

Use the existing tag vocabulary in project.json. Do not introduce new tags unless you also update the module-boundary rules.

Observed tags in this workspace include:
- type: app, feature, ui, data-access, util, domain, core, api, automation, data
- scope: summerysky, chrome-ext, common, shared, platform, flows, zoominfo, indeed
- platform: web, node
- layer: domain, runtime, browser-api, ui, core, automation

Library Types & Naming

Keep types small and predictable. Prefer placing new libs under libs/<scope>/<type>/<name> when possible, even if legacy libs do not yet follow the pattern.

Project Tags

Every lib/app must specify tags in project.json. Tags drive lint rules and dependency constraints.

Module Boundaries (lint-enforced)

Use ESLint flat config with @nx/enforce-module-boundaries. Keep constraints aligned with the tag taxonomy above.

Baseline constraints to keep layers clean:
- type:app → may depend on any
- type:feature → may depend on ui, data-access, util, domain, core (same scope or scope:shared)
- type:ui → may depend on ui, util (same scope or scope:shared)
- type:data-access → may depend on util, domain (same scope or scope:shared)
- type:domain → may depend on util only
- type:util → may depend on util only

Ban deep imports (*/src/*).

eslint.config.mjs (drop-in)
// eslint.config.mjs
import nx from '@nx/eslint-plugin';

export default [
  ...nx.configs['flat/base'],
  ...nx.configs['flat/typescript'],
  ...nx.configs['flat/javascript'],
  {
    files: ['**/*.ts', '**/*.tsx', '**/*.js', '**/*.jsx'],
    rules: {
      '@nx/enforce-module-boundaries': [
        'error',
        {
          allow: [],
          depConstraints: [
            { sourceTag: 'type:feature', onlyDependOnLibsWithTags: ['type:feature','type:ui','type:data-access','type:util','type:domain','type:core','scope:shared'] },
            { sourceTag: 'type:ui',      onlyDependOnLibsWithTags: ['type:ui','type:util','scope:shared'] },
            { sourceTag: 'type:data-access', onlyDependOnLibsWithTags: ['type:data-access','type:util','type:domain','scope:shared'] },
            { sourceTag: 'type:domain',  onlyDependOnLibsWithTags: ['type:util'] },
            { sourceTag: 'type:util',    onlyDependOnLibsWithTags: ['type:util'] },
            // Keep scopes local or shared
            { sourceTag: 'scope:summerysky', onlyDependOnLibsWithTags: ['scope:summerysky','scope:shared'] },
            { sourceTag: 'scope:chrome-ext', onlyDependOnLibsWithTags: ['scope:chrome-ext','scope:shared'] },
            { sourceTag: 'scope:common', onlyDependOnLibsWithTags: ['scope:common','scope:shared'] },
          ],
          bannedExternalImports: ['**/src/**'],
        },
      ],
    },
  },
];

Generators & Creation Rules

Always generate via Nx generators (no hand-rolled libs). Use --directory, --importPath, and --tags.

Only mark a lib buildable/publishable when you truly need to build or publish it (requires --importPath).

Prefer as-provided path semantics for predictable folder roots in new workspaces.

Linking & Imports

Import only from the library’s root export (no deep paths).

Nx sets up TS path aliases; ensure importPath matches desired alias and avoid collisions with package names.

Task Pipelines & Defaults

Define repo-wide targetDefaults in nx.json for build, test, lint, e2e (including caching, inputs/outputs, and dependsOn).

Use nx affected in CI to scope work; enable remote cache (Nx Replay) to share results across dev/CI.

Caching & CI

Understand computation hashing; don’t break cache with ad-hoc scripts. Prefer Nx executors and declare outputs.

Remote caching cuts CI time dramatically; wire a provider (e.g., Nx Replay).

Create (React/JS example)
# feature lib (web)
nx g @nx/react:lib summerysky-feature-records \
  --directory=libs/summerysky/feature/records \
  --importPath=@ss/feature-records \
  --tags="scope:summerysky,type:feature,platform:web" \
  --unitTestRunner=vitest --linter=eslint

# data-access (node)
nx g @nx/js:lib summerysky-data-access-core \
  --directory=libs/summerysky/data-access/core \
  --importPath=@ss/data-access-core \
  --tags="scope:summerysky,type:data-access,platform:node" \
  --bundler=tsc --unitTestRunner=vitest --linter=eslint

# ui
nx g @nx/react:lib chrome-ext-ui-shell \
  --directory=libs/Google-Chrome-Extension-Library/ui/shell \
  --importPath=@chrome-ext/ui-shell \
  --tags="scope:chrome-ext,type:ui,platform:web" \
  --unitTestRunner=vitest

Create (publishable when needed)
nx g @nx/js:lib shared-util-dates \
  --directory=libs/shared/util/dates \
  --importPath=@shared/util-dates \
  --tags="scope:shared,type:util,platform:node" \
  --publishable --bundler=tsc --unitTestRunner=vitest

(Requires --importPath; use publishable/buildable only if distributing/isolated builds are needed.) 

project.json (tagging & targets)
{
  "$schema": "../../node_modules/nx/schemas/project-schema.json",
  "name": "summerysky-data-access-core",
  "sourceRoot": "libs/summerysky/data-access/core/src",
  "projectType": "library",
  "tags": ["scope:summerysky","type:data-access","platform:node"],
  "targets": {
    "build": { "executor": "@nx/js:tsc", "options": { "outputPath": "dist/libs/summerysky/data-access/core" } },
    "test": { "executor": "@nx/vite:test", "options": { "passWithNoTests": true } },
    "lint": { "executor": "@nx/eslint:lint" }
  }
}

Linking Between Libs

Import using the alias (set by --importPath), e.g.:

import { PatientRecordStore } from '@ss/data-access-core';

Ensure every lib has a proper src/index.ts public surface.

Avoid deep imports; ESLint rule bans them. (See boundaries rule.) 

TS Path & Scope Notes

Nx manages tsconfig.base.json paths for you; avoid manual edits unless necessary. If you change, keep alias ≠ package name to prevent resolution conflicts.

Prefer the latest generator behaviors (as-provided paths).

npmScope in nx.json is deprecated; rely on package names/importPath instead.



==== RULE FILE: .cursor/rules/surgical-edits-rule/RULE.md ====
---
globs: .cursor/rules/**
alwaysApply: false
description: Surgical edits guidance when modifying Cursor rule files
---

# Surgical Edits Rule - Chrome Extension Development

**Applies to:** All code in `.cursor/rules/` directory

## 🎯 Purpose
Enable precise, targeted modifications to Cursor rules that propagate consistently across the entire rules ecosystem without breaking existing functionality or introducing conflicts.

## 📋 Implementation Requirements

### Edit Types Supported
- **Configuration Updates**: Modify settings, paths, or parameters in rule files
- **Content Additions**: Add new sections, examples, or clarifications
- **Documentation Fixes**: Correct typos, improve clarity, update examples
- **Cross-Reference Updates**: Maintain consistency between related rules
- **Version Bumps**: Update version numbers and change logs

### Edit Constraints
- **NO Breaking Changes**: Never remove existing functionality without explicit approval
- **Preserve Structure**: Maintain existing file organization and section hierarchy
- **Update References**: Ensure all cross-references remain valid
- **Test Compatibility**: Verify edits don't break existing rule relationships

## 🔧 Surgical Edit Protocol

### Step 1: Impact Assessment
```markdown
Before making any edit:
- [ ] Identify all files that reference the target content
- [ ] Assess downstream effects on dependent rules
- [ ] Check for version dependencies
- [ ] Verify backward compatibility
```

### Step 2: Atomic Changes
```markdown
Each edit must be:
- [ ] Self-contained (no external dependencies)
- [ ] Reversible (can be undone without side effects)
- [ ] Documented (clear change description)
- [ ] Tested (verified against existing content)
```

### Step 3: Validation Checks
```markdown
After making changes:
- [ ] Cross-reference integrity maintained
- [ ] All linked examples still valid
- [ ] Rule hierarchy preserved
- [ ] No orphaned references created
```

## 🎨 Edit Categories

### Configuration Edits
```markdown
# ✅ ALLOWED: Update configuration values
OLD: timeout: 10000
NEW: timeout: 15000

# ✅ ALLOWED: Add new configuration options
OLD: timeout: 10000
NEW: timeout: 10000, retries: 3

# ❌ NOT ALLOWED: Remove configuration without replacement
OLD: timeout: 10000, retries: 3
NEW: timeout: 10000
```

### Content Edits
```markdown
# ✅ ALLOWED: Add clarifying information
OLD: Use this method for API calls.
NEW: Use this method for API calls. Recommended timeout: 10 seconds.

# ✅ ALLOWED: Fix documentation errors
OLD: Use `chrome.tabs.query()` for tab management
NEW: Use `chrome.tabs.query()` for tab information retrieval

# ❌ NOT ALLOWED: Change core functionality
OLD: Use `chrome.tabs.query()` for tab management
NEW: Use `browser.tabs.query()` for Firefox compatibility
```

### Structural Edits
```markdown
# ✅ ALLOWED: Add new sections
OLD: ## Implementation
NEW: ## Implementation
## Best Practices

# ✅ ALLOWED: Reorganize within sections
OLD: 1. Step one 2. Step two
NEW: 1. Step one 2. Step two 3. Step three

# ❌ NOT ALLOWED: Remove major sections
OLD: ## Implementation ## Examples
NEW: ## Implementation
```

## 🔍 Validation Commands

### Pre-Edit Checks
```bash
# Verify rule file integrity
find .cursor/rules -name "*.mdc" -exec echo "Checking: {}" \;

# Check for broken cross-references
grep -r "\]\(" .cursor/rules/ | grep -v "http" | wc -l
```

### Post-Edit Validation
```bash
# Verify all files still parse correctly
find .cursor/rules -name "*.mdc" -exec head -1 {} \; | grep -v "^#"

# Check for orphaned references
grep -r "](#" .cursor/rules/ | grep -v "\.mdc" | sort | uniq
```

## 🚨 Emergency Rollback

If a surgical edit causes issues:

1. **Immediate Revert**: Undo the change within 5 minutes
2. **Impact Assessment**: Document what broke and why
3. **Alternative Approach**: Implement fix using different method
4. **Prevention**: Add the problematic pattern to "NOT ALLOWED" list

## 📊 Success Metrics

- **Zero Breaking Changes**: No edits that break existing functionality
- **100% Reference Integrity**: All cross-references remain valid
- **< 5 min Validation**: Each edit validates in under 5 minutes
- **Clear Documentation**: Every edit has clear reasoning and impact

## 🔗 Related Rules

- [chrome-extension-app-structure](../15-chome-extension/chrome-extension-app-structure.mdc) - For structural changes
- [code-style-and-structure](../15-chome-extension/code-style-and-structure.mdc) - For code formatting consistency
- [messaging-contracts](../15-chome-extension/messaging-contracts.mdc) - For communication protocol updates

## 📝 Change Log

### Version 1.0.0
- Initial surgical edits protocol established
- Validation procedures defined
- Edit categories documented
- Emergency rollback process created

# Codex Continuity Files

This directory is the machine-readable continuity layer for Codex work in this workspace.

## Read Order

1. `.codex/projects/<project>/current-task.json`
2. `.codex/projects/<project>/SDLC_CHARTER.md`
3. Project-local handoff docs under the target app's `docs/` directory
4. Existing `.cursor/rules/**` documents referenced by the charter and handoff docs

## Rules

- Keep this data committed and deterministic.
- Update `current-task.json` whenever the active branch, phase, next commit, or verification commands change.
- Prefer referencing existing `.cursor` rules instead of duplicating policy text here.

# Archived workspace projects

These trees were moved from active `apps/`, `libs/`, and `tools/` to shrink the default Nx graph and local checkout size. **Folder layout under `archived/` mirrors the original paths** (e.g. `apps/bravado` → `archived/apps/bravado`).

## Contents

| Original path | Archived path |
|---------------|-----------------|
| `apps/automation-chrome-ext` | `archived/apps/automation-chrome-ext` |
| `apps/bravado` | `archived/apps/bravado` |
| `apps/bravado-extraction-cli` | `archived/apps/bravado-extraction-cli` |
| `apps/chrome-extensions/autom8` | `archived/apps/chrome-extensions/autom8` |
| `apps/chrome-extensions/Florida-Treasury-Scrapper` | `archived/apps/chrome-extensions/Florida-Treasury-Scrapper` |
| `apps/chrome-extensions/job-hunt-extension` | `archived/apps/chrome-extensions/job-hunt-extension` |
| `apps/chrome-extensions/PMHNP-Administration-SummerySky` | `archived/apps/chrome-extensions/PMHNP-Administration-SummerySky` |
| `apps/chrome-extensions/remote-Notary-Extension` | `archived/apps/chrome-extensions/remote-Notary-Extension` |
| `apps/chrome-extensions/test-results` | `archived/apps/chrome-extensions/test-results` |
| `apps/google-apps-script` | `archived/apps/google-apps-script` |
| `apps/kareo` | `archived/apps/kareo` |
| `apps/notaryresume-automation` | `archived/apps/notaryresume-automation` |
| `apps/opencall` | `archived/apps/opencall` |
| `apps/optimispt` | `archived/apps/optimispt` |

| `libs/summerysky` | `archived/libs/summerysky` |
| `tools/html-audit` | `archived/tools/html-audit` |
| `tools/html-capture` | `archived/tools/html-capture` |

## Nx / TypeScript notes

- **`.nxignore`** at the repo root lists `archived/**` so Nx does not register these as workspace projects.
- **`tsconfig.base.json`** maps `@ss/*` to `archived/libs/summerysky/*/src/index.ts` so support scripts under `support-pipelines/.../summerysky-support` that import `@ss/*` or relative `archived/libs/summerysky/...` paths keep resolving.
- **`project.json` files inside `archived/`** still reference old `apps/...` / `libs/summerysky/...` roots in some `cwd` or `sourceRoot` fields; fix those when you move a project back.

## Restore a project

1. Move the directory back (example): `git mv archived/apps/kareo apps/kareo`
2. If restoring `libs/summerysky`, also set `@ss/*` back to `libs/summerysky/*/src/index.ts` in `tsconfig.base.json` and update any `support-pipelines` imports that point at `archived/libs/summerysky`.
3. Remove or narrow the `archived/**` line in `.nxignore` if you want Nx to pick the restored tree up again (or only ignore paths that stay archived).
4. Restore any `package.json` scripts you need (see git history for removed `dev:*` / `serve:*` entries for kareo, bravado, opencall).

## Active apps (after archive)

Under `apps/`: `documentation-scraper`, `chrome-extensions/blue-notary-extension`, `chrome-extensions/proof-extension`.



==== RULE FILE: documentation-3rd-party/README.md ====
# Documentation Tools

This folder contains tools for managing third-party documentation.

## Generate Master Documentation Script

The `generate-master-documentation.cjs` script combines all `.md` files from a specified folder and its subfolders into a single master documentation file.

### Usage

```bash
node generate-master-documentation.cjs <folder-path>
```

### Features

- **Recursive Search**: Finds all `.md` files in the specified folder and subfolders
- **Duplicate Prevention**: Automatically deletes existing master files to prevent duplication
- **Organized Output**: Creates `master-{folder-name}-documentation.md` in the target folder
- **Structured Content**: Each file becomes a numbered section with file path reference
- **Error Handling**: Continues processing even if individual files have errors

### Example

```bash
# Generate master documentation for Chrome Extension API docs
node generate-master-documentation.cjs chrome-ext-api

# This creates: chrome-ext-api/master-chrome-ext-api-documentation.md
```

### Output Structure

The generated master file includes:
- Header with generation timestamp and file count
- Numbered sections for each `.md` file
- File path references for each section
- Original content from each file
- Section separators between files

### Testing

The script has been tested on the `chrome-ext-api` folder containing 70 `.md` files (1 root + 69 in subfolder), successfully generating a 30,000+ line master documentation file.








==== RULE FILE: docx-generation-export/README.md ====
# DOCX Generation Modules

Standalone extraction of the DOCX generation modules and supporting components from the patient-record-generation app. Generates Microsoft Word (.docx) documents from encounter JSON records with configurable styles, headers, and page layout.

## Contents

- **src/docx/** – Core DOCX generation
  - `docx-generator.ts` – Main generator (Document, sections, numbering, header)
  - `docx-styles.ts` – Style config (title, headings, body, field label/value, header table, page margins)
  - `docx-header.ts` – Header table (patient info, DOB, MRN, column widths, borders)
  - `index.ts` – Public API
- **src/types/encounter-types.ts** – TypeScript types (EncounterRecord, Block, DocxStyleConfig, EncounterHeader, etc.)
- **src/cli/generate-docx.ts** – CLI for single file, patient folder, or batch

## Install

```bash
cd docx-generation-export
npm install
# or: pnpm install
```

## Usage

### Programmatic

```ts
import { generateDocx, createDocxGenerator, DEFAULT_DOCX_STYLE_CONFIG } from './src/docx/index.js';
import type { EncounterRecord } from './src/types/encounter-types.js';

const encounter: EncounterRecord = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync('encounter.json', 'utf-8'));
const buffer = await generateDocx(encounter, { includeHeader: true });
fs.writeFileSync('output.docx', buffer);
```

### CLI

```bash
# Single file
npx tsx src/cli/generate-docx.ts --input=path/to/encounter.json

# Custom output
npx tsx src/cli/generate-docx.ts --input=encounter.json --output=out.docx

# All JSONs in a patient folder (expects patient folder to have encounters/ subdir)
npx tsx src/cli/generate-docx.ts --patient=patient-12345

# All patient folders (default base: output/Bravado-Unified-Patient-Folders)
npx tsx src/cli/generate-docx.ts --all

# Help
npx tsx src/cli/generate-docx.ts --help
```

## Encounter JSON shape

Input JSON must be an `IDEEncounterRecord` or `MMEEncounterRecord`: `schemaVersion`, `encounter`, `patient`, `provider`, `clinical`, `header` (with `table.rows`), and `blocks` (title, heading, paragraph, bullets). See `src/types/encounter-types.ts` for full definitions.

## Dependencies

- **docx** (^8.6.0) – Word document generation
- **tsx** (dev) – Run TypeScript CLI
- **typescript** (dev) – Build

## License

Same as the source workspace.



==== RULE FILE: libs/cursor-flow/README.md ====
# Cursor Flow - Web Automation Made Easy

A comprehensive web automation system for creating, editing, and managing automated workflows with AI assistance.

## 🚀 Features

- **🎯 Visual Flow Editor**: Create and edit automation flows with a user-friendly interface
- **🤖 AI-Powered**: Generate flows automatically using AI models
- **📦 Staging & Promotion**: Test flows in staging before promoting to production
- **🔧 Step Management**: Add, edit, move, and delete individual steps
- **🎨 Element Picker**: Visually select elements on web pages
- **📊 Observability**: Comprehensive logging and action tracking
- **🔄 Bridge Communication**: Seamless integration with backend platforms

## 📋 Architecture

The cursor-flow system consists of several key components:

### Core Components

1. **FlowEditorUI** (`src/ui/FlowEditorUI.ts`)
   - Main UI component for flow editing
   - Handles modal display and user interactions
   - Manages flow state and step editing

2. **FlowEditor** (`src/ui/flow/FlowEditor.ts`)
   - Core business logic for flow management
   - Implements the `FlowEditorApi` interface
   - Handles flow validation and step operations

3. **CursorCLIIntegration** (`src/cursorCLI.ts`)
   - Interface for AI model integration
   - Handles flow generation and optimization

4. **StagingWorkflowManager** (`src/stagingWorkflow.ts`)
   - Manages flow deployment lifecycle
   - Handles staging, testing, and promotion

### Interface Definition

The `FlowEditorApi` interface defines the contract for flow editing operations:

```typescript
interface FlowEditorApi {
  getSteps(): FlowStepDef[]
  setSteps(steps: FlowStepDef[]): void
  append(step: FlowStepDef): void
  loadFlow(flow: Flow): void
  getFlow(): Flow
  addStep(step: FlowStep): void
  moveStep(fromIndex: number, toIndex: number): void
  validateFlow(): void
  updateStepLocator(stepIndex: number, locator: any): void
  saveFlow(): void
  canRunFlow(): boolean
  updateContent(content: string): void
}
```

## 🎯 Usage

### Basic Flow Creation

1. **Open Editor**: Click the "Open Editor" button in the main menu
2. **Create Flow**: Click "Create New Flow" to start a new automation
3. **Add Steps**: Use the step editor to add individual actions
4. **Configure Elements**: Use the element picker to select page elements
5. **Save & Test**: Save your flow and test it in the staging environment
6. **Promote**: Move successful flows to production

### Step Types

The system supports various step types:

- **click**: Click on an element
- **type**: Type text into an input field
- **select**: Select an option from a dropdown
- **goto**: Navigate to a URL
- **waitFor**: Wait for an element or condition
- **assertVisible**: Assert that an element is visible
- **assertText**: Assert that text is present
- **iterate**: Repeat steps for multiple elements

### Element Locators

Elements can be located using various strategies:

- **CSS Selectors**: `.button`, `#element-id`
- **Text Content**: Elements containing specific text
- **ARIA Roles**: `button`, `textbox`, etc.
- **Labels**: Form elements with specific labels
- **Placeholders**: Input fields with placeholder text
- **Test IDs**: Elements with `data-testid` attributes

## 🔧 Bridge Events

The system uses bridge events for communication between the overlay and platform:

### Outgoing Events (from overlay to platform)

- `openEditor`: Open the flow editor modal
- `saveSteps`: Save flow steps to storage
- `runDraft`: Execute the current draft flow
- `promoteDraft`: Promote draft to production
- `testFlow`: Test flow in staging environment
- `generateFlow`: Generate flow using AI

### Incoming Events (from platform to overlay)

- `cursorflow:testResults`: Test execution results
- `cursorflow:aiSuggestions`: AI-generated step suggestions

## 🧪 Testing

### Running Tests

```bash
# Run all tests
npm test

# Run specific test files
npm test FlowEditorUI.spec.ts

# Run tests with coverage
npm test -- --coverage
```

### Test Structure

- **Unit Tests**: Test individual components in isolation
- **Integration Tests**: Test component interactions
- **E2E Tests**: Test complete user workflows

## 📊 Observability

The system provides comprehensive observability features:

### Action Tracking

Every action is tracked with timing information:

```
🔄 Action started: openEditor at 2025-09-24T00:18:26.727Z
✅ Action completed: openEditor in 150ms
```

### Bridge Communication Logging

All bridge events are logged for debugging:

```
[CursorFlow] 🔓 User clicked Open Editor button
[CursorFlow] Bridge function found, calling...
🎯 Handling bridge event: openEditor
🎯 Opening Cursor Flow Editor via bridge event
```

### Error Handling

Errors are caught and logged with context:

```
❌ Action failed: generateFlow after 3000ms
Error: AI service unavailable
```



## 📚 API Reference

### FlowStepDef Interface

```typescript
interface FlowStepDef {
  type: FlowStepType
  locator?: FlowStepLocator
  value?: string
  description?: string
  url?: string
  state?: string
  text?: string
  each?: FlowStepDef[]
}
```

```typescript
interface FlowStepLocator {
  by: 'css' | 'text' | 'role' | 'label' | 'placeholder' | 'testId'
  selector?: string
  text?: string
  role?: string
  id?: string
}
```

==== RULE FILE: libs/Google-Chrome-Extension-Library/README.md ====
# Chrome Extension Library

A composable, modular Chrome Extension library for MV3 with pluggable authentication providers and a fluid design system.

## Overview

This library provides a foundation for building Chrome Extensions with:

- **Modular architecture** - Pick and choose only the modules you need
- **Pluggable authentication** - Support for Google, Microsoft, Social, SSO, and Email/password
- **MV3 compliant** - Service worker, promises, and modern Chrome APIs
- **Fluid design system** - Responsive typography and spacing using `clamp()` and viewport units
- **Side panel support** - Optional MV3 side panel UI (Chrome 114+)
- **Type-safe** - Full TypeScript support with strict typing
- **Observable** - Structured logging, correlation IDs, and metrics

## Library Structure

```
libs/Google-Chrome-Extension-Library/
├── core/
│   ├── runtime/        # MV3 scaffolding, manifest builder, message bus
│   ├── storage/        # chrome.storage wrappers with namespacing
│   └── telemetry/      # Structured logging, correlation IDs, metrics
├── ui/
│   └── design-tokens/  # Fluid typography/spacing tokens
└── auth/
    ├── base/           # AuthProvider interface and registry
    ├── google/         # Google Identity API provider
    ├── microsoft/      # Azure AD provider
    ├── social/         # OAuth2 social providers
    ├── sso/            # OIDC/SAML providers
    └── email/          # Email/password provider
```

## Quick Start

### Installation

Import the modules you need:

```typescript
import { buildManifest, bootstrapBackground } from '@chrome-ext/core-runtime';
import { ChromeStorage } from '@chrome-ext/core-storage';
import { logger } from '@chrome-ext/core-telemetry';
import { googleProvider } from '@chrome-ext/auth-google';
import { microsoftProvider } from '@chrome-ext/auth-microsoft';
import { registerProviders } from '@chrome-ext/auth-base';
```

### Basic Usage

#### 1. Build Manifest

```typescript
import { buildManifest } from '@chrome-ext/core-runtime';

const manifest = buildManifest({
  name: 'My Extension',
  version: '1.0.0',
  permissions: ['storage', 'identity'],
  sidePanel: {
    defaultPath: 'sidepanel.html',
  },
  oauth2: {
    clientId: 'your-google-client-id',
    scopes: ['openid', 'profile', 'email'],
  },
});
```

#### 2. Setup Background Service Worker

```typescript
import { bootstrapBackground, setupSidePanel } from '@chrome-ext/core-runtime';

bootstrapBackground({
  onInstalled: async (details) => {
    console.log('Extension installed:', details.reason);
    await setupSidePanel({
      openPanelOnActionClick: true,
      defaultPath: 'sidepanel.html',
    });
  },
});
```

#### 3. Setup Authentication

```typescript
import { registerProviders } from '@chrome-ext/auth-base';
import { googleProvider } from '@chrome-ext/auth-google';
import { microsoftProvider } from '@chrome-ext/auth-microsoft';

const providers = registerProviders(
  googleProvider({
    clientId: 'your-google-client-id',
    scopes: ['openid', 'profile', 'email'],
  }),
  microsoftProvider({
    clientId: 'your-microsoft-client-id',
    scopes: ['openid', 'profile', 'email', 'User.Read'],
  }),
);

// Use providers
const googleAuth = providers.find(p => p.id === 'google');
if (googleAuth) {
  const result = await googleAuth.signIn();
  console.log('Signed in:', result.user);
}
```

#### 4. Use Storage

```typescript
import { ChromeStorage } from '@chrome-ext/core-storage';

const storage = new ChromeStorage('local', 'my-namespace');

await storage.set('key', { value: 'data' });
const value = await storage.get('key');
```

#### 5. Use Logging

```typescript
import { logger } from '@chrome-ext/core-telemetry';

logger.info('User signed in', { userId: '123' });
logger.error('Sign-in failed', error, { provider: 'google' });
```

## Design Tokens

Import the CSS file to use fluid design tokens:

```html
<link rel="stylesheet" href="path/to/tokens.css">
```

Tokens include:
- **Fluid typography** using `clamp()` (H1, H2, H3, body, subtext, caption)
- **Fluid spacing** (8-pt system with clamped values)
- **Safe-area support** for mobile devices
- **Container query units** for resizable panes
- **Touch targets** (minimum 44×44px)

## Authentication Providers

### Google

Uses MV3 Identity API (`chrome.identity.getAuthToken`):

```typescript
import { googleProvider } from '@chrome-ext/auth-google';

const provider = googleProvider({
  clientId: 'your-client-id',
  scopes: ['openid', 'profile', 'email'],
});

const result = await provider().signIn();
```

**Required manifest:**
```json
{
  "oauth2": {
    "client_id": "your-client-id",
    "scopes": ["openid", "profile", "email"]
  },
  "permissions": ["identity"]
}
```

### Microsoft

Uses `chrome.identity.launchWebAuthFlow` with PKCE:

```typescript
import { microsoftProvider } from '@chrome-ext/auth-microsoft';

const provider = microsoftProvider({
  clientId: 'your-client-id',
  tenant: 'common', // or your tenant ID
  scopes: ['openid', 'profile', 'email', 'User.Read'],
});

const result = await provider().signIn();
```

## Module Boundaries

Libraries follow Nx module boundaries:

- `core/*` - Runtime utilities (can be used by all)
- `ui/*` - UI components and tokens (can be used by features)
- `auth/*` - Authentication providers (can be used by features)

## Testing

Each library includes test configuration:

```bash
nx test chrome-ext-core-runtime
nx test chrome-ext-auth-google
```

## Contributing

When adding new providers or modules:

1. Follow the existing structure
2. Implement the `AuthProvider` interface for auth providers
3. Export through `src/index.ts`
4. Add proper TypeScript types
5. Include tests

- [API Reference](./docs/API_REFERENCE.md) - Complete API documentation
- [Operations Runbook](./docs/RUNBOOK.md) - Deployment and operations procedures

## Testing

Run tests for all libraries:


==== RULE FILE: libs/web-documentation/README.md ====
# Web Documentation Scraper

A TypeScript library for scraping documentation websites and converting them to clean Markdown format.

## Features

- **HTTP Scraping**: Robust web page fetching with retry logic and timeouts
- **HTML to Markdown**: Intelligent conversion preserving structure and formatting
- **File Output**: Batch writing of documentation files with customizable naming
- **CLI Tool**: Command-line interface for easy usage
- **TypeScript**: Full type safety and modern ES modules

## Installation

This library is part of the monorepo. To use it:

```bash
# Build the library
pnpm nx build web-documentation

# Run the CLI
pnpm nx run web-documentation:scrape -- <urls...>
```

## Usage

### CLI Usage

```bash
# Basic usage
pnpm nx run web-documentation:scrape -- https://docs.example.com/api

# Multiple URLs with custom output directory
pnpm nx run web-documentation:scrape -- \
  https://docs.example.com/api \
  https://docs.example.com/guide \
  --output-dir ./documentation

# With numbered filenames
pnpm nx run web-documentation:scrape -- \
  https://docs.example.com/api \
  --output-dir ./docs \
  --numbered

# Use browser automation for dynamic API documentation
pnpm nx run web-documentation:scrape -- \
  https://api.example.com/docs \
  --browser \
  --headed \
  --output-dir ./api-docs
```

### Programmatic Usage

```typescript
import { DocumentationScraper, MarkdownConverter, FileWriter } from '@mm/web-documentation';

// Initialize components
const scraper = new DocumentationScraper({
  userAgent: 'My-Scraper/1.0.0',
  timeout: 30000,
});

const converter = new MarkdownConverter({
  includeCodeExamples: true,
  includeApiReference: true,
  includeToc: true,
});

const writer = new FileWriter();

// Scrape, convert, and save
const urls = [
  'https://docs.zoominfo.com/reference/overview',
  'https://docs.zoominfo.com/reference/searchinterface_searchcontact',
];

const pages = await scraper.scrapePages(urls);
const docs = pages.map(page => converter.convert(page));
await writer.writeFiles(docs, './documentation-3rd-party/Zoom-Info-API');
```

## API Reference

### DocumentationScraper

```typescript
class DocumentationScraper {
  constructor(options?: ScraperOptions);

  scrapePage(url: string): Promise<DocumentationPage>;
  scrapePages(urls: string[]): Promise<DocumentationPage[]>;
}
```

### MarkdownConverter

```typescript
class MarkdownConverter {
  constructor(options?: ConverterOptions);

  convert(page: DocumentationPage): ScrapedDocumentation;
}
```

### FileWriter

```typescript
class FileWriter {
  writeFile(doc: ScrapedDocumentation, outputPath: string): Promise<void>;
  writeFiles(docs: ScrapedDocumentation[], outputDir: string, filenamePattern?: Function): Promise<void>;
}
```

## Configuration Options

### ScraperOptions

- `userAgent?: string` - Custom user agent string
- `timeout?: number` - Request timeout in milliseconds (default: 30000)
- `maxRetries?: number` - Maximum retry attempts (default: 3)
- `retryDelay?: number` - Base delay between retries in milliseconds (default: 1000)
- `useBrowser?: boolean` - Use browser automation for dynamic content (default: false)
- `browserWaitTime?: number` - Wait time for dynamic content in milliseconds (default: 5000)
- `headless?: boolean` - Run browser in headless mode (default: true)

### ConverterOptions

- `includeCodeExamples?: boolean` - Include code blocks in output (default: true)
- `includeApiReference?: boolean` - Include API reference sections (default: true)
- `includeToc?: boolean` - Generate table of contents (default: true)
- `frontmatter?: object` - Additional frontmatter metadata

## Output Format

Each scraped page is converted to Markdown with frontmatter:

```markdown
---
title: "Contacts"
url: "https://docs.zoominfo.com/reference/searchinterface_searchcontact"
scrapedAt: "2025-12-19T10:00:00.000Z"
lastModified: "2025-12-19T09:00:00.000Z"
---

# Contacts

## Overview

This endpoint allows you to search for contacts...
```

## ZoomInfo API Documentation

This library was specifically designed to scrape ZoomInfo API documentation. The following URLs are commonly scraped:

- `https://docs.zoominfo.com/reference/overview` - API Overview
- `https://docs.zoominfo.com/reference/searchinterface_searchcontact` - Contacts Search
- `https://docs.zoominfo.com/reference/enrichinterface_enrichcontact` - Contacts Enrichment
- `https://docs.zoominfo.com/reference/lookupinterface_lookup` - Data Lookup
- `https://docs.zoominfo.com/reference/userinterface_userusage` - Usage Data

### Example Command

```bash
pnpm nx run web-documentation:scrape -- \
  https://docs.zoominfo.com/reference/overview \
  https://docs.zoominfo.com/reference/searchinterface_searchcontact \
  https://docs.zoominfo.com/reference/enrichinterface_enrichcontact \
  https://docs.zoominfo.com/reference/lookupinterface_lookup \
  https://docs.zoominfo.com/reference/userinterface_userusage \
  --output-dir documentation-3rd-party/Zoom-Info-API \
  --numbered
```

This will create files like:
- `001-overview.md`
- `002-searchinterface_searchcontact.md`
- `003-enrichinterface_enrichcontact.md`
- `004-lookupinterface_lookup.md`
- `005-userinterface_userusage.md`

## Dependencies

- `jsdom` - HTML parsing and DOM manipulation
- `tsx` - TypeScript execution for CLI

## Development

```bash
# Build
pnpm nx build web-documentation

# Test
pnpm nx test web-documentation

# Lint
pnpm nx lint web-documentation

# Type check
pnpm nx typecheck web-documentation
```

## Error Handling

The scraper includes robust error handling:

- **Network failures**: Automatic retries with exponential backoff
- **Invalid HTML**: Graceful fallback to basic text extraction
- **Missing content**: Clear error messages for debugging
- **Rate limiting**: Respects delays between requests

## Contributing

1. Follow the monorepo's TypeScript and ESLint standards
2. Add tests for new functionality
3. Update documentation for API changes
4. Use conventional commits for changes


==== RULE FILE: README.md ====
## Cursor Flow (Select → Describe → Apply → Test → Promote)

Install Cursor CLI:

```bash
curl https://cursor.com/install -fsS | bash
```

Usage (default TARGET_APP=kareo):

```bash
nx run kareo:dev:watch
node tools/cursor/feature-bridge.cjs
```

In the app UI:
1. Click "🔎 Pick Element" and select a target element
2. Enter a short description
3. Click "⇢ Send to Cursor" to apply changes via agent
4. Watch logs stream in the overlay (SSE)
5. Click "🧪 Test" to run project tests
6. Click "⬆️ Promote" to create a local feature branch (no push)

Security: .env files are blocked by `.cursor/cli.json` permissions.





==== RULE FILE: scripts/google-apps/README.md ====
# Google Apps Script: IDE JSON to Google Docs Converter

This Google Apps Script converts AI-generated EncounterNoteDraft JSON files into formatted Google Docs and organizes them in Google Drive by patient status and name.

## Setup Instructions

### Step 1: Create a New Google Apps Script Project

1. Go to [https://script.google.com/](https://script.google.com/)
2. Click **"New Project"**
3. Name your project (e.g., "IDE JSON to Docs Converter")

### Step 2: Copy the Script

1. Open `ide-json-to-docs.gs` from this repository
2. Copy the entire contents
3. Paste into the Google Apps Script editor (replacing any default code)

### Step 3: Configure the Script

Update the `CONFIG` object at the top of the script:

```javascript
const CONFIG = {
  // Root folder ID where ACTIVE/INACTIVE folders live
  rootFolderId: '1Tb5v39DqRwk2341iYwOKqMXxA3e1Iu-8',
  
  // Leave empty for auto-detection (recommended)
  statusFolder: '',
  patientName: '',
  
  // Paste your JSON here, or use jsonFileId
  jsonInput: '',
  jsonFileId: '',
  
  // Options
  createJsonCopy: true,
  includeMissingData: false,
  includeClinicalInferences: false,
  autoDetect: true, // Recommended: automatically detect patient name and status
};
```

### Step 4: Grant Permissions

1. Click **"Run"** → **"generateRecordFromJson"**
2. When prompted, click **"Review Permissions"**
3. Select your Google account
4. Click **"Advanced"** → **"Go to [Project Name] (unsafe)"**
5. Click **"Allow"** to grant permissions:
   - Access to Google Drive (to create folders and files)
   - Access to Google Docs (to create documents)

## Usage

### Method 1: Paste JSON Directly (Recommended)

1. Copy the AI-generated JSON from your AI tool
2. In the script editor, paste it into `CONFIG.jsonInput`:
   ```javascript
   jsonInput: `{
     "schemaVersion": "1.0",
     "encounterId": "ide-1766464684536-2022-09-01",
     ...
   }`,
   ```
3. Ensure `autoDetect: true` in CONFIG
4. Click **"Run"** → **"generateRecordFromJson"**
5. The script will:
   - Automatically detect patient name from JSON header
   - Detect status (ACTIVE/INACTIVE) 
   - Create/use the appropriate folder structure
   - Generate the Google Doc
   - Save a JSON copy in the patient folder

### Method 2: Use Drive File ID

1. Upload the JSON file to Google Drive
2. Get the file ID from the file URL: `https://drive.google.com/file/d/{FILE_ID}/view`
3. Set `CONFIG.jsonFileId` to the file ID:
   ```javascript
   jsonFileId: '1a2b3c4d5e6f7g8h9i0j',
   ```
4. Click **"Run"** → **"generateRecordFromJson"**

## Folder Structure

The script creates the following structure in Google Drive:

```
Root Folder (1Tb5v39DqRwk2341iYwOKqMXxA3e1Iu-8)
├── ACTIVE/
│   └── {Patient Name} - {patientId}/
│       ├── IDE - {date} - {Patient Name}.doc
│       └── EncounterNoteDraft-IDE-{date}.json
└── INACTIVE/
    └── {Patient Name} - {patientId}/
        └── ...
```

## Auto-Detection Features

When `autoDetect: true`:

- **Patient Name**: Extracted from `draft.header.patientLegalName`, or searched for in existing folders by patient ID
- **Status**: Detected from `draft.status` field, or defaults to "ACTIVE" for IDE notes
- **Folder Matching**: If patient ID is found in encounterId (pattern: `ide-{patientId}-{date}`), searches existing folders to find the correct patient name

## Error Handling

The script validates:
- JSON syntax
- Required fields: `schemaVersion`, `encounterId`, `noteType`, `dateOfService`, `sections`, `missingData`, `clinicalInferences`
- Folder access permissions

Common errors:
- **"Invalid JSON"**: Check JSON syntax
- **"Missing required field"**: Ensure all required fields are present
- **"Cannot find folder"**: Verify `rootFolderId` is correct and you have access

## Configuration Options

| Option | Description | Default |
|--------|-------------|---------|
| `rootFolderId` | Google Drive folder ID for patient records | Required |
| `statusFolder` | "ACTIVE" or "INACTIVE" (empty for auto-detect) | '' |
| `patientName` | Patient name (empty for auto-detect) | '' |
| `jsonInput` | JSON string to paste directly | '' |
| `jsonFileId` | Google Drive file ID containing JSON | '' |
| `createJsonCopy` | Save JSON copy in patient folder | `true` |
| `includeMissingData` | Include missingData section in doc | `false` |
| `includeClinicalInferences` | Include clinicalInferences section | `false` |
| `autoDetect` | Auto-detect patient name and status | `true` |

## Troubleshooting

### Script can't find root folder
- Verify the folder ID is correct
- Ensure you have access to the folder
- Check that the folder exists

### Patient name not detected
- Ensure `draft.header.patientLegalName` exists in JSON
- Or manually set `CONFIG.patientName`
- Check that patient ID pattern matches: `ide-{patientId}-{date}`

### Document not created
- Check Google Apps Script execution log for errors
- Verify all required permissions are granted
- Ensure JSON structure matches EncounterNoteDraft schema

## Example JSON Structure

The script expects JSON matching the EncounterNoteDraft schema:

```json
{
  "schemaVersion": "1.0",
  "encounterId": "ide-1766464684536-2022-09-01",
  "noteType": "IDE",
  "dateOfService": "2022-09-01",
  "provider": "Dr. Smith",
  "header": {
    "patientLegalName": "DOE, JOHN",
    "dob": "1980-01-01",
    "mrn": "12345"
  },
  "sections": [
    {
      "title": "CHIEF COMPLAINT",
      "blocks": [
        {
          "type": "paragraph",
          "text": "Patient presents with..."
        }
      ]
    }
  ],
  "missingData": [],
  "clinicalInferences": []
}
```

## Support

For issues or questions:
1. Check the execution log in Google Apps Script editor
2. Verify JSON structure matches the schema
3. Ensure all permissions are granted
4. Review error messages for specific field issues
